### 2026-10-10 — The account popover's route builder read the drawer's `ref` — FIXED

- **Report (the user's).** Tapping **Logout** in the side menu showed `Bad state: Using "ref" when a widget is about to or has been unmounted is unsafe. … (widgets library · building Builder)` — the same message as 0.0.449, but a **different** throw site.
- **Root cause (reproduced, not guessed).** `_openAccountSheet` built the route as `builder: (_) => _AccountPopover(email: ref.read(authIdentityProvider)?.email ?? '', drawerNavigator: Navigator.of(iconContext), router: GoRouter.of(iconContext))`. That closure is **not** run once: a `PopupRoute`'s page is rebuilt inside `_ModalScopeState`'s own `Builder` (hence *building Builder*), so the drawer's `ref` and `iconContext` were read on **every** rebuild. Logout first closes the screen the drawer lived on, so the next rebuild touched a **deactivated** context. Reproduced on the **real app router** with the real screens (auth harness + free-with-data logout branch): the full stack named `_openAccountSheet.<anonymous closure>` → `_AccountPopoverRoute.buildPage` → `_ModalScopeState`'s `Builder`.
- **Fix (one method).** The email, the `NavigatorState` and the `GoRouter` are now read **before `navigator.push`**, while the drawer is certainly mounted, and the builder closure only captures those values. Nothing else changed; the panel is still its own `ConsumerWidget` with its own `ref` (the 0.0.449 fix stands).
- **Test.** New case in `app_navigation_drawer_test.dart` (now **19**): a **shell-shaped** router — the real app's shape, where the drawer's screen sits on the shell's **nested** navigator, so closing the panel pops that screen and deactivates the drawer while the panel is up — then Logout on the free branch, asserting the safety-copy window opens, **No** wipes, the session ends and the router lands on Login. It fails on the pre-fix code with the device's own message (proven by temporarily restoring the old closure).
- **Why the earlier tests missed it.** The drawer suite mounts the drawer on a **flat** router, where `Navigator.of(iconContext)` is the root navigator: closing the panel then pops the popover itself and the drawer stays mounted, so the page never rebuilt against a dead context.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `app_navigation_drawer` **19/19**; full suite **1524 passed**; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.450**. Records updated in both `docs/implementation_plan.md` (the popover bullet at line 120) and Task 06 (rule 14).
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: open the side menu → tap the avatar → **Logout**; the branch's window should appear and the app should land on Login with no error entry on Diagnostics.

### 2026-10-09 — Logout from the side-menu profile popover crashed — FIXED

- **Report (the user's).** Tapping **Profile → Logout** in the side menu showed `Bad state: Using "ref" when a widget is about to or has been unmounted is unsafe.`
- **Root cause (reproduced, not guessed).** `runBranchedLogout` (`lib/features/auth/presentation/logout_dialogs.dart`) is handed a **widget** `ref` — the account popover's, or a screen's — and read `ref.read(backupMetadataProvider)` at line 62, *after* two awaits (`ref.read(logoutFlowProvider.future)` and `await flow.plan()`). Riverpod 3 forbids touching a widget `ref` once its widget is unmounted (`WidgetRef.read` → `_assertNotDisposed`), and on the device the popover is gone by the time those real-I/O awaits resume. A throwaway test that pops the owning route mid-flow reproduced the exact `Bad state: Using "ref" …` message; the same test passes with the fix.
- **Fix.** Every provider value the orchestration needs is now read **before the first await** — `final flowFuture = ref.read(logoutFlowProvider.future)` and `final encrypt = ref.read(backupMetadataProvider).encryptBackups` — so the widget `ref` is only touched synchronously while its widget is certainly mounted. Nothing else in the flow changed.
- **Why tests missed it.** `app_navigation_drawer_test` and `logout_dialogs_test` override `logoutFlowProvider` with an already-resolved value, so the flow never crossed a real await with a live widget ref. The regression test now does exactly that.
- **Tests.** New `test/features/logout_ref_lifetime_test.dart` — pops the owning route while the flow provider is still pending, then completes it, and asserts the logout finishes (`sessionsEnded == 1`) with no exception.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; logout suites 60/60; full suite **1523 passed**; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.449**.

### 2026-10-09 — Automatic Backup is wired for real — COMPLETE

- **The gap (found on the user's "check Automatic Backup" request).** The Backup & Restore screen's five schedule choices (Off / Everyday / Every 3 / 7 / month) were only *stored* — `backup_schedule_days` in SharedPreferences — and **nothing ever read them**. The metadata store's own comment admitted it: *"nothing runs on a schedule until Part B's background executor exists."*
- **The fix — a backup scheduler mirroring the notification one.** New `lib/core/backup/`: `backup_schedule.dart` (pure rules — `BackupSchedule.isEnabled`/`isDue`, `decideBackupJob` → `disabled | encryptionOnSkips | run`), `backup_alarm_callback.dart` (the `@pragma('vm:entry-point')` job: builds its own DB + prefs, decides, and either exports a plain backup into `Download/LegalCaseDiary` and records it, or posts the skip notice), `android_backup_scheduler.dart` (`AndroidBackupScheduler` arming a daily alarm via the notification gateway's helpers), `backup_scheduler.dart` (the contract), `backup_scheduler_scope.dart` (re-arms on every launch).
- **The daily alarm re-arms itself** each firing (the one-shot chain the hearing alerts use), so the schedule survives without the app being opened, and `rescheduleOnReboot: true` survives reboot. The interval (Every 3 days etc.) is decided *inside* the job from `last_successful_backup_at`, so "Every 3 days" means "at most every 72 h", not a weekday. The alarm id base (`backupAlarmIdBase`, 1301 + 1440 + 100) is kept clear of the alert range; a guard test checks the two never collide.
- **Encryption interplay (user chose "skip").** A background job cannot ask for the passphrase (it is never stored), so with `encryptBackups` on and a backup due, the job **skips** and posts a localized "Automatic backup skipped — back up manually" notice on its own channel (`backupScheduler_backupNoticeChannelId`, new ARB keys in English + Bengali). It never writes a plain file over the user's encrypted-choice.
- **Wiring.** `backupSchedulerProvider` (`core/providers/backup_providers.dart`); `BackupSchedulerScope` mounted in `lib/app.dart`'s builder chain (arms on launch); the screen's `_ScheduleCard.onChanged` now calls `syncSchedule()` after storing the interval; `_clearPerUserCaches` (logout/account switch) calls `cancelAll()` so the next account inherits nothing. The stale "until Part B" comment in `backup_metadata_store.dart` is corrected.
- **Background-safe folder.** The alarm isolate cannot call the `public_downloads` MethodChannel (host-only), so `backgroundBackupDirectory()` resolves `/storage/emulated/0/Download/LegalCaseDiary` directly on Android, falling back to the documents directory.
- **Tests.** `backup_schedule_test.dart` (10 pure cadence/decision cases), `backup_local_only_test.dart` (source guards: no network/sync imports on the job path, `@pragma('vm:entry-point')` kept, alarm-id ranges distinct), `FakeBackupScheduler` + a screen test that picking a schedule calls `syncSchedule()`.
- **Known limitation / device check pending.** The background job itself (real DB + alarm) is not exercised by tests (same as the notification job). Android build still can't be verified in this sandbox (Gradle loopback), and the manual device pass is owed: set Everyday (encryption off) → a file appears in `Download/LegalCaseDiary` after the alarm; encryption on → the skip notice; Off → no alarm; reboot → survives.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; 15 new tests (`backup_schedule_test` 10, `backup_local_only_test` 5) + the screen's schedule test green; screen suite 31/31; full suite **1522 passed**; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.448**. One real regression caught and fixed: an `await`ed `cancelAll()` in the logout wipe path hung on the unmocked alarm channel and broke five logout tests — cancelling is now `unawaited` (best-effort cleanup must never gate a logout).

### 2026-10-09 — Dialog-free backups into a diary folder — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to the Backup & Restore screen & system).** (1) Backup buttons should stop asking where to save; write automatically into Internal storage/LegalCaseDiary. (2) Rename the file to `Backup_dd-mm-yy_hh:mm:ssam/pm.<ext>`. (3) Restore: if the folder has one backup, restore directly; if multiple, list them in a slightly rounder-corner popup. Follow-up: keep the system picker as a fallback when the folder can't be written or restore can't find it. **Excel also goes to the folder** (user choice).
- **Destination (corrected after the user's check).** The first try wrote into the app's private documents directory — `/data/user/0/com.legalcasediary.app/app_flutter/LegalCaseDiary` — invisible and unnavigable. `path_provider`'s Android paths are all app-scoped (`Android/data/<pkg>`), so the **true public Downloads folder** is now answered by a small host channel: `MainActivity.kt` serves `com.legalcasediary.app/public_downloads` → `Environment.getExternalStoragePublicDirectory(DIRECTORY_DOWNLOADS)` (deprecated but real; writable directly by the app for its own files under scoped storage, no permission). `backupDocumentsDirectory()` returns **`Download/LegalCaseDiary`** (`/storage/emulated/0/Download/LegalCaseDiary`), visible in file managers and over USB; it falls back to the private documents directory where the channel is absent (test hosts, non-Android). `BackupFolderSink` writes into it; `BackupFolderFirstSink` falls back to the `FilePickerBackupGateway` save dialog only when the folder throws `FileSystemException`. All three backup actions (Local, Excel, Drive) write here; the automatic safety copies (logout, extended-offline) keep the private `LocalArchiveSink` (`<documents>/backups`) unchanged.
- **File name.** `defaultBackupFileName`/`defaultExcelFileName` now produce `Backup_<dd>-<mm>-<yy>_<h>-<mm>-<ss><am|pm>.lcbak/.xlsx` — local civil date/time, lower-case am/pm. `_fileStamp` switched from UTC `YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS` to local; the folder scan (`listBackupFiles`/`backupFileNamePattern`) accepts only this `Backup_…` stem (both `.lcbak` and `.xlsx`), so a stray PDF/photo is ignored.
- **Restore.** `_pickFromFolderOrPicker` scans the folder (via `backupFolderDirectoryProvider`): 1 file → direct restore; several → `_BackupFilePickerDialog` (the rounder-corner popup, `sheetRadius + radiusSm`) listing the files; empty/missing/no app-named file → the system picker (the fallback). The rest of the restore flow (open/validate/confirm/replace/import) is untouched.
- **L10n.** New `settingsBackupRestore_chooseBackupTitle` key ("Choose the backup to restore" / Bengali) added to both ARBs + generated files + hand-maintained `app_strings.dart`/`app_translations.dart`.
- **Tests.** `backup_repository_test` filename cases updated (local-time expectation, Excel shares the stem) and 2 new `backupDocumentsDirectory` cases — uses the public Downloads path when the host channel answers, falls back to documents when it is absent. `backup_restore_screen_test`: folder-sink fake (`_FakeFolderSink`, sync write) so widget tests avoid real-async hangs, plus new cases — single-file direct restore, multi-file choice popup (asserts the rounder corner), empty-folder → picker fallback; fallback tests inject `_ThrowingFolderSink`.
- **Android build note.** The debug APK could not be verified in this session (`java.io.IOException: Unable to establish loopback connection` — the sandbox blocks the Gradle daemon's loopback, an environment restriction, not a code error). The Kotlin change mirrors the existing `device_setup`/`app_exit` channel pattern; a local (non-sandboxed) build should confirm `assembleDebug` passes.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `backup_repository` 22/22, `backup_restore_screen` 32/32, full suite **1508 passed**; record_version `--check` OK; version **0.0.446**.
- **Resume point:** device check — pressing Local/Excel backup writes a `Backup_…` file into `Download/LegalCaseDiary` (visible in the file manager's Download folder, no dialog); restore with one file restores without any picker, with several shows the rounder-corner list; confirm the safety-copy/Drive paths are unchanged. Verify `assembleDebug` on a machine where Gradle's loopback is not sandbox-blocked.

### 2026-10-09 — Subscription Details: Free plans hide Expiry and show their limits — COMPLETE

Three ARB strings, en + bn, the case count a `{limit}` placeholder carrying the active language's digits.

### 2026-10-09 — Turn Off App Lock now demands proof — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** "When the user selects Turn Off button from the confirmation window ask the user to verify with the current pin or fingerprint before turning off."
- **A third step, between the warning and the wipe.** `_confirmTurnOff` now runs: the warning dialog → `_proveIdentity` → the clear. The proof is the **same current-PIN-or-fingerprint pad the change-PIN flow opens**, so there is one way to prove the owner and it cannot drift; a cancelled warning or a failed/cancelled proof leaves the lock exactly as it was, because nothing is cleared until the proof returns.
- **Why it matters.** Turn Off is reachable from the settings screen, which on a phone left unlocked is exactly where a stranger would go — and with no proof, one tap in a two-step dialog would strip the lock. The proof is the whole point of the feature applied to the one action that removes it.
- **How "proved" is read from the pad.** The proof flow's single stage captures a PIN (one entry) and, where the fingerprint is enabled and the device can answer it, also accepts a fingerprint — which completes the stage with **no** pin. So a non-null result is proof (a captured pin *or* a proven fingerprint) and a null result is a cancel; the code returns `captured != null`.
- **Three tests** (24 in `app_lock_test.dart`): the existing Turn Off case now asserts the proof appears and nothing is cleared before the PIN, and passes on the correct PIN; a wrong PIN shows the mismatch line and leaves the lock set, and cancelling the proof leaves it set too; and the fingerprint glyph alone proves the turn-off (the fake records one verify call). Reverting the proof step made the confirm-then-assert test fail on the now-missing proof dialog.
- **Correction to the previous entry.** The 0.0.436 note claimed the suite held "26 cases"; it held 24 (5 store + 19 widget). The count is corrected in `docs/currentlyWorking.md`.
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's **App Lock** bullet and Task 12's matching bullet now name the proof step between the confirmation and the clear, with **(0.0.437)**.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `app_lock` suite **24/24**; full suite **1503 passed**; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.437**.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: Turn Off → confirm → the pad asks for the PIN (or the fingerprint glyph, where enabled) → only a correct proof switches the lock off.

### 2026-10-09 — App Lock: Turn Off, and a collapsing timing control — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to App Lock only).** Add a **Turn Off App Lock** button at the top; and change **When To Ask For The Pin** from its dropdown into a button like the other two, which on tap "collapses the section and shows two more buttons" — *When The App Starts* and *Every Time The App Opens*.
- **Two decisions asked, not guessed (rule 1).** (1) The timing control **starts collapsed and expands on tap** (the user picked the collapsible preview, chevron turning down when open), rather than always showing its two options. (2) **Turn Off confirms first** — a Cancel / Turn Off dialog — rather than acting immediately.
- **Turn Off, written in the crash-safe order.** The new `SettingsSubMenuRow` sits at the top of the set-up screen; confirming calls `AppLockStore.turnOff()`, which clears the **existence flag first and the digest last**. The dangerous residue is a "set up" flag with no digest behind it — that locks the owner out with nothing that can ever match — so an interrupted turn-off must fail toward an *unlocked* app. It also clears the fingerprint flag, and the screen drops back to the **Setup App Lock** row and collapses the timing section, so the next setup opens on the same plain column it shipped with.
- **The timing control, rebuilt as the user drew it.** `_TimingHeader` is a row in the same shape as the two buttons above it (its `schedule` icon in the circle, the same 44 px floor) with an `AnimatedRotation` chevron; opening it reveals `_TimingOption` × 2 — the same row design, the active one tinted and ticked. Choosing writes the timing and closes the section. The old dropdown dialog and its `settings_controls` import are gone.
- **Labels moved to the user's wording.** *When The App Opens* → **When The App Starts**; *When The App Opens Or Returns From The Background* → **Every Time The App Opens**. New ARB keys for the turn-off row, its confirm dialog and its snackbar (en + bn); the dead `whenAppOpens` key renamed to `whenAppStarts` in both files, gen-l10n re-run, ARB parity test green. **Manage Account's row title is left as "App Lock"** — the request scoped the change to the App Lock screen, and a sub-menu row and its destination page do not have to share a name.
- **Tests.** Two rewritten/added cases in `app_lock_test.dart` (24 in that file at 0.0.436): the timing button opens onto both choices with the active one ticked, choosing writes the timing and collapses, reopening marks the new choice; and Turn Off — declining changes nothing, confirming clears the PIN *and* the fingerprint flag, shows the snackbar, and returns the screen to Setup. Teeth verified by reverting the fingerprint clear: the test failed `Expected: false, Actual: true`, then the fix was restored.
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's **App Lock** bullet corrected — its *When To Ask For The Pin* description now names the collapsing button and the two choices, and gains the **(0.0.436)** Turn Off paragraph; Task 12's matching bullet corrected the same way.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `app_lock` suite **26/26**; full suite **1501 passed**; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.436** (the tree had moved on since 0.0.420).
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: Turn Off asks, then the screen shows Setup again; on iOS/macOS the raw `turnOff` name would trip the calendar-entitlement analyzer, but this is an Android-first build and `flutter analyze` is clean.

### 2026-10-09 — The account panel lines up with the drawer, gains a grey edge, and the icon says it is a menu — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, three follow-ups on the account panel).** (1) The profile icon needs an indicator that says it opens something, without wrecking the look of the profile. (2) The panel had a strip of drawer showing to its left — move it to the left of the side menu/screen so it matches the panel and does not cross it. (3) Give the panel a thin but clearly visible grey outline, so the user can see it floating over the side panel.
- **What changed, and where.** [app_navigation_drawer.dart](lib/shared/widgets/app_navigation_drawer.dart), three small edits, no new ARB key and no new dependency.
  - **(1) The badge.** `_ProfileIconButton` wraps the avatar in a `Stack` and pins a 16 px disc to its bottom-right (`right: 0, bottom: 0`, so it stays inside the avatar's 48 px box). The disc is filled with the panel's own `surfaceContainerLowest`, ringed with a hairline of `AppColors.outlineVariant`, and holds `Icons.expand_more` at 12 px in `onSurfaceVariant` — the same chevron the plan row already uses for "a menu opens here". It covers a corner of the avatar, not its face: it does not move the name column (the badge ends at x 72, the name starts at 84), does not grow the tap target, and does not change the rectangle the panel is measured from (the Stack still reports 48×48), which the unchanged 8 px top-gap assertion proves.
  - **(2) Flush left.** `_AccountPopoverLayout.getPositionForChild` now returns `Offset(0, anchor.bottom + _gap)` instead of `anchor.left`: the panel's left edge is the drawer's left edge. The 300 px width stays inside the 304 px Material gives a drawer (verified in the SDK — `_kWidth = 304.0`, and the app sets no `DrawerThemeData`), so it never crosses the panel; a narrower screen still shrinks it (`maxWidth - 8`). The old trailing-edge clamp is gone with the old left position, so the delegate is smaller than before.

The badge case asserts the 16 px disc exists inside the avatar's own rect, with the panel's fill, a circle shape, the divider grey on its border and the chevron in `onSurfaceVariant` — the case that fails if the affordance is removed. The outline case reads the `Material`'s own shape and asserts the side colour and that its width is 1.0. The geometry case asserts the panel's left is the **drawer's** left (measured from `find.byType(Drawer)`), that it is **left of the icon's** left — the assertion the old anchored position fails — and that its right edge stays inside the drawer's. Teeth, each run and reverted, each failing exactly the intended case: the badge's key renamed → *the profile icon carries a small chevron badge* (15 pass, 1 fail); the position put back on `anchor.left` → *the account popover lines up with the side menu panel* (15 pass, 1 fail); the `side:` removed → *the account popover carries a thin grey outline* (15 pass, 1 fail).

### 2026-10-09 — The drawer's profile icon opens the account popover — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to the side menu panel).** Clicking the profile icon must offer the e-mail the user is logged in with, **Profile**, **Manage Account** and **Logout**.
- **What it does.** [app_navigation_drawer.dart](lib/shared/widgets/app_navigation_drawer.dart) — the header avatar is now a 48 px `InkWell` (`CircleBorder`, plus a `Semantics(button: true)` label from the existing `settingsScreen_accountAndProfile`, since an icon-only button has no text for a screen reader). Tapping it opens a panel **anchored to the icon** that names the signed-in address, captioned with the existing `auth_emailLabel`, and lists **Profile** (→ `/settings/profile`), **Manage Account** (→ `/settings/manage-account`) and **Logout**. **No new ARB key** and no `gen-l10n` run: all four strings already exist — `settingsScreen_profile`, `settingsScreen_manageAccount` and `settingsManageAccount_logout` are the very ones the Account & Profile menu and Manage Account show.
- **Logout is the app's own, not a second one.** It calls `runBranchedLogout` — the single orchestrator Manage Account's Logout card calls — and then lands on **Login explicitly**, because the router's sign-in gate is decided once per launch and a signed-out user would otherwise be left on the now-empty shell.
- **The bug the test caught, and the shape it forced (this is the interesting part).** The first implementation closed the panel and *then* showed the sheet, acting on the choice it returned — the pattern every other drawer row uses. `flutter test` failed with *“Using ref when a widget is about to or has been unmounted is unsafe”*: the panel is unmounted by the time the sheet closes, so the drawer's `ref` — which `runBranchedLogout` needs for `logoutFlowProvider` and `backupMetadataProvider` — is dead. That is a real defect, not a test artifact. The panel is now **a `ConsumerWidget` with its own `ref`**: it is shown on the **root navigator with the panel still open**, the chosen row closes the panel (`NavigatorState.pop`, a state object passed in rather than a context), and for Logout the panel stays up behind the logout windows — so its `ref` and `context` are alive for the whole flow — then closes itself when they finish. The two navigators reach it as state objects (`NavigatorState`, `GoRouter`), which are safe once the drawer is gone.
- **The panel moved from the screen's foot to the icon (0.0.432 — user follow-up).** 0.0.431 opened it with `showModalBottomSheet`, so it rose from the bottom edge. The user asked for it at the icon instead — *“so they look connected to the profile icon like Google does in their various app”* — and it now is: `_AccountPopoverRoute`, a `PopupRoute` placed by `_AccountPopoverLayout` (a `SingleChildLayoutDelegate`) from the icon's own rectangle, **measured in the root navigator's overlay** because that is the space the route lays out in. Its top edge sits **8 px under the icon's bottom**, its left edge **on the icon's left**, its width is **300 px** (less twice an 8 px inset on a narrower screen) so it stays a menu rather than spanning the screen, and it carries the app's own card radius (`AppSpacing.cardRadius` — the radius Material's menus use) with elevation 8. The barrier is **transparent**: a tap outside still dismisses, with no dimming and nothing that would detach it from the icon. It fades and scales out of its own top-left corner over 150 ms. The measurement is why the avatar became its own widget (`_ProfileIconButton`): a composite element's `findRenderObject` descends to the first render object *below* it, which is the avatar's 48 px circle — checked in the Flutter SDK source rather than assumed. Nothing else about the panel moved: the four items, the shared `ref`, the closing of the drawer and the explicit landing on Login are as they were.
- **Tests, with teeth proven in both directions.** `app_navigation_drawer_test.dart` gained five cases (13 total): the icon opens the panel and it names the address **from `authIdentityProvider`** (stubbed to a fixed address, so a panel that invented one would fail), Profile lands on `/settings/profile`, Manage Account on `/settings/manage-account`, and Logout runs the branched logout (a stubbed flow records `endSession`) and lands on Login. **The anchoring has its own case**, asserting the 8 px gap under the icon, the icon's left edge, the 300 px width and that the panel's top is nowhere near the screen's foot — the assertion a bottom sheet cannot pass. Teeth, each run and reverted: removing the icon's `onTap` fails exactly the four action cases (8 pass, 4 fail); swapping `runBranchedLogout` for a constant fails exactly the logout case (12 pass, 1 fail); and placing the panel at the screen's foot (`size.height - childSize.height`) fails exactly the anchoring case (12 pass, 1 fail).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean, exit 0 — the two `use_build_context_synchronously` infos the first shape raised were fixed by guarding with `navigator.mounted` (for a `State.context` use the lint requires a `mounted` check on the `State` itself), not suppressed. Full suite **1495 passed**, exit 0 (up from 1490 — the five new cases). **No SQL, no ARB change, no mockup:** the panel has no mockup anywhere in `mockup_html_screens` (rule 9), and `admin_panel/code.html` mentions the drawer only in prose.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A **(0.0.431, anchored in 0.0.432)** bullet in the plan's *Resolved Design Clarifications* and a matching note in [Task 06](docs/tasks/06_shared_widgets_navigation_and_router.md) Phase 2 item 1, which owns the panel's body. Both were corrected in place for the move rather than duplicated.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: open the side menu on any case screen, tap the avatar, and confirm the panel **hangs off the icon** — top edge level with the avatar's underside, left edges in line — with the address readable; each of the three rows must do what it says, and a tap outside must return you to the open panel.

### 2026-10-09 — The empty case lists say what is missing — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to five screens).** Overdue Dates, Active Cases, Upcoming Cases, All Cases and Disposed Cases showed their own screen name in the middle when empty; each must instead show its own message — All Cases *No case added yet. Tap + to add one.*, Active Cases *No active case to show. Tap + to add one.*, Overdue Dates *No overdue case*, Upcoming Cases *No upcoming case*, Disposed Cases *No disposed case*.
- **What changed.** Five new ARB keys — `allCasesScreen_empty`, `activeCasesScreen_empty`, `overdueDatesScreen_empty`, `upcomingCasesScreen_empty`, `disposedCasesScreen_empty` — added after each screen's `_title` in `app_en.arb` and `app_bn.arb` in the same order, Bengali written fresh with a danda where the English sentence ends, and `flutter gen-l10n` regenerated. Each of the five screens now passes `l.xxxScreen_empty` to the shared `CaseListEmptyState` (`case_search.dart`) instead of `l.xxxScreen_title`. **No new widget, no layout change:** the empty state is the same shared `Center`/scrollable it already was, so pull-to-sync on an empty list is untouched.

- **What changed.** The title's box now spans the card and its text is centred inside it (`SizedBox(width: double.infinity)` + `TextAlign.center` — the width matters as much as the alignment, because a shrink-wrapped `Text` would centre the text inside its own width and move nothing on screen); the subtitle below keeps the card's left alignment, which is all the request asked for. The subtitle drops **one font size** through the app's own mechanism — `SteppedTextScaler(media.textScaler, -1)` — so the step is taken off whatever scale is in effect and therefore follows the Font Size setting rather than overriding it; the style still names the `bodyMd` token (a hardcoded 13 would stop following the token, and hardcoding is exactly what the additive scaler exists to avoid). The mockup follows the app (rule 9): the title is `text-center` and the subtitle carries the exact shipped 13 px inline, because the mockup's token scale jumps 14 px → 12 px with nothing between.
- **The tests found two real problems, one of them mine.** (1) The subtitle case failed immediately at `Actual: -1` — my constant was written `+1` under a name that said "step down", so the subtitle rendered one size *bigger*; the code was wrong, not the test, and the sign was fixed. (2) The title case, first written against the paragraph's own text boxes, failed at `Actual: 183.7` against a required 169: a line's box **includes the whitespace after it**, and on the test font this title's first line is exactly one trailing space wider than its glyphs. Making the paragraph's line tight was one option; measuring the **painted ink** off the frame was the better one, since a space paints nothing — so `inkExtentInside` was added beside the existing `darkestPaintedInside` and the title's ink centre is compared with the middle of its box.
- **Teeth, both directions.** Removing the title's `textAlign` moves the ink centre to **103** against the required 169 and fails the case; the subtitle's wrong sign was caught by the case before the code shipped. Both were restored and re-run green. The centring was then measured where real fonts exist — the mockup in the running browser: title ink centre **199 = card centre 199**, subtitle computed `13px`.

### 2026-10-09 — Exit App? really closes the app again — FIXED

- **Request (the user's).** "Exit App? window doesn't close the app properly. It just keeps the app in memory like pressing the home button."
- **The cause was read out of the shipped engine, not guessed.** `PlatformPlugin.popSystemNavigator()` (bytecode read from `bin/cache/artifacts/engine/android-arm64-release/flutter.jar`, Flutter 3.44.2) calls `activity.finish()` **only** when the host activity is not an `OnBackPressedDispatcherOwner`; otherwise it calls `activity.getOnBackPressedDispatcher().onBackPressed()` — `SystemNavigator.pop()` now *synthesizes an Android back press*. Our host is `FlutterFragmentActivity` (App Lock's fingerprint needs it), always a `ComponentActivity`, so the finish never happened. The `PlatformPluginDelegate` hook that can answer this is implemented by `FlutterActivity`/`FlutterFragment`, **not** by `FlutterFragmentActivity` — so no Dart call and no activity override could reach it. A back press at the root of a task leaves the app alive in memory, resumed on relaunch: Exit had become Home, exactly as reported.
- **The fix, split across the two sides.** New `lib/core/services/app_exit.dart` exposes `closeApp()`, which asks the host on its own channel (`com.legalcasediary.app/app_exit`) to `closeApp`; `MainActivity.kt` answers by finishing the task **and** dropping it from recents (`finishAndRemoveTask()`; the reply is sent first so the Dart future completes). `AppShellRoot`'s confirm and `requestExit` now call `closeApp()` instead of `SystemNavigator.pop()`, which stays as the fallback for a host without the hook (a test runner, Windows) so the button is never dead. The now-unused `flutter/services.dart` import left the router; `main.dart` and every screen are untouched.
- **Both ends pinned, because a Dart-only assertion would have passed with a dead host.** `test/core/app_shell_exit_test.dart`: the confirm test asserts the host channel is called with `closeApp`, and the fallback test drives **both** degraded paths — an unanswered channel (an empty reply, which is what `MissingPluginException` means) and a host that refuses (`PlatformException`) — asserting one framework pop each, two in total. Writing that second test found a genuine harness trap worth remembering: leaving a channel simply *unregistered* in a widget test makes `invokeMethod` hang rather than throw, because the test messenger never replies — the raw `setMockMessageHandler(..., (m) async => null)` is what stands in for a real host's not-implemented reply.
- **The Kotlin is verified by compiling it, not by inspection.** `./gradlew :app:compileDebugKotlin` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL, exit 0** (the same check App Lock used for its Android side), and the compiled `build/app/tmp/kotlin-classes/debug/com/legalcasediary/app/MainActivity.class` was disassembled to confirm the three things the Dart side depends on are really in it: the channel string `com.legalcasediary.app/app_exit`, the method `closeApp`, and the `finishAndRemoveTask()` call.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A **(0.0.422)** bullet appended to Dev Workflow §6 in `docs/implementation_plan.md` and a matching item 7 in `docs/tasks/06_shared_widgets_navigation_and_router.md` (the exit window is that task's scope). No UI change, so no mockup; no server change, so no migration or ledger rebuild.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `app_shell_exit_test.dart` **5/5**; full suite **1478 passed** (exit 0, up from 1477 — one test became two); Kotlin compile exit 0; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.422**.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check on the next install: Exit App? from a bottom-nav screen (and from the shell root) must remove the app from recents — reopening it must cold-start, and the app must not sit in memory — while Cancel still leaves it running.

### 2026-10-08 — App Lock audit fixes: the lock's tooltip crash and the rested fingerprint — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** Fix the three audit findings *and* two reports from the device: the lock screen "looks broken" logging `No Overlay widget found … RawTooltip`, and the fingerprint "doesn't work" though set up in the app.
- **One root cause behind both device reports — and it was a shape test gap.** `app.dart`'s builder hands the gate **the navigator itself**, so the lock screen draws *outside* the navigator's `Overlay`; the pad's backspace `IconButton(tooltip:)` builds a `Tooltip`, which asserts an Overlay ancestor **at build time**. The assert fires before the fingerprint button in the column, so the throw took the whole keypad down with it — the glyph was never built, which is why the fingerprint "didn't work". Every existing gate test pumped the scope under `home:` — *inside* the navigator's overlay — where the bug cannot exist. **The fix:** no `Tooltip` anywhere in the pad; both buttons carry `Semantics` labels instead (the screen-reader label the tooltip was holding), so the pad has no Overlay dependency at all and test and production now have the same shape constraints.
- **The lockout finding, fixed as audited.** `PinPad` gained `biometricsEnabled`, decoupled from `enabled`; the lock screen passes `!_checking` for it, so five wrong pins rest the digits and leave the fingerprint as the way in — which is what the lock's own contract (and the plan) always said.
- **Also as audited:** the dead `settingsAppLock_setupTitle` key is gone from both ARBs (gen-l10n re-run), and the three stale comments corrected — `verify` now documents `restartAt`, the store doc reads "can **never** claim set up" (the write order guarantees it), and `_digitKey` says 64 × 52.
- **Teeth, both directions.** Two new tests: `pumpGateInShell` reproduces the **production builder shape** and asserts `tester.takeException()` is null with the glyph present and tappable; the refusal test asserts `glyph.onPressed` is not null after five wrong pins while the digits are dead. Reverting each fix reproduced its own failure — the tooltip revert failed with **the user's exact string**, `No Overlay widget found`, and the `biometricsEnabled` revert failed with `Expected: not null, Actual: null` — then both were restored and re-verified.
- **Records (rule 14).** None needed: the plan's App Lock bullet already states the fingerprint-stays-usable contract this fix now honors, and the Overlay detail is implementation, not plan scope.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `app_lock` suite **21/21**; full suite **1477 passed**; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.421**.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: reinstall and reopen — the lock must render complete (digits, backspace, fingerprint), a fingerprint tap must prompt, five wrong digits must show the countdown *with* the glyph still tappable.

### 2026-10-08 — App Lock: a device PIN, a fingerprint, and a gate over the whole app — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** A new **App Lock** sub-menu inside Manage Account: **Setup App Lock** (shown only until a PIN exists), **Change App Lock Pin** via popup accepting the previous PIN *or* the fingerprint, and **Enable/Disable Fingerprint** — each direction verified by a real fingerprint prompt. Four decisions were asked before building: PIN length (**4 digits**), setup UI (**popup**, same shape as change), wrong-PIN policy (**lockout after 5**), and timing — the user added a requirement there: *"Let the user decide. Add another button for that. By default ask for pin when the app starts."*, which became the **When To Ask For The Pin** row (default *When The App Opens*; *…Or Returns From The Background* re-locks on every resume).
- **Four files and their jobs.** `app_lock/data/app_lock_store.dart` (salted SHA-256 digest in secure storage + the three preferences + the reactive `appLockStateProvider` that re-reads after every mutation rather than trusting the write), `data/biometrics.dart` (the `Biometrics` seam over `local_auth`, `biometricOnly: true` so a device screen lock can never satisfy it), `presentation/pin_pad.dart` (one shared 4-dot keypad driving every flow through `PinPadStage`s — capture, verify, or verify-by-fingerprint — with a declared `restartAt` for where a rejection sends the flow), `app_lock_screen.dart`, and `app_lock_scope.dart` (the gate: first read decides, covers the whole app while undecided, resume re-locks only for the background option, 5 wrong → 30-second countdown that the fingerprint ignores).
- **The lock covers the root, not a route.** `AppLockScope` sits inside `FontSizeScope` in `app.dart`, so it wraps every route, dialog and gate below it — the recovery screen included — while everything underneath keeps *running* (sync, queues) behind an opaque cover; only the text of the lock screen follows the font-size step.
- **`local_auth` needed its documented Android setup, not just the pub entry.** Added `local_auth ^3.0.2`, `USE_BIOMETRIC`, iOS `NSFaceIDUsageDescription`, and — read from the plugin README — `MainActivity` → `FlutterFragmentActivity` plus AppCompat theme parents with the `androidx.appcompat` dependency, because the old `Theme.Light.NoTitleBar` parents crash on Android 8 and below once the activity is a FragmentActivity. A **debug APK built clean** (exit 0), which is what proves the Kotlin/Gradle/resource side compiles.
- **Three real bugs the work itself produced, all caught before shipping.** (1) The first gate decided only on `hasValue`, but `FlutterSecureStorage.read` **never completes in widget tests** on this host — an App-pumping test sat under the cover forever and its tap missed; the fix is architectural: *isSetup now reads a plain preference flag* (mockable, answers synchronously; written only after the digest lands, so it can never claim a PIN that has nothing to verify against), while the digest stays in secure storage for verification only. (2) On that error path the gate called `ref.read(errorLogProvider)`, which **throws unless main.dart overrode it** — breaking every App-pumping harness; now wrapped, with the decision outside it. (3) A mismatch originally retried the *confirm* stage in place, contradicting the "Start Again." message; `restartAt` now sends setup's mismatch back to choosing (0) and change's back to the new-PIN entry (1), discarding captures from that point.
- **Tests: 19 in `test/features/app_lock_test.dart`, in three layers.** Store: digest never contains the PIN, four-digit validation, change retires the old pin, corrupt record never verifies, switch defaults. Screen: the visibility flip, the full setup flow, mismatch-restarts, change-pins with wrong-current-rejected, fingerprint flip only after a *verified* prompt (a refused prompt changes nothing), no-hardware message, timing picker. Gate: locked→unlocked with the pin, fresh install never covered, five-wrongs→countdown→comes-back (the countdown is frame-driven, so a test pumps it in place of waiting 30 real seconds), default never re-locks on resume while the background option does, fingerprint unlocks. The fake biometrics records its calls; the old suites (`notification_tap_test`, `manage_account_test`) pass with the scope in front of the whole App.
- **Records (rule 14 — a feature absent from the plan).** New **App Lock** bullet in `docs/implementation_plan.md` (after Manage Account), a matching bullet in Task 12, and `settingsAppLock` added to the ARB section-marker test's required list.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1475 passed**; debug APK build exit 0; `record_version.py --check` OK; version **0.0.420**.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check on the next install: Manage Account → App Lock → set up a 4-digit PIN, kill and reopen the app (it must ask), wrong-pin five times (countdown), enable the fingerprint (prompt really appears), background the app with *When The App Opens Or Returns From The Background* (re-locks on return). **Not covered here:** there is deliberately no *turn App Lock off* action — the request never asked for one; a PIN can only be changed, not removed.

### 2026-10-08 — The tap ripple is back on Manage Account and Subscription — FIXED

- **Request (the user's, scoped to button click/touch effect).** "The following buttons are not showing touch/click effect like rest of the app's buttons: Manage Account: All 3 buttons. Subscription: Both the buttons."
- **One cause behind all five.** The five buttons are three widgets: `SettingsActionCard` (Logout, Delete Account), `SettingsSubMenuRow` (Change Password, and Subscription's two rows). All three were opaque `Container`s wrapping `InkWell`s — an InkWell paints its splash on its nearest `Material` ancestor, and the Container's background sat on top of it, so the ripple painted *behind* the card and was invisible. Same disease as the 0.0.407 Settings-list fix; these were the last two card widgets that predate that rule.
- **The fix is the codebase's own pattern, twice.** Both widgets are now `SizedBox(width: double.infinity) → shaped Material(clipBehavior: antiAlias) → InkWell` — the same white card, the same outline border (and the destructive tone keeps `errorContainer` with its red-tinted border), with the ink now painting on the card itself. The `SizedBox` restores the full width the old `Container(width: double.infinity)` provided, which a bare `Material` would not (the Subscription screen's Column is not stretched).
- **Teeth, verified against the shipped bug.** New case in `manage_account_test.dart` reads, for each card, the ink controller its InkWell resolves to (`Material.of`) and requires the three to be **distinct** — in the buggy shape every card shared the one screen-level controller behind them all. Ran against the stashed 0.0.418 widgets: the test **fails** (a shared controller); against the fix it passes.
- **Two slips during the work, both caught before shipping.** (1) The first conversion dropped the `width: double.infinity` and would have left the Subscription rows hugging their text width — caught while reviewing the diff shape and restored via `SizedBox`. (2) A scripted revert for the teeth check mangled brackets in the sub-row; the analyzer flagged it immediately and the block was rewritten cleanly from the current source.
- **Records (rule 14 — a correction of the shipped body, absent from the plan).** A **(0.0.419)** sentence appended to the plan's Manage Account bullet and to Task 12's `SettingsActionCard` entry, naming the rule: a card wrapping an `InkWell` must itself be a `Material`.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; both affected suites pass; full suite **1456 passed**. Version **0.0.419** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Device check: tapping Logout, Change Password, Delete Account, Subscription Details and Purchase New Subscription should all show the ripple like the rest of the app.

### 2026-10-08 — The Font Size default moved up one pixel, and the bar kept its size — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** "Make Settings > View Settings > Font Size's +1 size the new default font size of the app. As part of the default font size, decrease the font size of Bottom Navigation by -1 font size. Keep the same step for font size like before."
- **What changed, in one number.** `FontSizeScope` now adds a `defaultFontSizeStep` of **1** to the user's own step before it builds the scaler, so `Default` renders one pixel above the design size and that is the size every screen is laid out for. The stepper is untouched — same ±1 step, same −2..+6 range, same `Default` label at 0 — it simply starts from here (the user's third bullet).
- **The bottom bar takes the same pixel back out.** `BottomNavBar` wraps itself in a `MediaQuery` whose scale is the inherited one minus `defaultFontSizeStep`, so its labels stay at the designed 12 px while every other screen grows, and the user's step still reaches it: the subtraction is from whatever scale is in effect, not from zero, so at step +3 the bar paints at 15 px beside the rest at 16. Icons are unaffected either way — `IconTheme.applyTextScaling` is unset in this app, so a 24 px icon stays 24 px at every step.
- **The fallback is honest.** With no `MediaQuery` above there is no `FontSizeScope` either, so there is no default to take back out and the bar keeps `TextScaler.noScaling` rather than inventing a `-1` over a scale the app never installed.
- **Tests.** `font_size_scope_test.dart` grew three: the default is now pinned numerically (`scale(16) == 16 + defaultFontSizeStep`, and the pair across −2/0/+6), and a new group pumps a real `BottomNavBar` under a real `FontSizeScope` and reads the scale each side paints with — the body at `12 + default + step`, the bar at `12 + step` at step 0 and step 3. The old "exactly the design size" and "device scale is dropped" expectations were rewritten against the new default rather than left describing a size the app no longer ships.
- **Records (rule 14 — the plan said the base was a fixed standard size).** A **(0.0.418)** sentence appended to the plan's `font_size_step` bullet and to Task 12's matching Font Size bullet. (The renumbering was done by string replace and caught one unrelated **(0.0.408)** on the `side_nav_options` bullet — reverted, so only the two intended lines differ.)
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1455 passed**. Version **0.0.418** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Device check: the whole app should look one pixel larger at `Default`, the bottom bar should look exactly as it did before, and `+1`/`-2` should each move both by the same single pixel.

### 2026-10-08 — Open Mail was hijacking the app's own task — FIXED

- **Report (the user's, on a device running 0.0.415/0.0.416).** Open Mail does open the mail app now, but after that: "pressing the back button, the app closes with mail app showing inside it but doesn't go back to the app. Re-opening (not restarting) the app again still shows previous mail app inside our app. Pressing back again closes the app without going back to the main app."
- **The cause was read out of the plugin's own source, not guessed.** `android_intent_plus`'s `IntentSender.send()` does `activity.startActivity(intent)` when the plugin holds a live Activity — **with no flags at all** (it only adds `FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK` on the fallback path where it has no Activity). So the mail app's screen was pushed onto **our** task instead of into its own: the task the system resumed was ours with the mail app on top, which is exactly the reported pair of symptoms — Back taking the whole app down with it, and reopening the app from the launcher bringing the mail screen back inside it.
- **The fix is the flag the launcher itself sends.** `androidMailAppIntent` now carries `Flag.FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK`. The mail app gets its own task, Back returns to Check Your Email, and this app's task stays ours — the same behaviour the earlier `mailto:` route had (`url_launcher`'s external-application mode) and the same thing that happens when the user taps a mail app's icon. Nothing else changed: the action, the category, the `<queries>` declaration and the `mailto:` fallback are untouched, and no user-visible string moved.
- **Pinned so it cannot be dropped again, and checked at both ends of the channel.** `test/core/contact_launcher_test.dart`'s first case asserts the flag alongside the action and category, and then pins the **integer the platform actually receives** (`convertFlags` ORs the list into one int; the plugin's Java side calls `intent.addFlags()` with it before `activity.startActivity()`). The plugin's `IntentSender.java` was read to confirm that path — a Dart-side assertion alone would have passed even if the platform dropped the flag.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `contact_launcher_test.dart` **3/3**; full suite **1452 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0). No server change, so no migration and no ledger rebuild.
- **Records (rule 14 — a correction of the plan's own 0.0.415 entry).** A **(0.0.417)** sentence appended to the plan's *Check Your Email Screen Finishes The Sign-Up* bullet and to Task 17's addendum.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check (the one that matters here): press **Open Mail**, then **Back** — the app must reappear on the Check Your Email screen; and reopening the app from the launcher or Recents must show our screen, never the mail app.

### 2026-10-08 — The Confirm Password box answers as it is typed — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to the Sign Up screen).** "Make the Confirm Password box background light-red until the password matches the Password box's input. Once matched, the background should turn green."
- **One decision was asked (rule 1): what an untouched field looks like.** The user chose **neutral until Confirm Password is typed** — the app's usual pale field fill — so an empty form never looks like a mistake; from the first character it is light red until the two agree, and light green the moment they do. The tint is **live in both directions**: editing the Password box after a match turns the confirmation field red again, and clearing the field returns it to neutral.
- **The colours already existed in the palette — bar one.** The light red is `AppColors.errorContainer` (#FFDAD6) and the green is the **new** `AppColors.successContainer` (#D1FAE5), the pale twin of `success` #059669, pairing exactly as `error` #DC2626 pairs with its container. Nothing else in the app uses the new token.
- **One optional parameter, and no other field changed.** `AuthTextField` gained `fillColor`; when it is null the decoration is left **unset** (`filled: null`, `fillColor: null`) so the theme's own pale fill still decides — passing `false` instead would have switched the fill off for every auth field in the app. The tint therefore appears on exactly the one field that asks for it.
- **Only that field rebuilds.** The confirmation field is wrapped in a `ListenableBuilder` over `Listenable.merge([_password, _confirmPassword])`, so a keystroke in either box repaints that field alone — no new screen state, no listener bookkeeping in `initState`/`dispose`, and no screen-wide `setState` on every character.
- **Tests, with teeth, down to the pixel.** Two new cases in `auth_signup_test.dart` (33 now). The first reads the field's **effective** `InputDecoration.fillColor` in five states — empty (the theme's `surfaceVariant`), one character differing (errorContainer), matched (successContainer), the Password box edited afterwards (errorContainer again), and cleared (back to surfaceVariant and never stuck red); its first run failed with `Expected: null, Actual: Color(0xfff1f5f9)` and taught the shape of the assertion, because `TextFormField` merges the theme's defaults into the decoration it builds, so the neutral state is the app's pale fill rather than a null. The second goes further and samples the **painted pixel** at the field's centre out of the rendered frame (`RenderRepaintBoundary.toImage` inside `tester.runAsync`), demanding light red in one state and light green in the next: a property assertion cannot see a theme default or an enclosing `Material` painting over the fill, and this can.
- **Mockup (rule 9).** `settings_onlineSync_signup/code.html` never drew a Confirm Password field at all; the Create Account card now has one, in its light-red state with the states named in a comment, and `success-container: #d1fae5` joined its inline Tailwind palette.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `auth_signup_test.dart` **33/33**; full suite **1452 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1450). No server change, so no migration and no ledger rebuild.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan, which never described this field's appearance).** A new bullet in the plan's auth block and a new Task 17 addendum bullet.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Device check: open Sign Up and type in Confirm Password — the box must be light red while it differs from the password, turn green the moment they match, go red again if the password above is then edited, and stay neutral while it is empty.

### 2026-10-08 — Open Mail opens the mail app, not an empty draft — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to Check Your Email's Open Mail).** "The button shows mail apps properly, but when clicked it opens the compose option (writing an email). I want that button to simply open the mail app."
- **The cause is the scheme, not the code.** A `mailto:` URI means *compose a message* by definition (RFC 6068), so the chooser Android raises for it can only list each mail app's **compose** activity — picking Gmail there starts an empty draft. There is no standard "open my inbox" URI; opening the app itself means asking the OS for the *app*.
- **One decision was asked (rule 1) and the user chose the small one: let Android pick the email app.** The alternatives were our own chooser (a host method channel enumerating every `mailto:` handler, so the exact list they had seen is preserved) and opening the default mail app with no chooser. They chose Android's own mechanism, accepting that the list is the apps that declare themselves an email app (Gmail and Outlook do) rather than every app that handles `mailto:`.
- **`ContactLauncher` gained `openMailApp()`.** `UrlContactLauncher` sends `androidMailAppIntent` — `android.intent.action.MAIN` plus `android.intent.category.APP_EMAIL`, a documented constant so a test can state what is sent without a phone — and Android opens the email app on its main screen, raising **its own chooser of app entries** when several apps declare the category, which is the "pick your mail app" the button wanted without the draft. The bare `mailto:` route is kept as the **fallback** for a device that cannot answer that intent, where reaching a mailbox still beats a dead button. The screen no longer builds a URI at all: `_openMail` asks the seam for the mail app.
- **The resolvability check is not decoration.** `android_intent_plus` short-circuits off Android: `launch()` returns without doing anything, so a platform that cannot answer would have reported a success it did not have. `canResolveActivity()` is therefore asked first and answers `false` there, which is what sends a non-Android device (or one with no app registered as the mail app) to the fallback.
- **Android 11+ needs the query declared.** `AndroidManifest.xml`'s `<queries>` block gained the `MAIN` + `APP_EMAIL` intent; without it package visibility hides the email app and the intent is neither resolvable nor launchable on the user's Android 12 device.
- **Tests, with teeth.** New `test/core/contact_launcher_test.dart` (3): the action, the category and the absence of any `data`/extras are pinned (so the intent cannot drift into a compose request); a platform that cannot answer falls back to exactly `mailto:` through `url_launcher`'s own channel, mocked; and a device where nothing handles mail reports `false`. `auth_signup_test.dart`'s Open Mail case now asserts the screen asked for the **mail app** and opened **no URI at all** — reverting `_openMail` to a `mailto:` fails it. The two other test doubles of the seam (`case_contact_actions_test.dart`, `support_screen_test.dart`) declare the new method and say why they never use it.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; the three touched suites **60/60**; the new launcher suite **3/3**; full suite **1450 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1447). The manifest was re-parsed to confirm the query is present and the file is well formed. No Dart host code changed, so there is no Kotlin to compile and no server change — no migration and no ledger rebuild.
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's *Check Your Email Screen Finishes The Sign-Up* bullet and Task 17's addendum each gained a **(0.0.415)** sentence — the deviation is Open Mail's route, which both described as the bare `mailto:` chooser.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Device check: on Check Your Email press **Open Mail** — the phone must open the mail app itself (its inbox), with Android's own chooser when more than one app qualifies, and no empty draft anywhere.

### 2026-10-08 — The green card says Use The App, and does it — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to the Email Confirmed card).** "Change the button text from Back To Login to Use the App — because pressing it doesn't take the user to the Login screen but to the app." The second half of the message, a question about Google sign-up, is answered below and needed no code.
- **Three decisions were asked first (rule 1), not guessed.** The card's sentence: the user chose to **change it too**, since "Log in to continue" would contradict the new label — it now reads *Your email address {email} is confirmed. You can use the app now.* The casing: **Use The App** (rule 11's Title Case, the same ARB convention that produced "Back To Login" and "Open Mail"). And what the tap should actually do: the user chose **sign the user in there** — the card still holds the password the form was typed with, so the button should do what it says in every case rather than quietly landing a session-less device on Login.
- **The label change is the smaller half of it.** `_ConfirmedEmailBody` — the green card — swaps a single `AuthPrimaryButton(auth_backToLogin)` for `AuthPrimaryButton(auth_useTheApp, busy: _usingApp)`, and the card's own sentence changed with it in both languages (Bengali too: *এখন আপনি অ্যাপটি ব্যবহার করতে পারেন।* for the new sentence and *অ্যাপটি ব্যবহার করুন* for the label). The waiting card is untouched: **Open Mail**, **Back To Login**, **Resend Email**, **Change Email Address** all stay exactly as they were, and the shipped mockup `settings_onlineSync_signup/code.html` green card now draws the new label and sentence (rule 9).
- **"Use The App" now means it.** The new `_useTheApp()` calls the very same `AuthViewModel.signIn` the Login screen calls, with the address and the password the form is still holding, then leaves for the diary — so the card is not a detour to Login, it is the finish. **Parity with Login was the point that was easy to miss:** a join-a-firm sign-up that had to confirm its email leaves its invite code pending, and Login redeems it on the first sign-in that has a session, so the green card does the same (and shows the same join result), because without it the two buttons would have joined the same user to different places. A refusal is said on the card (the card *is* the screen, so it speaks through `_say` and `failureText`, which words either domain) and leaves the user on it with a working button. **The automatic check still signs nothing in** — the credentials are spent only by this deliberate tap — and a card restored after a restart holds no password, so `_backToLogin()` remains the way in there.
- **Google sign-up needs no confirmation flow, and the answer was measured, not assumed.** `signInWithGoogle` → `signInWithIdToken` returns a session immediately, so `SignUpConfirmationRequired` — the only thing that shows Check Your Email — is unreachable from that path; GoTrue sets `email_confirmed_at` for provider-verified addresses, so `AuthIdentity.emailConfirmed` is already true for a Google user; and the *Confirm email* setting under Auth → Providers → Email is the **email/password provider's** setting, which OAuth sign-ins do not participate in. Read on the live project (read-only, user confirmed as `postgres.lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`, rule 12): the one Google account in `auth.users` has its address confirmed. Recommendation to the user: **leave it off** — a second verification mail to an address Google already proved is friction with no security gain.
- **Tests, with teeth.** `auth_signup_test.dart` is now **31** (up from 29): the green-card case asserts the new label and that **Back To Login is gone from it**, and two new cases prove the tap — *Use The App signs in and leaves for the diary* (the address and password handed to `signInWithPassword`, the identity adopted, the pending invite code redeemed, both local records cleared, and the diary marker reached) and *a refused sign-in keeps the card and says so* (nothing adopted, the green card still the screen, and the failure said rather than silent).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `auth_signup_test.dart` **31/31**; full suite below. No server change, so no migration and no ledger rebuild.
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's *Check Your Email Screen Finishes The Sign-Up* bullet and Task 17's matching addendum each gained a **(0.0.414)** sentence — the deviation is the green card's action and its wording, since both already describe that card.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: sign up on a confirmation-on project, confirm the mail and return — the card turns **green** with one button reading **Use The App**; pressing it must put the user in the diary (not back on Login), and for a join-firm sign-up the firm must be joined with the code they typed.

### 2026-10-08 — The Check Your Email screen now finishes the sign-up — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to the Check Your Email card a confirmation-on project shows after sign-up).** (1) Add an **Open Mail** button that shows the mail apps — Gmail, Proton Mail, the vendor client — so the user picks the one holding the confirmation. (2) When they come back to the app, **check whether the address is really confirmed**: a **green congratulations card with only a Back To Login button** if it is, and the same screen as before if it is not. Three further items were added to the plan mid-flight at the user's request and are built here too: **Resend Email**, the screen's **state surviving an app restart**, and **Change Email Address** for a mistyped address — and for that last one the user chose, from two options, a **new password-checked server cleanup** over a client-only return to the form.
- **Two questions were asked first (rule 1), not guessed.** The confirmation signal: **ask the server again with the typed e-mail and password** (chosen), rather than only reacting to the mail link opening the app — which works whether the link opens the app or a browser page. And whether the screen should also offer a manual re-check: **no, automatic only**, because the only thing that asked for it was doubt about the return itself.
- **The check is a lifecycle event, not a tap, and it is not a sign-in.** A return from the mail app is `AppLifecycleState.resumed`, so the screen observes it and asks `AuthRepository.isEmailConfirmed` with the credentials the form is still holding. That call is deliberately **not** `signInWithPassword`: a session would hand the app the account — adoption, the router's signed-in redirect, a profile pull — and turn "Back To Login" into a sign-out. It is a read of GoTrue's password grant (`POST /auth/v1/token?grant_type=password`) whose reply is discarded: `200` means confirmed, the unconfirmed answer is the same refusal Login shows, and **anything else — offline, a wrong password, a 5xx, the server's rate limit — is simply "not yet"**, which leaves the screen exactly as the user left it. Nothing new appears on the screen while the answer is no, because the screen they are reading *is* the "not yet" state.
- **Confirmed turns the card green and spends the wait.** A green mark, a green title (**Email Confirmed!**), a one-line message naming the address, and **one** action — Back To Login (`AuthPrimaryButton`) — with no Open Mail, no resend and nothing to correct: the account exists and its address is verified, so Login is the only step left. The waiting record is cleared at that moment, so a process death afterwards lands on Login, which is where that button would have taken them anyway.
- **Four actions on the waiting card, and one shared style behind them.** **Open Mail** is the forward action and takes the filled button; **Back To Login** wears the app's outlined button; **Resend Email** and **Change Email Address** are quiet text links that guard themselves, so a second tap while one is in flight does nothing. Open Mail opens `mailAppUri()` — the bare `mailto:`, added beside the other documented URI routes in `contact_launcher.dart` and handed to the existing `ContactLauncher` seam, so **no recipient is named and Android raises its own chooser** rather than one compose window. A device that cannot handle it gets "No mail app could be opened." Resend calls GoTrue's own `resend` with the app's redirect passed again, so a resent link still opens the app, and shows the server's refusal verbatim — its rate limit already has its own sentence. `auth_widgets.dart` gained one **`AuthOutlinedButton`** and `AuthGoogleButton` now reuses it, so the outlined look has one definition instead of two.
- **The wait outlives the process, and the password never leaves memory.** `LocalAuthService` gained a pending-confirmation record — **the address and when it was written, never the password** — written when sign-up answers `SignUpConfirmationRequired`, and `appRouterProvider` opens **Sign Up** on a launch that has one, so a user who closes the app on the way to their mailbox comes back to the screen instead of to an empty form and no idea what happened. The record is ignored after **seven days**, and cleared when the address is confirmed, corrected, or the user leaves for Login; a completed sign-in clears it too, because a session can only exist for a confirmed address. **The honest limit, and the one thing the user should know:** after a restart the typed password is gone, so the automatic check cannot run — the restored screen still offers Open Mail, Resend Email and Change Email Address, and logging in is itself the proof the address is confirmed.
- **A mistyped address is now correctable, which it was not.** An unconfirmed account cannot sign in, so no client call can reach it to change its address, and `abandon_provisional_account()` works from `auth.uid()` and can never have a session here. The new `public.discard_unconfirmed_signup(p_email, p_password)` (`supabase/migrations/20261008110000`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public, extensions`) verifies the password against the account's own **bcrypt** hash with pgcrypto's `crypt()` — checked first, so only the account's owner learns anything — then refuses an address that is **already confirmed** (log in instead) and refuses an account that has **done anything**, with exactly `abandon_provisional_account()`'s guards (a firm, a role, a subscription, firm ownership or membership, an issued invite, any authored row, a payment, an approval, admin rights, a receipt), and finally removes the `auth.users` row so the profile, the preferences and the sessions cascade with it — **freeing the address** for a corrected sign-up. It is granted to **`anon`**, because this caller has no session at all, and is therefore **rate-limited**: five refusals per address per fifteen minutes, in the new **server-managed** `signup_discard_attempts` ledger (RLS on, no policies — nothing but the function can touch it). A missing account and a wrong password answer **identically**, so the function is not an address oracle. On success the screen brings the **form back with everything but the address still filled in** — the controllers were never disposed, so the name, bar association and password are still there and only the address is cleared, which is the whole point of the feature. A refusal keeps the screen and says which refusal it was; `DiscardSignupOutcome` is unit-tested directly, the way `outcomeFromDeleteResponse` is.
- **Verified on the real project, the project's own way.** Applied as migration **35** with `dart run tool/apply_supabase_migrations.dart` (which named `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau` before touching anything — AGENTS.md rule 12), then proved by a new `supabase/tests/discard_unconfirmed_signup_test.sql` in **one rolled-back transaction on the live database**: a wrong password refused with the account untouched; a confirmed address refused as `confirmed`; an account holding a custom court refused as `not_provisional` and keeping its row; a waiting sign-up **removed** with its profile and preferences cascading away, its attempts forgotten and its address free; five refusals filling the window and the **sixth answer `rate_limited`** even with the right password; and the **`anon` grant exercised** by removing an account as `anon` — the caller this function exists for. After the rollback the project was untouched (2 users, **1,016 seeded courts**, 0 attempt rows), and `courts` was asserted unchanged inside the transaction too. ### 2026-10-08 — Manage Account: the Logout button, Change Password back, and Delete Account wired end to end — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to Settings > Account & Profile > Manage Account).** (1) A **full-size Logout** button, the same size as the Delete Account button. (2) **Change Password** moved back from Account & Profile into Manage Account, placed after Logout; **Security** removed from Account & Profile. (3) **Delete Account wired**: clear all local data for an offline (free) user and delete the account on Supabase; clear local **and** cloud data for a premium user with **no residue** left in Supabase; and warn free/premium users first about what it does and that it is permanent. (4) A red bold section title before Delete Account. (5) The small Logout under the name and e-mail removed.
- **Five decisions were asked first (rule 1), each answered with the recommended option.** The **Security** sub-page is **deleted outright** (screen, route, ARB keys, mockup folder, test); the **Logout** button is the **same card as Delete Account**, in a neutral tone; **offline deletion is refused with a message** rather than queued or half-done; **firm accounts and app administrators are refused, with the reason**; and the confirmation is **one warning** with Cancel and a red Delete Account (no typed or two-step confirmation).
- **One card, two tones.** `SettingsActionCard` + `SettingsActionTone` joined `settings_menu_card.dart` — the shared sizing file — so "the Logout button is the same size as the Delete Account button" is a property of the code (16 px padding, 40 px icon circle, the 44 px content-row floor) rather than two layouts kept in step by hand. The screen's body is now: profile summary (avatar, name, e-mail only), the Logout card, the Change Password row (`/settings/change-password`), the red bold **Danger Zone** label, the Delete Account card.
- **The deletion, in four steps and in this order.** Warn (a dismissed window cancels); refuse offline (`settingsManageAccount_deleteNeedsConnection`, nothing local touched — the account row is only on the server); call the server; then `LogoutFlow.wipeAndComplete()` — the logout's own complete wipe **with the baseline reference data restored** — and `context.go(AppRoutes.login)`. A refusal (firm, administrator) or a failure each stop with their own sentence and delete nothing. The plan's wording matters: a **free** account's diary has never left the device, a **premium** account's is in the cloud as well, and the dialog says exactly that.
- **The server half: `delete_my_account()`** (`supabase/migrations/20261008100000_delete_my_account.sql`), `SECURITY DEFINER`, no arguments, `auth.uid()` only — the same shape as `abandon_provisional_account()`, but where that one refuses to remove an account that ever did anything, this one removes one that has done everything **and leaves no residue**. It **refuses, changing nothing,** a **firm account** (`profiles.firm_id` or owning a `firms` row: firm domain rows are shared) and an **app administrator** (their rows include other people's payment ledger), answering `{status:removed}` / `{status:refused,reason:…}` / `{status:unauthenticated}`. Otherwise it hard-deletes children before parents — the caller's `delete_requests` (a request they merely **reviewed** is somebody else's, so only its `reviewed_by` link is cleared), `conflict_log`, `case_history`, `notes`, `case_references`, `case_notification_preferences`, `cases`, their custom rows in the six editable reference tables (seeded rows have `user_id is null` and survive), `payment_approval_events` then `payment_submissions`, `mutation_receipts`, `firm_invites`, `firm_members` — and finally the `auth.users` row, which cascades `profiles`, the preference rows, `invite_attempts`, `app_admins` and the sessions. **The one deliberate exception:** `payment_approval_events` is an immutable audit ledger whose trigger refuses deletes for every role, so the function disables it for its own two statements and re-enables it **inside the same transaction** — it can never be left off.
- **Verified against the real project, on transactions that roll back.** Applied at migration 34 with `dart run tool/apply_supabase_migrations.dart`; the function is present, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, executable by `authenticated` and not by `anon` (checked on `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau` only). Then, inside `begin … rollback` blocks over a real account holding a case, a payment, its approval event and three receipts: the admin path answered `refused/administrator` and the owner-in-a-temp-firm path answered `refused/firm`, both changing nothing; the removal path answered `removed` with **every residue count 0** (users, profiles, cases, history, preferences, payments, approvals, receipts, requests, conflicts, invites), the **1,016 seeded court rows untouched**, and the ledger trigger **enabled again**; and after the rollbacks the project still had its 2 users, 1 case, 1 payment and 3 receipts.
- **Tests, with teeth.** New `delete_account_test.dart` (6): the free and premium warnings, Cancel deleting nothing, the offline refusal, a firm refusal and a failure each keeping the account, and a confirmed deletion wiping the device (`notes`, the profile cache and the remembered account all gone) and landing on Login. `manage_account_test.dart` gained *the action column is Logout, Change Password, Danger Zone, Delete* — the two cards' widths equal and the Logout card exactly as tall as the one-line row beside it, the label in `AppColors.error` and bold, the five elements in order by geometry, and exactly one logout affordance on the screen (so the one inside the profile card cannot come back). `accounts_profile_menu_test.dart` now pins that the menu offers **no Security row** (three cards for a free individual) and that Change Password is not offered from there. `security_screen_test.dart` and the Security mockup were deleted with the screen.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1425 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1420). Version **0.0.411** recorded by script; `--check` OK; the ledger rebuilt and `generate_all_supabase_sql.dart --check` OK. (Two tool crashes on the way — `FileSystemException: Deletion failed … errno = 5` from Flutter's own native-assets copy step, a Windows file lock after two test runs were killed mid-flight; clearing `build/native_assets` fixed it, and the suite then ran clean.)
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's §8 Account & Profile block now reads **Manage Account** + Subscription, the deleted Security bullet was replaced by the (0.0.411) Manage Account bullet with the `delete_my_account()` contract and its rollback verification, the recovery-code bullet's "pops back to Security" parenthetical was corrected to Manage Account, and Task 12 gained the (0.0.411) addendum paragraph, the `SettingsActionCard` line in its sizing contract, an updated design-reference list (no `settings_security`) and a corrected placeholder list (Manage Account's actions are no longer pending). The generated ledger `supabase/AllSupabaseSql.md` gained section 34 plus its `0.0.411` group heading, and the two migration-count guards were updated with it.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: Settings > Account & Profile > Manage Account — the profile card has no Logout of its own; Logout and Delete Account are the same size; Change Password sits under Logout and opens the reset flow, which returns here; a red bold **Danger Zone** precedes Delete Account; and tapping Delete Account shows the warning first, then (online, on an individual account) empties the diary and lands on Login.
