### 2026-10-07 — The framework error identified: the Settings rows' ListTile ink assert — FIXED

- **Request (the user's).** They installed 0.0.406, opened Diagnostics, and pasted the error it now names in full: *"ListTile background color or ink splashes may be invisible. The ListTile is wrapped in a DecoratedBox that has a background color…"* — the 179 bare `FlutterError` entries of the investigation, identified.
- **The site, matched from the assertion's own evidence.** The payload named a `DecoratedBox` with a **white** background and **top+bottom** borders of `Color(0.7765, 0.7765, 0.8039)` — exactly `#C6C6CD`, this app's `outlineVariant`. One place in the codebase builds that: `settings_main_screen`'s section card (white `AppColors.surface`, top/bottom `outlineVariant` hairlines, per the `settings_main` mockup) wrapping each section's `_SettingsListTile`s. Every `ListTile` with an `onTap` inside it reported the assert **once per row per build** — the bursts of ~10 the device log showed, one per visible Settings row.
- **Why tests never saw it.** `settings_drawer_share_test.dart` carried a `drainFrameworkNoise` helper that swallowed the assert, with a comment calling it "unrelated to Share and to this file's subject". It is deleted — after this fix those tests pass without draining, and a test that swallows framework noise cannot report the next one.
- **The fix is the codebase's own pattern.** `backup_restore_screen`'s schedule card already documents it: a shaped `Material`, not a decorated `Container`, "so each row's ink splash and background paint on it — a DecoratedBox in between trips a ListTile assert". The Settings section card is now `Material(color: AppColors.surface, shape: Border(top: …, bottom: …))` — the same white card and the same two hairlines (mockup unchanged, rule 9), with the rows' ink landing on it. The other `ListTile` sites were swept: Notes' sheet is a `showModalBottomSheet` (Material), court lists use `Card`s, the member sheet's switches sit in the sheet's own Material — none affected.
- **Teeth, measured.** `settings_drawer_share_test` now fails with the exact device assertion when the `Container` is put back (verified by reverting the one edit, running, and restoring) — before the fix it passed only because the drainer hid it.
- **What this closes.** The 0.0.403 ANR thread left "the first error the user's session produced is still unidentified"; it is this assert, and with the Settings screen no longer reporting on every layout the error log's ring stops being filled by noise — which is also what made the two real errors visible in the first place.
- **Records (rule 14).** A **(0.0.407)** sentence appended to the plan's *The App Keeps Its Own Error Log* bullet (closing the ANR loop), and a new bullet in Task 12's sizing-contract section stating the rule: a card wrapping `ListTile`s must be a `Material`, never a colour-bearing `DecoratedBox`.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1413 passed**. Version **0.0.407** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Device check on the next build: Settings rows should ripple on tap, and Diagnostics should stop growing new `FlutterError` rows while the Settings screen is used.

### 2026-10-07 — The error log now keeps the message, so an error can be fixed — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** "I want Diagnostics screen's error logs to show full error message so that it becomes easy to identify the error and fix it properly." — the entry after the investigation of `AuthRetryableFetchException` and 179 bare `FlutterError` rows.
- **Two forks were the user's to decide (asked, not guessed).** (1) *Where* the message may appear: chosen **Diagnostics screen + Copy Diagnostics + the Support message**, not the screen alone. (2) Whether the app's typed failures include the **server's reply**: chosen yes. Both reverse a documented rule, so both are recorded as such.
- **What an entry is now.** A fourth field, `detail`: a typed failure answers through a new `DiagnosableFailure.diagnosticDetail` (AuthFailure: server reply then cause; FirmFailure: cause; SyncFailure: its classified message; PaymentSubmitException: the server detail), the capture passes a framework error's text **with its library and context** (`"A RenderFlex overflowed by 12 pixels on the right. (rendering library · during layout)"` — this is what names a `FlutterError`), and anything else falls back to `toString()`. Everything is flattened to one line and cut at 500 characters; an error whose text is merely its own type adds no detail, so a row never grows a line that repeats its label. Old stored entries load unchanged (the field is optional in both directions).
- **Where it shows.** The Diagnostics row prints the message under the label (full text, wrapped, `bodySm`). The report gains it as an indented line under the aligned `time | area | label` columns, which is how Copy Diagnostics and the Support message both carry it — one formatter, so the two can never disagree.
- **The policy change, stated honestly.** The log's header used to promise "no raw server message is ever written"; that promise is gone, replaced by the reasoning: a label alone cannot be fixed from. The risk is answered by *where the text can go* — it leaves the device only inside a report the user copies or sends and can read first — and the encrypted bundle now carries the same detail for the same reason (still passphrase-gated, still the file the user exports deliberately).
- **Tests, including the two that had pinned the old rule.** `error_log_test`'s "never by its server text" and "named by type and nothing else" became the inverse: the label leads, the message is kept; plus flattening, the 500-char cut, no-detail cases, and old-JSON tolerance. New `error_capture_test.dart` pins `describeFlutterError`'s message + library/context line. `diagnostics_screen_test` proves the message renders; `diagnostics_report_test` proves the indented line; `support_screen_test` proves a server reply rides into the sent WhatsApp body.
- **Records (rule 14 — this reverses the plan's own rule).** A **(0.0.406)** sentence appended to the plan's *The App Keeps Its Own Error Log* bullet, to Task 17's matching addendum, and a correction to Task 19's bundle bullet (its `errors` entries now carry the message).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1413 passed**. Version **0.0.406** recorded by script; `--check` OK.

The scope no longer leads the screen; it sits with the two things a user must know before writing.

Restored, and the test below now pins the order so a future edit cannot silently drop a card.

### 2026-10-07 — Diagnostics: the two actions moved into the body — COMPLETE

Same rule as before on when they appear: only when the log has entries, because there is nothing to copy or clear otherwise.

Copy is the filled primary — the report is the one form of the log that can leave the phone — and Clear is outlined secondary. Both styles are spelled out because this app's theme paints `FilledButton` **and** `OutlinedButton` black, as `more_about_the_app_screen` already documents.

The probe was temporary and was deleted; the reason is recorded in a comment at the layout.

### 2026-10-07 — Support: the crash was an ANR, and the app's own error log was causing it — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to Settings > About > Support).** "Pressing the back button or Diagnostic button from the screen crashes the app."
- **It is not a Dart crash — the device said so.** Read only, on the phone the user is testing with (`M2101K6G`, Android 12): `ActivityManager: Completed ANR of com.legalcasediary.app in 9292ms` and `Killing … (adj 0): user request after error`, with Android's CPU dump showing one app thread at **~98% user CPU**. The app was hanging, not throwing; Android asked the user to close it. The logcat also shows the Dart VM service, so the installed build is a debug one — where framework assertions are live.
- **The app's own error log on the device was the smoking gun.** `adb shell run-as com.legalcasediary.app cat files/error-log.json` returned a **full ring: 200 entries, every one `framework | FlutterError`**, stamped 17:47:00 → 17:49:51 (171 s) with gaps down to **5 ms**. A framework error was being reported over and over while the user was on those screens.
- **Why it must be those two screens.** Support and Diagnostics are the **only** screens in `lib/` that rebuild from the log (`support_screen.dart:228`, `diagnostics_screen.dart:46`; every other use reads or records). `installErrorCapture` writes each framework error into that log, and `ErrorLog.record()` called `notifyListeners()` **synchronously, from inside the frame that failed** — so the notification rebuilt the listening screen *mid-failure*: `setState() or markNeedsBuild() called during build`, which is itself a framework error, recorded, notifying again, rebuilding again. A loop that never yields a frame, at 100% CPU, until input starves and Android ANRs it. Back and the Diagnostics row are simply the two taps that reach that screen.
- **The fix, one helper used by two methods.** `ErrorLog` now hands its notification to a microtask (`_notifyOutsideThisTurn`), so it lands once the frame has finished its build and layout phases and a listener may safely rebuild; `record` and `clear` both use it. A microtask needs no binding, which keeps this class usable from plain `test()` cases as before (a `SchedulerBinding` post-frame hook would have thrown outside a widget test). Nothing a user sees changed: the viewer still reflects a failure recorded while it is open, one microtask later.
- **Test, with teeth, on the mechanism.** New widget case in `test/core/error_log_test.dart`, *a screen that rebuilds from the log is told after the frame, never from inside it*: a `ListenableBuilder` over the log (standing in for Support's preview) beside a widget that records from inside its own build (what the capture does). It asserts the entry is there **and** that the listener was rebuilt. Reverting `scheduleMicrotask(notifyListeners)` to a direct call fails it with exactly the device's cascade — "setState() or markNeedsBuild() called during build" — and the file was restored byte-identically (md5 `6cfb1090…`).
- **What was NOT the trigger, measured rather than assumed.** The harness showed a 120 px `RenderFlex` overflow in the Daily Cause List's date switcher at the phone's width, and I first read that as the feeding error. Measuring the switcher with the **real bundled font** (IBM Plex Sans, 12 px, letterSpacing 0.5 — the default test font makes every glyph a square and inflates text by roughly half) gives **323.65 px needed against 360.7 px available** on this phone (and ~4 px of slack at 360 dp): it does **not** overflow on a device. The switcher was therefore left untouched — it is not the defect, and the change was not made. The first error the user's session produced is still unidentified; it is now recorded **once** instead of cascading, and the difference is that the screen no longer hangs on it.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; error-log + Support + Diagnostics + About + report suites **41/41**; full suite **1406 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1405). Version **0.0.403** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14 — the plan documents the log, not this timing).** A paragraph appended to the plan's *The App Keeps Its Own Error Log* bullet and to Task 17's matching addendum bullet.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: open Settings > About > Support, press Back and the Diagnostics row — the app must return/advance normally, and `files/error-log.json` must stay small instead of climbing to 200. Open, reported and unchanged: the Diagnostics screen cannot say *what* went wrong for a framework error, because the entry's label is the type — `FlutterError` — which is why the device's log reads as 200 identical rows.

### 2026-10-07 — Privacy Policy & Terms of Service: the status bar is the app's black again — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to the two document screens only).** "Both the screens currently show in full screen with white status bar. Change this to like rest of the app, black status bar." Nothing else on those screens, and no other screen, was to change.
- **The cause is 0.0.390's own move, one step removed.** Every shell screen is wrapped by `AppShellRoot`, whose `SafeArea(top: true)` is what keeps content out from under the status bar ("Shell screens own the app's status-bar inset"). Moving the two documents out of `_shellRoutes` to top-level routes — done so Back would return to Sign Up — took them out of that wrap. Nothing replaced it, so each screen's own `AppBar` (background `AppColors.surface`, `0xFFFFFFFF`) painted its white surface up behind the status bar and the phone's clock and icons sat on that white instead of on the app's black from the root `AnnotatedRegion` in `app.dart`. "Full screen with a white status bar" is exactly that.
- **The fix is the app's existing pattern, one wrapper.** `LegalDocumentScreen` now wraps its `SettingsSubPageModal` in `SafeArea(top: true, bottom: false)` — the very inset the paywall already carries, with its comment ("the shell's own SafeArea does not reach this route, which sits outside it") naming the same cause. Login and Sign Up get it from `AuthScaffold`, and Forgot Password only builds a `SettingsSubPageModal` on its in-shell variant, so these two were the last top-level screens without one. One file, no shared widget, no string, no mockup: the mockups are web pages with no OS status bar to draw, and the change is an inset rather than a visual token.
- **Test, with teeth.** `legal_document_screen_test.dart` gained *both documents leave the status bar to the app*: it pumps each of the two documents with a 24px top inset and asserts the `AppBar` starts at **y == 24**. It measures the screen's real geometry rather than a private style constant. Removing the `SafeArea` wrapper made exactly that test fail — the bar sat at y == 0, painting behind the status bar — and the other nine passed; the file was then restored byte-identically (md5 `48e2d82d…`).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; the legal suite **10/10**; full suite **1405 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1404). Version **0.0.402** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14 — the top-level move is recorded, its status-bar consequence was not).** A sentence appended to the plan's *The Legal Documents Are Readable Before Sign-Up* bullet and to the matching Task 17 bullet.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: open Terms of Service and Privacy Policy from Settings > About and from Sign Up — the status bar must be black with light icons, exactly as on the case screens, and Back must still return to About or Sign Up. **Reported, not fixed (out of scope):** `lib/features/payments/presentation/checkout_screen.dart` is a top-level route with no status-bar `SafeArea` either, the long-standing loose end this fix's pattern would also cure.

### 2026-10-07 — The wipe deletes through Drift, so the screens are told — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, on the 0.0.400 build).** "Only B's case in A's pending sync" was still there — the header count and the queued case survived a switch that had genuinely wiped the database.
- **The cause: the wipe deleted with raw SQL.** `wipeAllTables()` ran `db.customStatement('DELETE FROM "table"')` for every table. That removes the rows from the file but **tells Drift nothing**, so every live query over those tables keeps serving its last value for the rest of the app run. Measured, on the then-current code, with a listener on the very provider the header reads: after seeding one queued mutation the stream reported **1**, after the complete wipe it still reported **1**, while `SELECT count(*)` over `sync_queue` returned **0**. So the account switch was working — B's case and B's count were gone from the database — and the header badge and Sync Status were simply never told, which is exactly what the user was looking at.
- **The fix, one loop.** Every delete in `wipeAllTables` now goes through the table API — `db.delete(_tableInfo(name)).go()` for each table, and `db.delete(db.syncMetadata)..where((row) => row.key.isNotIn(keepMetadataKeys))` for the recovery carve-out — so each affected table's streams are invalidated, as every other writer in the app does. Re-measured: the same listener now reports `[1, 0]`. This is not only the count: every live query in the app (the note and case-reference libraries, the sync entry list, the profile rows) was stale after any wipe, so the same defect covered the previous account's records wherever a screen watched rather than re-read.
- **Verified with teeth.** `account_switch_leak_test.dart`'s switch case now subscribes to `syncQueuePendingCountProvider` **and** `syncQueueEntriesProvider` from *before* the switch, asserts both screens really were showing the previous account's work (`1`, one entry), and only then asserts they are told the work is gone. Restoring the raw-SQL deletes made exactly that test time out on the streams — the other four cases in the file still passed — so the assertion has teeth on the notification itself, not merely on the rows. Two probes were used and deleted; no scratch file remains.
- **Deliberately not changed.** `restoreSnapshot()` still deletes with raw SQL, and does not need this: the restore screen already re-opens the database afterwards (`ref.invalidate(databaseProvider)`), which is its own reload lever. Only the wipe had no such lever, because the logout and the account switch end on a screen the previous account's data must simply not be on.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; the eleven affected suites **127/127**; full suite **1404 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0). Version **0.0.401** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14).** One sentence appended to the plan §5 paragraph this work added and to both Task 19 bullets: the wipe's deletes go through Drift's table API.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. Device check: B signs in and queues a case, B's session ends without the Logout button, A signs in — A's header must show **0** and Sync Status must be empty, with no rebuild needed between the two.

### 2026-10-07 — Same device, two accounts: the second account's data is there, and the first account's is not — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, two reported device defects, "being extra careful not to change other things in the app").** (1) A logs out and B signs in on the same device; adding a case shows *Higher or Lower Court* and *Division* but **nothing for Court Category or anything after it**, and clearing app data fixes it. (2) B logs out without taking a backup and A logs back in: **B's case appears in A's pending sync and the header shows a count**.
- **Defect 1, one operation short of a fresh install.** `BackupRepository.wipeAllTables()` deletes the six editable reference tables **and** the `seed_migrations` ledger, because the plan's wipe scope says it must ("custom/seed data, seed ledger"). `SeedMigrationRunner` is run by `databaseProvider` only when the database is **opened**, so for the rest of that app run the baseline is simply gone. Extended-offline recovery already knew this and re-seeds explicitly ("the app's own baseline reference data first, then the authoritative rows over the top"); the logout path did not. Everything the user saw follows: only *Higher or Lower Court*, *Division* and *District* are static constants in the Add/Edit sheet, and **Court Category is the first database-driven field** (`districtCourtCategories` over `court_categories` + `courts`), so an empty schema empties it and every field after it — Name of Court, Case Category, Case Type, Steps, On Behalf — while the three static dropdowns still look right. A restart (or clearing app data) re-runs the seeds, which is exactly the workaround reported.
- **Defect 2, a requirement that was never wired.** Plan §5 opens with "the local Drift SQLite database and `sync_queue` are completely wiped whenever a user actively logs out" and the Revision 2.4 Addendum says "Logout/**account switch** follows the same complete wipe scope" — but only the Logout button ever wiped. A session the server revoked, one that expired, or one that could not be parsed back at all (`sessionMissing`) clears nothing on its own **by design**, so nothing was offered or wiped; `AuthViewModel._completeSignIn` then saw a different account id and dropped only the **caches** (`_forgetAccount`). Both probe results, on the app's own state machine: after `sessionExpired` and a sign-in as the next account, the previous holder's `notes` row and its `sync_queue` row were still there, `syncQueuePendingCountProvider` still counted them, and `adoptionBlocked` was **true** — the adopt refusal that was set and read by **no screen in `lib/`**, which is how the arriving account got the outgoing one's diary and count instead of a refusal.
- **The fix, one operation and two call sites.** New `BackupRepository.wipeToFreshInstall()` — the existing complete wipe, then `SeedMigrationRunner.runMigrations()` — so no caller can wipe without restoring the baseline; `LogoutFlow._wipeAndEnd()` uses it (recovery keeps its own ordering, since it seeds between its wipe and its pull), and `AuthViewModel._wipeLocalDiary()` runs it in the account-switch branch of `_completeSignIn` before adoption, which is the moment the wipe the Addendum demands can still happen. `signOut()` is untouched: the logout flow owns that wipe and already runs it. The fail-closed adoption check stays as the safety net behind the wipe. One question was asked first (rule 1) — destroy or refuse on a switch — and the user chose **Wipe And Start Fresh**, the plan's own rule.
- **Tests, with teeth.** `logout_flow_test.dart`'s `expectEverythingGone()` now asserts both halves: the account's own state gone **and** the baseline back (it previously pinned `seed_migrations` empty — the bug, in writing). Two new cases pin the reference data a next account needs and that the user's own category/edit is still gone. `account_switch_leak_test.dart` gained *a switch to another account wipes the leaving diary with its queue*: a local record and a queued mutation, the session lost, a sign-in as somebody else, then the rows gone, the **header's own provider** reading 0 (read through `ProviderContainer.listen`, since Riverpod 3 has no `.stream`), adoption no longer blocked, and the baseline present. **Teeth: reverting `wipeToFreshInstall` to `wipeAllTables` in the logout failed exactly the five baseline assertions and nothing else; removing the switch's wipe failed exactly the new switch test.** Both files were restored byte-identically (md5 `a63203ae…`, `55351e31…`).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; the eight affected suites **82/82**; full suite **1404 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1401). Version **0.0.400** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14 — the switch wipe was mandated but never implemented, and the baseline restoration was absent).** A new 0.0.400 paragraph in plan §5 and two bullets in Task 19 (Phase 6 item 4 and the Alignment Addendum). No new screen, string, mockup or schema: the change is one new method and two call sites.
- **Resume point (superseded by 0.0.401 below).** The device check reported the second defect still present: the rows were gone, but the screens were never told. That is the stale-stream defect the next entry fixes.

### 2026-10-07 — The header's sync button stops claiming a kind of sync a free account cannot do — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, two reports on the Cases, Notes and Case Reference headers).** (1) The app shows the **synced cloud-checkmark** for a free user too, while sync is totally off for a free account, so the **sync-off icon** should be shown. (2) When a free user saves a case, note or case reference — or changes any data that syncs to the cloud for premium users — the app shows a **count over the sync icon**; no count should be shown for a free user, because they do not sync.
- **Both symptoms, one file, three conditions.** `lib/shared/widgets/sync_button.dart` is the single control every bottom-nav screen's header builds (through `CasesScreenModal`) and the Admin Panel builds too, so all three named screens were one defect, not three. (a) `_showsOffCloud` knew only about the connection and the pause state, so a free account online and idle fell through to `Icons.cloud_done_outlined` — a checkmark claiming this device's work is safely in the account, which cannot be true when cloud sync is never active on the free plan. (b) The badge condition carried no entitlement gate, and the header passes `syncQueuePendingCountProvider` unconditionally; that count is of local `pending` queue rows, which **every write path populates deliberately** (rule 3's "100% ready to sync"), free accounts included — so saving a case really did raise a red 1 over the icon. (c) The free tooltip was reached only **last**, after the count and the connection, so a free account with queued work was told "1 waiting to sync".
- **The entitlement won every question, and that ordering is the decision.** `_showsOffCloud` gains `!isPremium` and takes precedence over the error mark (the engine never runs, so `sync_problem_outlined` would describe an attempt that never happened); the badge is drawn only for a premium account; and `_tooltip()` answers `sync_unavailable` **first**, so a free account never reads a queue count or *No Connection* — both describe a mechanism it does not have. Offline is incidental, free is permanent. Already right and deliberately untouched: the colour, the label and the tap were entitlement-aware before this, and the separate `sync_problem` badge counts only `blocked`/`conflict_pending`, states only a sync attempt can produce, so a free account can never have one.
- **The mockups were the source of the defect, and the user approved correcting them.** Seven case-screen headers (`allCases`, `disposedCases`, `dailyCauseList`, `caseReference`, `notes`, `overdueDates`, `upcomingCases`) drew the free variant as a greyed **`cloud_done`** — the very checkmark reported — with a `sync (disabled in Part A)` comment. All seven now draw `cloud_off` and say *the free-plan variant: the cloud-off glyph, no count* (rule 9 would otherwise have had the screens match the bug). `admin_panel/code.html` keeps its enabled success cloud: it is reached only by a signed-in admin with sync available. The eight `screen.png` files are unchanged and still lag by this one glyph.
- **One decision was asked and answered (rule 1):** for a free account that is *also* offline, the tooltip shows the premium message rather than *No Connection* — the entitlement is the permanent fact and offline is incidental (the icon is the off cloud either way).
- **Two existing tests were pinning the bug, and said so in their comments.** `daily_cause_list_test.dart` ("court hearing counts use Bengali digits") and `upcoming_cases_test.dart` ("the court title is the Daily Cause List heading, in Bengali") both harness a **free** account, seed two cases through `insertCase` (two queued sync rows), and asserted **two** Bengali ২s — "the court's hearing count, and the header sync button's pending count". They now expect exactly **one** ২, keep the strict "no Latin `2` anywhere" guard they existed for, and gain an assertion that the sync button carries no `Badge` at all, which is the free-account rule pinned on two more of the reported screens.
- **New coverage.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart`'s pump helper gained a `premium` flag (default true, so existing cases are untouched) and four cases: the free off cloud with no checkmark, no count with work queued, the entitlement tooltip rather than a queue count, and the free+offline tooltip. `library_search_test.dart` states its free premise explicitly (`premiumAccessProvider` overridden false) and asserts the off cloud and a bare button on **Notes and Case Reference** after a note and a reference are saved. **Teeth: each of the three conditions was reverted in turn** — dropping `!isPremium` from the off cloud failed exactly the three icon assertions, dropping it from the badge failed exactly the two count assertions, and putting the entitlement check back last failed exactly the two tooltip assertions; reverting the badge gate again failed the two Bengali-digit suites. `sync_button.dart` was restored byte-identically (md5 `d8caf36d1f25a23e7c8803002a799312`).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; the four affected suites green; full suite **1401 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1396). Version **0.0.399** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14 — this deviates from the plan, which named only the two premium clouds).** The plan's *The Header's Sync Button Shows Two Clouds* bullet gained the 0.0.399 free-account paragraph and the mockup correction, and Task 06's Alignment Addendum sync-button bullet gained the matching sentence; Task 06 item 2's "disabled with a localized entitlement explanation" needed no edit — this is that requirement finally being true of the glyph and the count too.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. For a device check: sign in free, add a case — the header must show the off cloud with no number over it, and its tooltip must read *Sync requires a premium subscription* rather than a count; then on Premium the same save must show the red count over the success cloud.

### 2026-10-07 — Logout: the safety copy is offered, never mandatory — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** In the backup system during logout only: **do not back up** for a premium user who has synced to the cloud, or a non-premium user who added no case, note or case reference and changed no default court/step/other settings data. For every other case the copy is **not mandatory** — show a popup titled **Backup Data to Local Storage** with two middle-aligned buttons, **Yes** and **No**, matching the design and button size of the Exit App? window.
- **Four decisions, asked first (rule 1) and all answered with the recommended option.** (1) The existing offline block and unsynced warning **stay**; only the export step becomes optional. (2) "Premium synced to the cloud" means **everything is on the server** — queue empty, or the mandatory sync completed this launch (so a premium account offline with an empty queue is silent too). (3) "Data worth a copy" is cases, notes and case references **plus any reference row added or edited** in the six editable reference tables (`is_custom` or `is_user_modified`); language, notification, view and theme changes alone do not trigger it. (4) Dismissing the passphrase window still **cancels the logout**, deleting nothing.
- **Detection, one method (rule 4 stated where it lives).** `BackupRepository.hasUserAuthoredData()` counts the three content tables through the existing `hasDataInTables`, then asks each of the six editable reference tables for `is_custom = 1 OR is_user_modified = 1`. Seeded reference data, settings and sync metadata are deliberately not data. It is a **different question** from `authLocalDataExistsProvider` — that gate asks "is this device already in use?" and ignores reference edits; the logout asks "would a wipe destroy something?" and counts them — so the two do not share a query, and each says why.
- **The flow.** `LogoutBranch.freeExportThenWipe` is renamed **`freeBackupChoice`** and `LogoutPlan.needsExport` becomes **`needsBackupChoice`**, because neither exports any more. `LogoutFlow.plan()` now selects `cleanWipe` for a free account with nothing authored; a premium account already synced was `cleanWipe`/`syncedThenWiped` before and is unchanged. `runBranchedLogout` keeps its step order (silent wipe → mandatory sync → offline block → warning) and every branch that used to call `_exportThenFinish` now calls `_backupThenFinish`, which opens `_confirmBackupChoice` first. **No** wipes through `flow.wipeAndComplete()` and says *Logged Out*; **Yes** runs the passphrase-then-export path unchanged; a **dismissed** window returns `null` from `_confirmBackupChoice` and cancels the logout — the app's other confirmations' rule — so an outside tap can never wipe.
- **The window.** A titled `AlertDialog` with **no body text** (the request named only the title and the two buttons), `actionsAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.center`, and two `TextButton`s at the Exit App? size — `minimumSize: Size(112, 48)`, `textStyle: fontSize 16`, repeated in `logout_dialogs.dart` with a comment pointing at the router's private `_exitButtonStyle`. One new ARB key, `logout_backupPromptTitle` (**Backup Data to Local Storage** / লোকাল স্টোরেজে ডেটা ব্যাকআপ করুন); **Yes**/**No** reuse `sharedActions_yes`/`sharedActions_no`. l10n regenerated.
- **Tests, verified with teeth.** `logout_flow_test.dart` gained the clean-free wipe, the reference-edit-only case (a `steps` row with `is_user_modified`) and a seeded-only case, and renamed the two moved members. `logout_dialogs_test.dart` gained the **No** path (no write, no passphrase window, logout completes), the **dismissed** path (ends 0, work intact) and the **window-shape** case (centred, 112x48, 16 pt). The shared helper `answerLogoutBackup` answers the new window, and `completeLogoutPassphrase` calls it first so existing call sites keep working; `manage_account_test.dart` and `offline_session_test.dart` now seed the note/case a free account needs, because a free account with nothing authored is silent by design. Teeth: making the reference predicate `AND` failed only the reference-edit test, and `if (!choice)` → `if (false)` failed only the No test; both files were restored byte-identically (md5 compared).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; the four logout suites **40/40**; full suite below. Version **0.0.398** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14 — this deviates from the plan's mandatory export).** Plan §5 branch (b)/(c) rewritten and a new bullet *The safety copy is offered, never mandatory (0.0.398)* added, the §Verification *Logout Flow Test* line updated, and the overview bullet changed; Task 19 Phase 6 items 2/3 rewritten with a new item 7 and the Alignment Addendum bullet extended.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed in code. For a device check: log out as a premium account with nothing pending (no window), as a free account with one case (window → Yes writes a file, No wipes), and as a free account with no data (no window).

### 2026-10-06 — Supabase > `user_preferences` was empty: the per-user preference path never existed — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, reported).** *"Supabase > `user_preferences` table is not updated with the user's data. The table is empty while I have 2 test accounts running and I have changed some settings like Language, Notification."*
- **Diagnosis, read-only on the allow-listed project (`lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`; ref taken from AGENTS.md rule 12, not from `list_projects`).** A count query returned `preference_rows = 0`, `case_preference_rows = 0` — while `accounts = 2`, `case_rows = 1`, `profile_rows = 2`. So the domain push works and both preference tables are genuinely untouched.
- **The cause: a documented requirement that was never implemented, on either side.** The plan (§4, line 1402) and Tasks 05/12/13/18 all say premium accounts sync these self-scoped; Task 05 even says *"Task 16 adds a self-only premium sync mirror"*. Nothing was built. `LocalSettingsRepository.upsertSettings` writes only the local Drift row — no sync columns, no `sync_queue` entry — and that is correct, because preferences are **deliberately excluded** from the generic push: `sync_entity_table()`'s allow-list is the twelve push/pull domain tables, and `sync_service_test.dart` pins that a ping for `user_preferences` is ignored. Meanwhile nothing in `lib/` touched the server tables: the only `'user_preferences'` literal was the wipe list in `backup_repository.dart`. Server-side the tables and their self-only RLS were already live, so **no SQL was owed** and rule 13 is untouched.
- **What was built — one service, one gate, five hook sites.** New `lib/features/settings/data/preference_sync_service.dart`: a `PreferenceSyncService` over a `PreferenceRemote` seam (`SupabasePreferenceRemote` / inert `OfflinePreferenceRemote`) plus the pure payload/companion mappers. `preferenceSyncServiceProvider` builds it (inert without a backend, like `UnavailableProfileRepository`), and `pushPreferencesProvider` in `account_mode.dart` is the **single premium-gated entry point**, returning immediately and never throwing so no save waits on the network. It is called from `SettingsViewModel.saveSettings`, the three `case_list_view_mode.dart` writers, the Add/Edit Case sheet's hierarchy-defaults save, its case save and its quick update — and once inside `accountRefreshProvider`, which is what makes the pull happen at launch, resume and reconnect.
- **Table class (rule 4), stated where it is enforced.** Both tables are **per-user preferences**: the service addresses only the caller's own rows, writes straight into the self-only server rows rather than queueing a domain mutation, and therefore maintains no `version`/`is_deleted`/`synced_at` columns and creates no `sync_queue` entry. A test asserts the local queue and all six firm-shared reference tables are still empty after a full push.
- **The three decisions the user settled.** (1) *Push on every save, with a remembered retry* — (2) *push and pull, newest wins* — (3) *both tables now*. The retry needs no flag: every attempt compares the two sides' `updated_at` row by row, so a save that could not reach the server simply leaves the local row newer than the account's and the next attempt sends it. **That comparison is the retry.** An equal pair is left alone, so neither side can win by accident.
- **Two details worth keeping.** (a) The `user_preferences` payload omits `theme` on purpose: nothing in the app writes it, and sending the stored `null` could only erase a value the account set elsewhere. (b) `case_notification_preferences.case_id` is a foreign key to `cases`, so a preference written here for a case the account has not received yet would fail the whole batch — `fetchKnownCaseIds` filters the push to cases the account actually holds, and the row waits on the device.
- **Live schema re-checked, not assumed.** A read-only `execute_sql` on `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau` returned both tables' columns, types and nullability (matching the payload name for name, including `updated_at bigint NOT NULL` and `enabled boolean NOT NULL default true`) and the six policies — SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE, self-only, no DELETE — which is exactly what an upsert needs. The deployed schema matches the migration files, so the fix is client-side only.
- **Tests, verified with teeth.** New `test/features/preference_sync_test.dart` (17 tests): the payload and companion maps, a newer local row pushed and not overwritten, a newer account row adopted with nothing pushed, the first-sign-in pull, the no-row-either-side push, an equal stamp writing nothing, the offline save retried by the next attempt, no account / no backend inertness, the per-case stamps resolving independently, the FK guard both ways, no queue entry and no firm-shared write, and the premium gate (free and signed-out both reaching nothing). Deleting the gate line made the free-account test fail, and relaxing `>` to `>=` made the equal-stamp test fail; both files were restored byte-identically (md5 compared).
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `preference_sync_test.dart` 17/17; full suite **1391 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0, up from 1374). Version **0.0.397** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14): deliberately none.** Plan §4 (lines 1402 and 1412) and Tasks 05/12/13/18 already describe this path, so this is the documented requirement implemented rather than a new change; and the Task 16 audit is not stale either — it claims only that the two *tables* exist and says outright that they carry no sync contract. Nothing was added to the plan or to any task file.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. To confirm on a real device, sign in as one of the test accounts on Premium, change Language or a Notification setting, and re-run the count query on `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau` — `user_preferences` should now hold one row for that account. Open, unrelated: the transient *Checking Your Subscription…* pill overflow, the Online Sync mockup's info-line drift, and the long-standing `paywall_planWasPrice` / `checkout_screen.dart` SafeArea / `_forgetAccount()` loose ends.

### 2026-10-06 — Online Sync: the sentence is as big as the info line, and spans the card — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to Settings > Online Sync).** (1) Make *"A subscription plan is required to sync across devices. Purchase a plan from here."* a bit bigger, the same as the info text above the Last Sync card. (2) The text is not shown in full available space to the right — fix it.
- **Size.** The sentence was `bodySm` (12/16, muted); the info line above the card is `bodyMd` (14/20, `onSurfaceVariant`). It now uses exactly that style — same size and same ink — via a local `body` style that the link span also inherits, so **here** stays `w700` + underlined and simply scales with the sentence.
- **Width — the actual cause.** The card's body line was drawn *inside* the header row, in the `Expanded` column next to the 48px icon (plus its 16px gap). That column is 64px narrower than the card for its **whole height**, not just beside the icon, so the sentence wrapped early with dead space to its right. It is now drawn after the row, in the card's own column, so it gets the card's full content width — which is exactly the width the `Sync Now` button takes (`double.infinity` in the same column). Measured at a 360px viewport: the sentence's box was **214px** against the button's 278px before, and **278 == 278** after; at 390, 308 == 308. The same move gives every other blocker's body line the full width too.
- **Finding, reported not fixed (out of the requested scope).** While probing the widths I found a *pre-existing* narrow-width defect on this card: the **checking** pill, `Checking Your Subscription…` (`settingsOnlineSync_checkingPlan`), needs ~337px in the test font and overflows its pill row by 160px at a 360px viewport (130 at 390, 108 at 412, none at 1080). It is transient — the pill is only drawn while the entitlement is still loading, and by the final layout it has been replaced by *Free Plan*/*Up to Date* (a tree walk of the final frame shows **no** horizontal overflow at any width). In the real app the label is ~197px against ~193px of pill width at 360dp, so it is a ≤~365dp edge case, most likely a few clipped pixels at worst. Not touched, because rule 2 keeps this change to what was asked.
- **Mockup (rule 9).** Both drawn status cards in `mockup_html_screens/settings_onlineSync/code.html` now put the body **outside** the header row, and the free-plan sentence moved from `font-body-sm text-body-sm` to `font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant` — the class set its own info line above the card already uses.
- **Tests, verified with teeth.** `online_sync_screen_test.dart` gained `the sentence is as big as the info line, and spans the card`: it asserts the sentence's style (size and ink) equals the info line's, and that the column the sentence is laid out in is exactly as wide as the `Sync Now` button's — a font-independent measure, since a wrapped `Text` reports its intrinsic width rather than the width it was given. Reverting the style, and moving the body back inside the header row, each made the test **fail**; the file was restored byte-identically (md5 compared). Suite 19/19.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `online_sync_screen_test.dart` 19/19; full suite **1374 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (exit 0). Version **0.0.396** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14).** Plan §6's free-plan bullet and Task 12's Alignment Addendum bullet each extended with the 0.0.396 sentence.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed on this change. Open: the checking-pill overflow above; `mockup_html_screens/settings_onlineSync/code.html`'s info line still reads *"Manage your real-time data sync across all your devices."* while the ARB and the app show the longer *"Your cases, notes and references are kept up to date on this device, and sent to your account when you are on Premium."* (pre-existing mockup drift, left alone); and the long-standing `paywall_planWasPrice` / `checkout_screen.dart` SafeArea loose ends.

### 2026-10-06 — Cross-account leak: the live reads and the device record now leave with the account — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, reported).** On one device: A logs out, B logs in — B can see A's data in **Subscription Details > Payment History** and in the **Admin Panel**, "even if the later user is not an admin". Answering the follow-up question, the user settled what "gone after re-login" means: a **fresh app run** shows and hides the Admin Panel and reads Payment History correctly, but **Settings > Account & Profile > Profile data** sometimes shows the previous account's data until a logout>re-login.
- **Root cause, two halves, neither of them in the database.** (1) Both screens are **live server reads** held by top-level, non-disposing Riverpod providers — `myPaymentSubmissionsProvider`, `adminStatusProvider`, `adminPaymentRequestsProvider`, and the same shape again for `firmMembersListProvider`/`firmInvitesListProvider`. They watch `activeSupabaseClientProvider`, which returns the app-wide `activeSupabaseClient` constant, so **nothing** rebuilt them across a session change; `signOut()` emptied the local rows and the profile/preference caches but never them. (2) The per-user SharedPreferences record (details, picture, language, backup history) was cleared **only** by `signOut()`, so a switch that began with a revoked or expired session — the SDK's own sign-out, which sets the identity and clears nothing — left A's record on the device, and the launch/resume refresh then merged B's server copy into it (blanks only) and pushed it to B's account.
- **The fix, one operation in one place.** `AuthViewModel._forgetAccount()` — the pull-only caches, the SharedPreferences caches, and the four account-scoped live reads, each named in a comment with the reason it belongs there. `signOut()` calls it, and so does `_completeSignIn` when the arriving account id differs from the remembered one (`_local.cachedUserId`). A cold start, or the same account arriving again (a token refresh, a restored session), drops nothing. The user approved extending the fix to the whole class rather than only the two reported screens.
- **Test, verified with teeth.** New `test/features/account_switch_leak_test.dart` (4 tests) drives the app's own state machine rather than the fix's internals: A signs in and reads their ledger, panel and roster; the session is lost with no Logout button; B signs in; B must read B's rows, no admin panel, no queue, no roster, and no device record. Each of the four invalidations was removed in turn and the matching assertion failed — including the firm pair and the admin status on their own. `FakeAuthRepository` gained a `signInUserId` field so a second sign-in can arrive as somebody else.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; all four new tests pass; full suite **1371 passed** with `--concurrency=2`. Version **0.0.394** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's §12.3.1 *Lifecycle hardening* bullet extended; Task 19's addendum bullet (the wipe/invalidation rule), Task 21 (Payment History), Task 23 (the panel, plus its non-admin checklist item) and Task 17 (the profile record on a switch) each extended.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed on this fix. The two long-standing loose ends still stand: `paywall_planWasPrice` is dead and the docs' "misnamed" note about it is stale (the `_PlanRow` field names are correct), and `checkout_screen.dart` still needs the paywall's status-bar `SafeArea` inset.

### 2026-10-06 — Subscription Details: the payment-card number is localized — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped).** "Make changes only in Settings>Subscription>Subscription Details screens: Subscription Details>Payment History card number is not localized for Bangla language." Scope was confined to the Subscription Details screen.
- **The defect.** Each Payment History card's number — the small tinted circle that marks the purchase — was a raw interpolation, `Text('$number')`. A `'$number'` is always ASCII, so বাংলা read `1` and `2` while the amount and the Date & Time on the very same card were already in Bengali digits and Bengali month/time words. This is the same class of defect the calendars got `formatDigits` for in 0.0.187 and the court case counts in 0.0.201.
- **The fix, one call.** The number now renders through `AppDateUtils.formatDigits(number, l.localeName, grouping: false)` — the helper the amount beside it already uses — so English reads `1`/`2` and বাংলা reads `১`/`২`. `grouping: false` because a card number is an identifier (a position), not a quantity, so it can never read `১,২`-style grouping. **No ARB key and no new string:** a number needs no translation, only the active language's digits, exactly as the earlier count fixes concluded.
- **Nothing else touched.** One widget's `Text` on one screen; no layout, wording, icon, colour, route or data change.
- **Test, verified with teeth.** `test/features/subscription_details_screen_test.dart` gained `the card numbers use the locale's digits`: it pumps the screen under `Locale('bn')` with two purchases and asserts `১` and `২` are shown and the Latin `1`/`2` are **not**. `pumpDetails` gained a `locale` parameter (default English) so a case can pump বাংলা. Run against the old `'$number'` interpolation it **failed**; it passes with the fix. The existing English test still pins the Latin digits under `en`.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A sentence appended to the plan's Subscription Details bullet (line 319) and a bullet in `docs/tasks/21_payments_and_subscription.md`'s Alignment Addendum.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; `subscription_details_screen_test.dart` 10/10; full suite **1365 passed** with `--concurrency=2`. Version **0.0.391** recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Gotcha, noted for next time.** `tool/record_version.py` prints its entry with the console's default code page, so a summary containing Bengali digits crashes on Windows (`UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap'...`). Re-run with `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8`; the file itself is UTF-8 and was never written by the failed run.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. The unrelated open question still stands whether AGENTS.md rule 11 should be amended so Title Case applies to titles only, project-wide.

### 2026-10-06 — Sign Up: Back from the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy returns to Sign Up — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, follow-up).** Viewing the Terms of Service or Privacy Policy from Sign Up, the back button did not return to Sign Up.
- **The cause.** Both documents were registered as **shell routes** (`_shellRoutes`). A shell route's page lives on the shell's nested Navigator, so a document pushed from Sign Up (a top-level route) had nothing beneath it on that Navigator and its back button (`Navigator.maybePop`) did nothing. Opened from Settings > About the same button worked, because there the shell Navigator had About beneath it — which is why the report was specific to Sign Up.
- **The fix.** The two documents are now **top-level routes** (the list that already holds the paywall and checkout), not shell routes. They are full-page screens that need no shell, `SettingsSubPageModal` draws its own header, and both entry points now put them on the root navigator, where back returns to Sign Up or to About. No screen, string or mockup changed.
- **How it was pinned (— measured, not assumed).** A new test in `test/core/app_router_guard_test.dart` drives the **real** router: Sign Up → push Terms → tap Back → Sign Up is shown again. It was run against the old shell placement and **failed** exactly as reported ("Found 0 widgets with type SignupScreen"), then passed after the move.
- **One test gotcha, recorded in the test itself.** `pumpAndSettle` cannot be used around the document: it shows a spinner until its bundled asset loads, and that is real asynchronous I/O which never completes under the widget tester's fake clock (already documented in `legal_document_screen_test.dart`), so a settle times out. The test pumps a fixed number of frames instead.
- **Files.** `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (the two `GoRoute`s moved from `_shellRoutes` to the top-level list, with a comment naming the cause). Records extended in the plan and `docs/tasks/17_auth_profiles_and_firm_onboarding.md`; the earlier shell-placement formatting glitch was also corrected.

### 2026-10-06 — Sign Up: the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy links open the same screens as Settings > About — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, scoped to Sign Up).** "Terms of Service & Privacy Policy in Signup screen should show the same screens as Settings>About>Terms of Service & Privacy Policy screens." Scope was stated as Sign Up only.
- **What was already true.** The Sign Up card already linked to the exact routes Settings > About pushes — `AppRoutes.settingsTermsOfService` and `AppRoutes.settingsPrivacyPolicy`, both building `LegalDocumentScreen`. So the screens were never a separate copy.
- **The real cause.** `appRouterProvider`'s sign-in guard sent a not-signed-in visitor away from any non-auth route, so on a brand-new install (where `authGateRequiredProvider` demands sign-in) tapping either link landed back on Login. The guard is the only reason the documents were unreadable before sign-up.
- **The fix.** The two legal-document routes are now exempt from the sign-in gate — exactly those two, and nothing else. The redirect was also extracted into a pure `resolveRouteRedirect` (same rules, same order) so the guard can be unit-tested without building the whole router. Both stores additionally require the documents to be reachable before sign-up, so this closes a compliance gap too.
- **Files.** `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (`_isPublicLegalDocument`, `resolveRouteRedirect`, the redirect now delegates to it). No settings screen changed, and no ARB string changed, because the documents were already right.
- **Tests.** New `test/core/app_router_redirect_test.dart` (8) pins every guard rule including the exemption; new `test/core/app_router_guard_test.dart` (3) builds the **real** router over the auth harness and proves a brand-new install opens Login while Terms and Privacy render `LegalDocumentScreen`. That test was verified to fail with the exemption removed, so it guards the actual regression. `test/features/auth_signup_test.dart` now covers both links (Terms and Privacy) instead of only Terms.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1363 passed** with `--concurrency=2` (up 12 from the new tests). Version `0.0.389` recorded by script; `--check` OK.
- **Records (rule 14 — the Task 17 guard wording did not have this carve-out).** A bullet in the plan's § Auth & Onboarding area ("The Legal Documents Are Readable Before Sign-Up") and a bullet in `docs/tasks/17_auth_profiles_and_firm_onboarding.md`'s Alignment Addendum.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. The unrelated question still open from 0.0.388 is whether AGENTS.md rule 11 should be amended to make Title-Case-for-titles-only a project-wide rule.

- **Request (the user's).** In Backup & Restore, the **Encryption On Backup** note and paragraph text was written in Title Case (every word capitalized). The user wants **normal sentence capitalization** for paragraphs: "capitalization of the first letter of every word is for title only in the app. Regular paragraph follows the usual capitalization."

The pair is changed together on purpose: they swap at runtime with the toggle, so re-casing only one would show a Title-Case paragraph and a sentence-case one for the same sentence. Generated l10n regenerated with `flutter gen-l10n`.

The mid-sentence reference to the section keeps its exact label ("Turn on Encryption On Backup to lock them behind a passphrase") so the reader can find the section they were just told to tap.
- **The mockup already agreed.** `mockup_html_screens/settings_backupRestore/code.html` writes its own note in sentence case ("Backups are encrypted and stored in your local storage or cloud provider."), so this moves the ARB toward the mockup rather than away from it.

### 2026-10-05 — Backup encryption is opt-in, off by default — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** In Backup & Restore only: add a collapsible **Encryption On Backup** section *before* Automatic Backup that first explains what encryption buys and what it costs (remembering the passphrase), then offers **Enable** and **Disable** on one line in the section's own button style; backups stay unencrypted until the user enables it; and update the docs that described encryption as mandatory. Three questions were asked first: the switch **governs every export including the diagnostic bundle**, an unencrypted backup is a **readable file** (not a device-sealed one), and the passphrase is still **asked fresh each time** and never stored.
- **What "off" means, in one place.** A new per-user preference `BackupMetadata.encryptBackups` (SharedPreferences `backup_encryption_enabled`, default false) is read by every export: the manual local/Drive backup, the logout safety copy, the extended-offline recovery archive, and the diagnostic bundle. `BackupRepository.exportSnapshot` and `exportDiagnosticBundle` now take an optional secret — no secret writes the readable versioned JSON envelope, a secret seals the existing authenticated container. No secret is ever stored.
- **Restore is honest about the file, not the setting.** `looksLikeEncryptedBackup` (pure Dart, beside `looksLikeSpreadsheet`) lets the screen ask for a passphrase **only** when the picked file is an encrypted container, so an old sealed backup still opens with the toggle off and a plaintext one never asks.
- **The section, and the mockup departure.** The screen gains `_Section.encryption` with a `_CollapsibleSection` (shared `SettingsMenuCard`) and an `_EncryptionCard`: the usefulness/challenges copy, a state caption, and Enable/Disable as an active-filled / inactive-outlined pair in the section's geometry. The recovery screen's passphrase fields now appear only when encryption is on.
- **Files.** `backup_metadata_store.dart` (preference), `backup_repository.dart`, `backup_restore.dart` (detector), `diagnostic_bundle.dart`, `backup_restore_screen.dart`, `logout_flow.dart` + `logout_dialogs.dart`, `recovery_flow.dart` + `recovery_required_screen.dart`, `sync_status_screen.dart`, both ARBs (+ generated l10n). Records (rule 14 — the plan said the opposite): plan §12.3.1 revised and a Settings §8 sub-bullet added; Task 15 gained *Encryption is opt-in, off by default*; Task 12's Backup & Restore note points at it.
- **Tests.** 1351 passed, `flutter analyze` clean. New: an unencrypted export writes the readable envelope and records it; with encryption off a backup asks nothing; the section switches the preference; `looksLikeEncryptedBackup` tells a container from plaintext; the diagnostic bundle is readable with no secret. The suites that expect a passphrase (screen, logout, manage-account, offline-session, recovery) now turn encryption on the way the section does.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Worth one device look: the new section's Enable/Disable on a phone, and an unencrypted backup's behaviour in the platform Save-to dialog.

### 2026-10-05 — The app ignores the phone's own font size — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** "Show the text of the whole app at a standard font size, irrespective of the user's font settings in the phone", so a device set to a very large scale stops making the app look awkward and break visually.
- **What already existed.** Settings > View Settings already had a Font Size stepper (`font_size_step`, −2..+6) applied by `FontSizeScope` at the app root — but the scope *wrapped* the device's `TextScaler` and added the step on top, so the phone's own scale still multiplied every size. That was exactly the input the user wanted gone.
- **The change.** `FontSizeScope` now always installs its own scaler: the base is `TextScaler.noScaling` (the app's designed sizes) and the user's step is added to that, so the inherited device scale is **replaced**, not wrapped, and cannot reach a widget below. Step 0 installs `TextScaler.noScaling` directly; the in-app stepper stays the only control that changes app text. `SteppedTextScaler`'s class doc now describes it as a plain additive wrapper over whatever base it is given (the app passes no-scaling), not as a platform-scale wrapper.
- **Files.** `lib/core/theme/font_size_scope.dart` (the scope plus the class and library docs), comments in `app.dart` and `view_settings_screen.dart`, and `test/core/font_size_scope_test.dart` — the two cases that asserted the platform scale is kept now assert it is dropped (step 0 → 16 px, step 2 → 18 px under a 2× device scale), and "the default step leaves the tree untouched" became "the app size is exactly the design size". `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` F2 now checks the phone setting changes nothing and that the in-app max step does not clip.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** `font_size_step` and the fixed-base rule were added to plan §10.1, and a bullet was added to Task 12's View Settings section.
- **Tests / version.** `flutter analyze` clean; affected suites 55/55; full suite **1346 passed** with `--concurrency=2`; version **0.0.386** recorded by script, guard OK.
- **Tradeoff, deliberately taken.** This switches the OS accessibility text scale off for the app; a user who needs larger text uses Settings > View Settings > Font Size, which still reaches +6 px on every size. The user asked for exactly this and the in-app control keeps a path to larger text.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed.

### 2026-10-05 — The payment ledgers carry a readable plan name — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** In Supabase, `payment_submissions` and `payment_approval_events` show `plan_type` as `monthly`/`annual` rather than a plan name; store the real names — `1 Month`, `3 Months`, `6 Months`, `1 Year` — either in `plan_type` or in a new column, whichever is better.
- **Why `plan_type` was not overwritten.** It is a machine key, not a label: `approve_payment` validates it against the accepted set and its `CASE` selects both the expected amount and the interval the plan extends by, so a name written into it would break payments and force the function to be rewritten. `payment_approval_events` is an immutable ledger, so replacing its key would erase what the audit row means; and a name in a data column is locale-wrong, because the app already renders `paymentPlanLabel` in English *and* Bengali from the key.
- **The choice: a generated `plan_name` column on both tables.** `20261005120000_payment_plan_name.sql` adds `plan_name text generated always as (case plan_type … end) stored` — `1 Month` / `3 Months` / `6 Months` / `1 Year`, falling back to the raw key for any future plan. Generated, so it can never drift from `plan_type`; no data migration, because existing rows compute on the spot; and no change to the approval path or to either table's write privileges. The English names mirror the ARB labels the app localizes from, so the column is a server-side readability aid, not the app's display source. A normalized `subscription_plans` lookup table was considered and rejected: it would rewrite `approve_payment` and add a table plus a join for a display concern.
- **Applied and verified on `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`.** The project's own applier could not run — its `supabase/.env.local` password is still the `REPLACE_WITH_DB_PASSWORD` placeholder — so the migration went through the Supabase connector, and the connector's own timestamped history row was reconciled to the applier's canonical shape (`20261005120000` / `payment_plan_name` / `statements NULL`) so `tool/apply_supabase_migrations.dart` still sees the set as applied. Verified by query: the existing rows in both tables read `1 Month` for `monthly`.
- **Files.** New migration; `tool/generate_all_supabase_sql.dart` (the ledger section and its group title), `supabase/AllSupabaseSql.md` regenerated — now 33 migrations; two count pins moved 32 → 33 — `test/supabase/all_supabase_sql_test.dart` and `test/supabase/migration_applier_test.dart` (the latter's `versions.last` follows the new file). Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan): a new bullet in plan §7 plus a `plan_name` row in both table definitions, and a Task 21 Addendum bullet.
- **Tests / version.** `flutter analyze` clean; `test/supabase/` 78/78; full suite **1346 passed** with `--concurrency=2`; version **0.0.385** recorded by script, guard OK.
- **Noted, not mine.** `AGENTS.md` was already modified in the working tree (rule 15's text replaced by a blank line); left untouched.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed.

### 2026-10-04 — Manage Associates is not offered to a free individual — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** The Manage Associates screen must stay hidden for free accounts. One question was asked and answered: only a free **individual** loses the entry — a firm owner keeps it after their plan lapses, because the server still lets an owner manage the firm they already have and plan §6 promises a lapsed owner keeps it.
- **What the row did before.** `Accounts & Profile` rendered it whenever `canOpenManageAssociatesProvider` was true, and that provider answers a **permission** question, not a plan one: it is true for every individual (adding the first associate is what creates the firm) and for a firm owner. A free account was therefore shown a row whose screen could only offer a Paywall button.
- **The fix, kept in the screen on purpose.** The row is offered when `canOpenManageAssociatesProvider` is true **and** the account is either in firm mode or premium (`firmModeProvider || premiumAccessProvider`). The shared provider keeps its meaning, because the screen itself uses it to choose between the create-firm body and the owner-only sentence — so a free account arriving by deep link still lands on the honest Paywall state rather than an owner-only message that would be false for an individual. **Unknown** entitlement counts as not-premium for the entry: a free account must never see the row, and the cost is that a premium account's row appears a moment after the profile pull lands (the same direction the sync gates already take).
- **Tests.** `manage_associates_test.dart` gained three entry cases beside the existing associate one — a **premium** individual sees the row, a **free** individual does not, and a **lapsed owner** still does — with the pump block the new cases would have copied for the fourth time folded into one `pumpMenu` helper, and `seedOwner` gained `premium: false`. Teeth: restoring the old condition fails exactly the new free-individual case and nothing else (premium individual, associate and lapsed owner stay green). `flutter analyze` clean; the file **19/19**; full suite **1346 passed**.
- **A full-suite run worth reading honestly.** The first run at default parallelism died at test 358 with `error: Out of memory` from the Dart VM (plus a JIT compiler crash) — no test had failed. After killing the orphaned processes, the same suite at `--concurrency=2` passed **1346, STATUS=0**. The crash was the runner's memory, not this change.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** Plan §6 gained *A Premium Entry Is Not Advertised To A Free Account*; Task 20 Phase 1 item 2 — the premium gate — gained the as-built note.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed.

### 2026-10-04 — Back on the diary screens offers Exit, not the previous screen — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** From Cases, Notes and Case Reference, pressing back should **always** show the Exit App? window; the app was returning to the previous screen. One scoping question was asked and answered: all eight bottom-nav screens — the six case lists, Notes and Case Reference — with Settings left alone.
- **Why back returned, and why the shell's window could not answer.** These routes are *pushed*: the navigation drawer pushes every entry it opens, and the All Cases summary cards push Active Cases and Disposed Cases. GoRouter's `popRoute()` tries the shell's inner navigator **before** the root one, so a pushed page with no `PopScope` of its own simply popped; `AppShellRoot`'s exit window sits above that navigator and is only reached when nothing else can pop. The first attempt hooked the window in `AppShellRoot` and the tests showed the callback never fired for a pushed page — the mechanism, not the code, was wrong.
- **The fix, in the one widget those eight screens share.** `CasesScreenModal` — the reusable shell every bottom-nav screen is built on — now carries `PopScope(canPop: false)` on its body: back offers `AppShellRoot.requestExit`, the same window as the root, whatever history the screen carries. An open drawer still closes on the first back (`Scaffold.maybeOf(screenContext)` → `closeDrawer()`), which is what back did there before, so the gesture gains the window only where it used to navigate away. Settings and its sub-pages keep the ordinary pop.
- **Tests.** New `test/core/app_shell_exit_test.dart` pumps the **real** screens inside the real shell: all eight, each opened from another screen, assert the window appears, the opening screen is not reached, and Cancel stays; a Settings sub-page still pops; an open drawer closes first and the window comes next; Exit reaches `SystemNavigator.pop`. Verified to **fail against a non-blocking gate** (3 of 4, `Found 0 widgets with text "Exit App?"`) and pass after. The bodies release their live Drift streams in a `finally`, because a failing expectation used to leave the tree standing and hang every later test — the first teeth run cost ten minutes proving that; the second exits in seconds.
- **Records (rule 14 — the plan stopped at the root screen).** The plan's §6 gained the extension, and Task 06's Phase 1 item 3 now points at its Phase 2 item 6, which describes the behaviour as built.
- **Tests / version.** `flutter analyze` clean; the new suite 4/4; full suite **1344 passed** (the date-dependent Bengali calendar case was green today); version **0.0.383** recorded by script, guard OK.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. Worth one device look: back from each named screen with a screen behind it, and with the drawer open — the first back should close the drawer, the second should offer Exit.

### 2026-10-04 — Payment History's Date & Time wrapped too — FIXED

- **Request (the user's).** After the expiry fix, the history card's Date & Time wrapped its AM/PM the same way. Why, when there is space to its left?

The timestamp needs more than half; the short Paid value wasted its half. The same disease as the expiry row, a different organ: my earlier fix touched only `_DetailRow` in the summary card.

Change nothing else.

### 2026-10-03 — Payment History became cards, and the values right-align — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** On Settings > Subscription only: make the Role, Current Plan and Expiry values right aligned, and show Payment History entries as cards instead of tables.
- **The right-alignment bug, and why it was invisible.** The value was in a `Flexible`, and `Flexible` is only as wide as the text inside it — so `TextAlign.right` aligned the text inside its own little box and it rendered immediately after its label. The card read as one left-aligned column with a label glued to each value. `Expanded` fixes it because the value then takes the rest of the row and right-aligns against the card's edge. The **Firm** row is a `_DetailRow` too and aligns with them, because right-aligning three of four rows would look broken.
- **The first test for it proved nothing, and that mattered.** I asserted `Text.textAlign == TextAlign.right` — which passes in **both** layouts, because the property is correct either way. The teeth check caught it: with `Flexible` put back, the test still passed. It now **measures the edge** — each value's `getTopRight().dx` against the card's content edge (420 less the page margin and the card padding) — and fails with `Expected 388.0 / Actual 380.5` when the layout is wrong. A property assertion on a layout question is the wrong instrument.
- **Cards instead of a table.** Each purchase is its own card in the standard Settings card: the number in a small tinted circle, the plan as the title, the verdict as a pill beside it, then a hairline and the two figures side by side — **Paid** on the left, **Date & Time** right-aligned on the right — with the reviewer's note underneath when there is one. The two column captions that carried meaning survived as captions inside each card, so **Paid** and **Date & Time** are still named; the two that only ever headed a column (**No.**, **Status**) are gone and their strings were deleted from both ARBs rather than left dead. The five-column table, its fixed widths and its sideways scroll are gone — they existed to stop a wrapped date column from drifting, and a card has no columns to drift.
- **The reviewer's note came with it.** The sheet's list showed it and the table dropped it; a rejection always carries a reason, and a ledger that hides it leaves a user who sent money guessing why no subscription arrived. It is back, and pinned by its own test.
- **Files.** `lib/features/settings/presentation/subscription_details_screen.dart` (table → cards, `Expanded`, pill restored), `test/features/subscription_details_screen_test.dart` (table test → two card tests plus a measured alignment test; now 7), both ARBs (2 dead keys removed), 3 generated l10n files, `docs/implementation_plan.md` and `docs/tasks/12_settings_screens.md` (both said *table* — my own record from the previous turn, now corrected).
- **Tests.** 7 in the details suite: firm owner, free individual, unknown entitlement, a card per purchase with all five facts, a rejection showing its note, the empty state, and the measured alignment. `flutter analyze` clean; the four affected files **19/19**. Full suite and version follow.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed on this thread. Still true from last turn: the page has no mockup, so its appearance is the one thing only a device can confirm.

### 2026-10-03 — Subscription Details is a sub-page with a ledger table — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** On Settings > Subscription only: (1) *Subscription Detail* is a popup today, make it a sub-menu page like the other sub-menu pages; (2) inside it show Role, Current Plan (Free / 1 Month / 3 Months / 6 Months / 1 Year), Expiry (date and time), and a **Payment History** section listing every purchase as **Serial - Plan - Paid - Date & Time - Status**, as a **table**; (3) it must look premium, professional and on-theme. Three ambiguities were asked rather than guessed: whether Role keeps today's design (the user's own wording was "whatever the current app design is"), whether five columns wrap or scroll on a phone, and whether the Plan cell reads "1 Month" or "1 Month Plan". All three answered, all three recommended options taken.
- **Why a sub-page and not a sheet.** It is reference material a user *reads* — a plan, an expiry and a ledger they reconcile against a bank or MFS statement — not a choice they are making, and the ledger needs room a bottom sheet never had. `/settings/subscription/details` is a `SettingsSubPageModal` like every other Settings destination: same header, same back button, room to scroll.
- **What it shows.** A summary card — **Role**, the firm where the account is in firm mode, **Current Plan**, **Expiry** — then **Payment History** as a real table: a header row (No., Plan, Paid, Date & Time, Status) and one row per purchase, numbered from 1, newest first, with the verdict as a tinted pill so a row that still needs attention is scannable. Columns are fixed width and the table scrolls sideways rather than wrapping, because a wrapped date column puts every row's baseline somewhere different and a ledger whose columns do not line up cannot be read. Empty state says *No Payments Submitted Yet* rather than drawing an empty grid.
- **Two rules the page deliberately keeps.** An entitlement that has not landed reads a dash and **never** *Free* — a premium user whose profile pull is in flight must not read Free beside an expiry that names a date (the plan's own entitlement rule, since this screen is where the user comes to check). And every timestamp is **Bangladesh civil time with its date and clock taken from the same shifted instant**.
- **A test failure that proved the code was right and my expectation was wrong.** The expiry asserted 02/12/2026 and rendered 01/12/2026, 11:30 PM. Rather than adjust either side by eye, the test was made to print every `Text` it rendered; it showed `17:30 UTC + 6h = 23:30 on the same day`, so the screen was correct and my arithmetic was not.
