### 2026-10-03 — An offline Online Sync card was green — FIXED

- **Request (the user's).** "Make changes only in the Online Sync screen: No Connection and WIFI Off icon should be red instead of green when internet is off."
- **The cause.** The sync card's one tint was decided by `attention > 0` alone, so with nothing blocked or conflicted the fallback was `AppColors.success` **whatever the blocker was**. Offline therefore rendered a green "No Connection" pill and a green `Icons.wifi_off_outlined` directly above a **disabled** Sync Now — the screen saying everything was well at the one moment nothing could sync. Nothing else on the card was wrong: the label, the icon, the disabled button and the explanation were all already correct, which is why it read as a colour mistake rather than a broken state.
- **The fix.** One term in the existing tint chain: `needsAction || blocker == _SyncBlocker.offline`. That colours the pill, its label text, the 48dp icon and its background tint together, so the whole indicator reads as the fault it is. **The mockup already asked for this** — `mockup_html_screens/settings_onlineSync/code.html` draws the blocked card with `text-error` / `bg-error-container`, so this restores rule 9 conformance rather than departing from it.
- **Files.** `lib/features/settings/presentation/online_sync_screen.dart` (the tint chain only), `test/features/online_sync_screen_test.dart` (the existing offline case).
- **Tests.** The offline case now asserts the "No Connection" label and every `wifi_off_outlined` icon are `AppColors.error`, not just that the button is disabled. Verified to **fail against the pre-fix tint** (`Expected: red 0.86/0.15/0.15 / Actual: green 0.02/0.59/0.41`) and pass after. `flutter analyze` clean; the three sync suites **27/27**. No other test pumps this screen.
- **Records (rule 14).** None to the plan or task files: the plan does not specify tint logic, and the binding mockup already specifies red for the blocked card, so there was nothing new to record. Version **0.0.377**.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed. The same fallback still paints `signedOut`, `needsPremium` and `checking` green when nothing is queued — the user scoped this request to the offline case, so those were left alone; they are the obvious next question if the screen is ever reviewed again.

### 2026-10-03 — "Will the app show me the error when a user gets stuck?" — COMPLETE (4 phases)

- **Request (the user's).** "Will the app show the user error code when the user gets stuck... and report me back?" The previous session left a log that persisted failures but could not answer that end to end. The user approved all four phases: (1) a plain-text report a user can hand over, (2) recording the failures the app *catches*, (3) the firm domain stops borrowing `AuthFailure`, (4) the encrypted diagnostic bundle stays passphrase-gated.
- **Phase 1 — a report a user can hand over.** `lib/features/settings/data/diagnostics_report.dart`: `buildDiagnosticsReport` writes a header (app version, generation time, "Errors Recorded: N (Showing M)") and one `time | area | label` line per failure, newest first, or "No Errors Have Been Recorded." when the log is empty. Diagnostics gained a **Copy Diagnostics** action (the real clipboard, not a stub) and the Support screen's WhatsApp and e-mail message carries the same text, rebuilt on every preview so a failure recorded while the screen is open is included. Timestamps are Latin digits, not Bengali numerals, because the report is read by whoever receives it.
- **Phase 2 — recording what the user actually hits.** Almost nothing escapes in this app: a wrong password, a rate limit, a refused payment and a list that will not load are all caught, shown and dropped, so the log was empty for exactly the faults users report. `recordFailure(ErrorLog, area, error)` records at the choke point: `AuthViewModel` (7 operations, plus the swallowed abandoned-onboarding cleanup as `auth.abandonOnboarding`; a dismissed Google sheet is not a fault), every `BaseListViewModel` subclass through a new `diagnosticArea` (`caseReference`, `notes`, `courtCategories`, `courts`, `steps`, `caseTypes`, `onBehalfs`, `deleteRequests`), and `settings.load`, `cases.refresh`, `payments.submit`.
- **Phase 3 — the firm domain's own vocabulary.** `FirmFailureKind`, `FirmFailure` (a `DiagnosableFailure`, so it logs as `FirmFailure(server)`) and `mapFirmFailure` replaced `AuthFailure` across every firm throw site, and `firmErrorText`/`failureText` in `auth_widgets.dart` are now the one place a screen asks for a sentence. A firm RPC fails for reasons a sign-in never can, and the old wording said so out loud: "Sign-In Is Not Available In This Build" about firm management, or "No Connection" about a 500 (the SDK raises `AuthRetryableFetchException` for both, and only the status code tells them apart). Three kinds got firm sentences of their own (`firm_errorServer`, `firm_errorUnauthorized`, `firm_errorUnavailable`); no connection, too many attempts, the premium gate and owner-offline stay shared with auth.
- **A real bug the new test found, not the review.** The roster read was recorded through a `ref.listen` in the screen's `build`, which re-registers on every rebuild: one failing read was written **eleven times**, and the same thing would have repeated for as long as an owner left the screen open. Moving the recording into the providers (`_recordedFirmRead`) fixed the per-frame flood, and the test then exposed the second half: **Riverpod 3 retries a failed provider on a backoff**, so the read failed again and again. An unchanged consecutive failure now collapses into one line, because the report is about what went wrong, not about how many times the framework knocked; the same fault after a *different* one is a new line. Verified to have teeth: with the guard removed the same test reports 23 duplicate lines.
- **Files.** New: `features/settings/data/diagnostics_report.dart`, `test/features/diagnostics_report_test.dart`, `test/features/failure_recording_test.dart`, `test/features/firm_failure_taxonomy_test.dart`. Edited: `core/errors/error_log.dart` (`recordFailure`), `core/presentation/base_list_viewmodel.dart` (`diagnosticArea` + `_guard`) and its five subclasses, the auth view model / widgets / login / sign-up, `accounts_and_profile/data/firm_repository.dart` (the taxonomy, the two roster providers, the shared helper), `manage_associates_screen.dart`, settings/case/payment view models, `diagnostics_screen.dart`, `support_screen.dart`, both ARBs, and the test harness (`errorLogProvider` override, `withErrorLog` in `test_utils.dart`).
- **Tests.** 11 new: 7 for the report, 3 for recorded failures, 10 for the taxonomy, and the two Manage Associates cases that assert a refused write lands as `firm.action FirmFailure(firmAdminOffline)` and a refused roster read lands once as `firm.members FirmFailure(server)`. `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1329 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, date-dependent and untouched here.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** The plan's error-log bullet gained phases 1-3 and keeps the rest owed; Task 17's follow-up note gained the auth recording and the firm taxonomy; Task 19's bundle note gained Copy Diagnostics and the Support message; Task 20 gained the firm failure vocabulary.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed on this thread. The taxonomy's remaining half — the profile, admin and delete-request repositories, which still throw `AuthFailure` for non-auth failures and still leak a raw `PostgrestException` — is a separate change, because it rewrites thrown types across flows whose screens and tests must be re-verified together. Still unverified on a device: the `FileErrorLogStore` write, which has only ever run against the in-memory seam.

### 2026-10-03 — The app keeps its own error log — COMPLETE (phases 1, 2 and 4 of 4)

- **Request (the user's).** "Do we have error handling in the app, especially for Supabase sections (task 16-21)? Without error handling it will be a nightmare to troubleshoot." The user asked for an error handler at every failure point and likely failure point.
- **The survey, with numbers.** 167 catch sites across `lib/`, and only **2** empty-bodied catches — so per-point handling already existed. Typed failures existed for four domains (`AuthFailure`, `SyncFailure`, `PaymentSubmitFailure`, plus the Excel/backup refusals), retry and backoff were real in `push_worker.dart` with `last_error`/`last_attempt_at` per queue row, and a Diagnostics screen with an encrypted export bundle was built. What was missing was everything that makes those usable: **zero** global handlers (`FlutterError.onError`, `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError`, `runZonedGuarded`, `ErrorWidget.builder` — no matches anywhere in `lib/`), and **zero** persisted logging, so nothing could be read back after the fact.
- **What was built.** (1) `lib/core/errors/error_capture.dart` — one capture at startup, recording and then **delegating to the previous handler**, because a capture that swallowed would hide the faults it exists to find. (2) `lib/core/errors/error_log.dart` + `error_log_store.dart` — `ErrorLog`, the last 200 failures in memory and in a file in the app's **private support directory** (never shared storage), each entry a time, an area and a redacted label; `DiagnosableFailure` lets `AuthFailure`/`SyncFailure`/`PaymentSubmitException` name their kind, and no raw server text is ever written. It is a `ChangeNotifier`. (3) A **Diagnostics** Settings sub-page (`/settings/about/diagnostics`, reached from Support) that reads it and clears it. (4) `buildDiagnosticBundle` gained an `errors` section, bundle format version **2**, filled from the log by the Sync Status export.
- **A real bug found by the new test, not by review.** The viewer was first written as a `ConsumerWidget`; `clear()` mutated the log without notifying, so tapping Clear left the entries on screen — exactly the class of defect the previous two fixes were about. The widget test failed, the log became a `ChangeNotifier`, and the screen now wraps its body in a `ListenableBuilder`.
- **Files.** New: `core/errors/error_log.dart`, `error_log_store.dart`, `error_capture.dart`, `error_log_provider.dart`, `features/settings/presentation/settings_about/diagnostics_screen.dart`. Edited: `main.dart` (build, load, install, override, and record a failed Supabase init), `diagnostic_bundle.dart` (the section + version 2), `sync_status_screen.dart` (fill it), `app_router.dart` (the route), `support_screen.dart` (the row), the three typed failures (`DiagnosableFailure`), and the two ARBs.
- **Tests.** New `test/core/error_log_test.dart` (7: the cap, newest-first order, redaction of a server text, persistence across a restart, clear, and unreadable input) and `test/features/diagnostics_screen_test.dart` (3: the empty state, a listed failure, and a clear that empties it). Two cases added to `test/core/diagnostic_bundle_test.dart` for the `errors` section. `flutter analyze` clean; full suite **1304 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, date-dependent and untouched here.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A new plan bullet, a note in Task 17 (startup capture and `auth.initialize`), and a note in Task 19 (the bundle's new section).
- **Resume point:** the third piece is **still owed**: one shared failure taxonomy and one failure-to-copy mapper, so the firm, profile, admin and delete-request repositories stop borrowing `AuthFailure` for non-auth failures and stop leaking raw `PostgrestException`. Deliberately left out of this change because it rewrites thrown types across flows whose screens and tests must be re-verified together. The user's agreed shape was "choke points first, then re-assess".

### 2026-10-03 — An unreadable stored session no longer signs out silently — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** After I flagged it as a gap, the user asked me to fill it: a stored session that cannot be recovered should prompt a sign-in instead of signing out with no explanation.
- **What the second reason is (verified in the SDK source, not assumed).** gotrue raises `SignOutReason.sessionMissing` in exactly two places — `setInitialSession` and `recoverSession` — and only when a stored session string exists but `Session.fromJson` returns null, i.e. the persisted blob is unparseable (gotrue 2.27.2 `gotrue_client.dart:1235-1246` and `1259-1270`). That is corruption, not rejection; `sessionExpired` (`:1303`) is the refresh token being refused by the server after the session parsed fine.
- **Why it could not misfire.** Both call sites are gated by `hasPersistedSession` and a non-null stored token (supabase_flutter 2.17.2 `supabase_auth.dart:107-121` and `145-150`), so a fresh install never emits it. It is never a connectivity condition, so it cannot turn an offline launch into a prompt — and `sessionStatusProvider` already downgrades to `staleOffline` while offline anyway. It cannot touch local data: the only consumer of the flag is the non-blocking `SessionExpiryNotice` strip.
- **What it looked like before.** Identity null, `sessionExpired` false, a cached user id present — which is *exactly* `SessionStatus.staleOffline`, the state whose doc says no notice is shown because there is nothing to do. The user sat in a fully usable diary whose sync was permanently dead, with no indication and no reason to ever sign in again; the only recovery was a manual Sign Out they had no cause to perform.
- **The fix.** A named predicate, `authSessionNeedsSignIn(SignOutReason?)`, in `supabase_auth_repository.dart`, true for `sessionExpired` **and** `sessionMissing`, false for `userInitiated` and null; the `onAuthStateChange` mapper now reads it instead of comparing the reason inline, and the `AuthSessionEvent.sessionExpired` field doc names both causes. No new UI, no new ARB strings, no schema, no data path: the existing strip and its copy are reused, and `reLoginRequired` was already the correct status (sign in again, diary untouched).
- **Files.** `supabase_auth_repository.dart` (the predicate + the mapper), `auth_repository.dart` (the field doc), `auth_bootstrap_test.dart` (the tests).
- **Tests.** A new *Session-end classification* group pins both true cases and both false ones (a deliberate sign-out, and no reason at all). It was verified to **fail with the gap reopened** — temporarily restoring the `sessionExpired`-only body made the `sessionMissing` assertion fail on `Expected: true / Actual: <false>` — and pass after. The state-machine consequences are already covered by `offline_session_test.dart`, which drives the same `sessionExpired: true` flag that `reLoginRequired` and the strip read. `flutter analyze` clean; the two affected suites **31/31**; full suite **1292 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, date-dependent and untouched here.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** The plan's *Offline Session Expiry (never gates local data)* bullet gained the third way a session ends, and Task 17 Phase 4 item 3 gained the same, since `sessionMissing` is a second cause of "online, session invalid".
- **Resume point:** nothing owed by me. **Owed by the user, in the Supabase dashboard:** the Reset Password template must keep `{{ .Token }}` alongside `{{ .ConfirmationURL }}`.

### 2026-10-03 — A typed recovery code is not an opened link — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** After filling the recovery code and the new password inside the logged-in app, the app showed the **Reset Password** screen instead of returning to **Settings › Manage Account**. The earlier "show the login page" ask was withdrawn in favour of "route to Manage Account, and leave the Login/Sign-up reset unchanged".
- **The cause: the SDK reports a typed code as a link.** gotrue's `verifyOTP` notifies `AuthChangeEvent.passwordRecovery` for **any** `OtpType.recovery` — it branches on the type, never on whether the token was typed or arrived in a link (gotrue 2.27.2, `gotrue_client.dart:718-722`). `AuthSessionEvent.passwordRecovery` therefore proves only that a recovery session exists. The ViewModel read it as "a link was opened", started `_beginPasswordRecovery` **unawaited**, and set the flag after `updatePassword` had already cleared it — leaving it set.
- **Why that parked the user on the Reset Password screen.** `passwordRecoveryPendingProvider` drives a router redirect that returns `AppRoutes.forgotPassword` for any location that is not an auth route. `/settings/change-password` is not one, so the sub-page was replaced mid-submit: the widget unmounted, its `if (!mounted) return;` skipped `maybePop()`, and Manage Account was never reached. The same stuck flag is why a reset begun at Login never showed Login either.
- **The fix: only a link may raise the flag.** `AuthViewModel` latches its own `verifyRecoveryCode` call (`_typedRecoveryInFlight`, released when the call is refused so a link opened later is never swallowed) and spends the next `passwordRecovery` event on it; `_beginPasswordRecovery` gained `raiseFlag`, true only for a link. The repository comment claiming "a verified recovery link arrives as its own event" was that bug written in prose, and was corrected.
- **The ending, per the user's choice.** A Change Password from Settings pops back to **Manage Account**. A reset begun at Login ends with the user **signed in** and the app opening on the Daily Cause List (the route sign-up uses) — the router cannot show Login while the session the reset created exists, and `go(AppRoutes.login)` was being swallowed by the same stuck flag. `auth_passwordUpdated` is now raised through a `ScaffoldMessenger` captured before the first await, so the confirmation survives the screen the router takes away.
- **Files.** `auth_viewmodel.dart` (the latch and `raiseFlag`), `supabase_auth_repository.dart` (the corrected comment), `forgot_password_screen.dart` (the messenger and the signed-in destination), `auth_test_support.dart` and `auth_recovery_test.dart` (tests).
- **Tests.** The fake repository now emits the recovery event from `verifyRecoveryCode` exactly as the SDK does — its silence is why twelve green tests hid a broken flow. The new case *"a typed code never claims a recovery link arrived"* asserts the pending-recovery flag stays false; it was verified to **fail against the pre-fix ViewModel** (by temporarily reverting that hunk) and pass after. The Login-path test now asserts the app opens. `flutter analyze` clean; the three auth suites **41/41**; full suite **1290 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, which is date-dependent and untouched here.
- **Records (rule 14 — the plan described the opposite).** A new *A Typed Recovery Code Is Not An Opened Link* bullet beside the other auth follow-ups, a correction to the *Change Password* bullet under Settings Page Structure, and an extension of Task 17's matching note.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed by me. **Owed by the user, in the Supabase dashboard:** the Reset Password template must keep `{{ .Token }}` alongside `{{ .ConfirmationURL }}` (the user chose "code + link" and confirmed a code now arrives).

### 2026-10-03 — "No connection" on Send Code was a server error, not a network error — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** Settings > Accounts & Profile > Manage Account > Change Password > **Send Code** answers *"No connection. Check your internet and try again."* on a working connection, with the correct e-mail.
- **What the server actually said** (project `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`, `auth_logs`, read-only): three `POST /auth/v1/recover` calls today at 05:13:18, 05:13:30 and 05:14:13, all **status 500**, `error_code: unexpected_failure`, and `error: 535 "5.7.8 Username and Password not accepted ... BadCredentials"` from **gsmtp**. Each carried `auth_event.action: user_recovery_requested` for actor `asad.msnd@gmail.com` — so the address was right, the request reached GoTrue, and GoTrue did try to send. The user was right about both counts.
- **So the cause is server configuration, not code: the project's custom SMTP credentials are rejected by Gmail.** No recovery mail can leave the project until that is fixed, and it affects **every** mail the project sends — sign-up confirmations included, not only this screen.
- **Why the app blamed the network (the code half, fixed in 0.0.372).** The `gotrue` Dart client raises `AuthRetryableFetchException` for *any* HTTP ≥ 500 as well as for a dead socket, and `mapAuthFailure` mapped both to `AuthFailureKind.network` → *"No Connection. Check Your Internet And Try Again."* The status code is the only thing that separates them (a transport failure carries none, `fetch.dart` passes `statusCode` only on the ≥ 500 path), so `network` now means **only** "the request never reached the server" and a 5xx classifies as `unknown`. The user's connection was never the problem and the app should never have said it was.
- **What the user must do, outside the app.** Supabase dashboard → the project's **Authentication → E-mail / SMTP** settings: Gmail rejects the account's normal password with exactly this 535, so the credentials must be a 16-character **App Password** (2-Step Verification on) against `smtp.gmail.com:587` with STARTTLS. Use the dashboard's **Send test email** to prove it before testing the app again. Then a second landmine: the **Reset Password e-mail template must contain `{{ .Token }}`** (already documented in `docs/google_oauth_setup.md` §10.1) — without it the mail goes out but the code field can never be filled.
- **Files.** `supabase_auth_repository.dart` (the split), `auth_repository.dart` (the tightened `network` doc), `auth_bootstrap_test.dart` (the regression).
- **Tests.** New case in the *Auth failure classification* group: a 5xx `AuthRetryableFetchException` must classify as `unknown`, while the existing `_offline()` case (no status code) still classifies as `network`. `flutter analyze` clean; the three auth suites **45/45**; full suite **1289 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, which fails on HEAD too.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A new *A Server Error Is Not A Network Error* bullet beside the plan's Google-failure/auth-e-mail notes, and a sentence in Task 17's matching follow-up note.
- **Follow-up, same day (later).** The user corrected the App Password: the 05:40:01 `POST /recover` returned **200** and the mail left the project. It then exposed the next two dashboard gaps, both already described in `docs/google_oauth_setup.md` §10.1 and its checklist: the Reset Password template has no `{{ .Token }}` (so the mail carries a link, not the code the app asks for), and `com.legalcasediary.app://login-callback` is missing from **Redirect URLs** while the **Site URL** is still `http://localhost:3000` (so the link GoTrue issued — the 303 + `login` at 05:40:28 proves it was valid — landed on a dead page). A second tap gave "One-time token not found"; the token is single-use, but every **Send Code** mints a fresh one.
- **The user chose to keep the emailed-code flow in both places** rather than a direct signed-in password change, so this screen is unchanged and no code work remains here.
- **Resume point:** nothing owed by me. **Owed by the user, in the Supabase dashboard:** add `com.legalcasediary.app://login-callback` to **Redirect URLs**, set a real **Site URL**, and add `{{ .Token }}` to the Reset Password template — then press **Send Code** and confirm the mail carries a code as well as a link that opens the app.

### 2026-10-03 — Change Password is a Settings sub-page, not Login's route — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** Settings > Accounts & Profile > Manage Account > **Change Password** took the user to the default landing page (Daily Cause List) instead of the screen. It should show **the same screen the Sign in page shows**, but with a **header and back button like every other Settings sub-menu screen**, and **without the "Back to Login" button**. The Login screen's presentation must not change; only the Manage Account one.
- **The cause: it pushed an auth route.** The row did `context.push(AppRoutes.forgotPassword)` — and `/forgot-password` is one of the three auth routes (`_isAuthRoute`). The router's redirect has a deliberate rule: `if (signedIn && atAuthRoute) return defaultLandingRoute`. From Manage Account the user *is* signed in, so the push was immediately redirected to the landing page. Nothing was broken in the reset flow itself.
- **The fix: a second presentation of one screen, on its own route.** `ForgotPasswordScreen` gained `asSettingsSubPage` (default false, so Login is untouched) which decides exactly three things: the shell, the "Back To Login" line and the destination after success. New route `AppRoutes.settingsChangePassword` (`/settings/change-password`, a **shell** route, so it stays in the shell like its siblings) builds it with `asSettingsSubPage: true`; the Manage Account row now pushes that. The card body itself is byte-for-byte the same widget — it is wrapped in `SettingsSubPageModal` (the standard header + back button) instead of `AuthScaffold`, so the two presentations cannot drift.
- **Two consequences of the second presentation, both deliberate.** (1) The "Back To Login" line is hidden — that user is signed in, and the sign-in route is somewhere they may not go; the comment in the screen says so. (2) **A successful change returns to Manage Account** (a pop) rather than `context.go(AppRoutes.login)`, which from here would have been redirected to the landing page for the same reason the row was. The Login presentation is unchanged: it still goes to Login with *"Your password has been updated."*
- **No mockup exists for this screen**, so rule 9 does not bind it: its body is the reset screen's, and its header is the shared `SettingsSubPageModal` every other sub-page uses.
- **Files.** `forgot_password_screen.dart` (the flag, the two branches, the return path), `app_router.dart` (the route constant + a shell route), `manage_account_screen.dart` (the row and its doc), plus tests.
- **Tests.** `manage_account_test`'s Change Password test now registers the **real** screen on the new route and pins what makes it a sub-page (the reset card is there, a back button is there, "Back To Login" is absent) rather than asserting on a stand-in marker. `auth_recovery_test` gained *"in Settings it is a sub-page and returns to Manage Account"*, which runs the whole flow — request code, enter code and new password — and asserts the user lands back on the screen they came from. `flutter analyze` clean; the two suites **21/21**; full suite **1288 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, which fails on HEAD too.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** The plan's *Settings Page Structure* now lists Change Password under Manage Account, and Task 12's *Phase 2 shells as built* gained the bullet.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open.

### 2026-10-03 — Premium state is settled before the gates read it — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** After logging in, Supabase data took over two minutes to arrive; for that whole window Settings > Accounts & Profile > Manage Account > Subscription Details showed *Subscription: Free* beside *Plan: 1 Month*, and Online Sync showed *Last Sync: Never* plus *Premium is required to sync across devices* — on an account with a premium 1-Month plan. The user also asked how long a post-login load should ideally take.
- **Diagnosis: one root cause, three symptoms.** Entitlement is derived from the **cached** `profiles` row (`resolvePremiumAccess`), and that row was not being pulled after a sign-in. `AuthViewModel._completeSignIn` never asked for a refresh or a sync; `AccountRefreshScope` / `SyncTriggerScope` sit at the root in `app.dart` and mount **once per process**, so a login on a running app re-ran neither the launch nor the resume hook. With no row cached, (a) the Subscription row read `premiumAccessProvider` = **false** and printed Free, (b) the Plan row read a *different* source — a live query against `payment_submissions` — and so named the month the user had paid for, and (c) `SyncService._isEntitled()` also read false, and `syncNow` returns **silently** when it is not entitled: no backoff, no retry, no `lastSuccessfulSyncAt` stamp. Hence *Last Sync: Never*. A cold start was just as exposed in a different way: the launch `requestSync(pull: true)` races the profile pull, loses the entitlement race, and is dropped; when entitlement finally flipped true, `SyncTriggerScope`'s listener opened the Realtime channel but never asked for the pull, so only a resume/reconnect/local write repaired it — the ~2-minute mark.
- **Ideal latency, for the record.** Offline-first: a device that has synced before shows its data at frame 1 and settles a delta pull in ~1–3 s. A first-ever pull is one `GET /profiles` plus the engine's delta pull — seconds, not minutes. Two minutes was never server latency; it was a missing trigger.
- **(1) The refresh gains a fourth moment.** `AccountRefreshScope` now listens to `authIdentityProvider` and refreshes as soon as a **different account id** appears, which covers sign-in, sign-up, Google, a password-recovery link and an account switch. Two dedupes keep a cold start to one pull: a re-entrancy guard, and `_refreshedUserId`, the account the last completed refresh was for (the listener can fire either side of the launch refresh finishing, so both are needed). It is cleared on sign-out so signing back in as the same account — whose caches the sign-out emptied — pulls again.
- **(2) The dropped pull is re-issued.** `SyncTriggerScope` now calls `requestSync(pull: true)` on a **false → true entitlement transition** — the moment the engine learns it may sync. This also covers a renewal landing mid-session.
- **(3) Unknown is not Free.** New `entitlementKnownProvider` (`account_mode.dart`) is the other half of the same question: `premiumAccessProvider` answers false both for a genuinely free account and for one whose row has not landed, and printing Free for the second is a lie. The Subscription row now shows `—` while unknown, and Online Sync gained a `_SyncBlocker.checking` state (*Checking Your Subscription…*) instead of claiming premium is required. Online Sync's entity rows are unchanged: "Not synced" is true in every blocker state.
- **Files.** `account_refresh_scope.dart`, `sync_trigger_scope.dart`, `account_mode.dart`, `manage_account_screen.dart`, `online_sync_screen.dart`, `app_en.arb` / `app_bn.arb` + regenerated `app_localizations*` (3 new keys).
- **Tests.** `account_mode_test` gained "a session that arrives after launch is pulled at once" (it emits a real `AuthSessionEvent` on a running scope and asserts one pull plus the cached row); `online_sync_screen_test` gained "an account whose plan is not known yet is not called free"; `manage_account_test` gained "an account whose row has not landed says nothing, not Free" and its existing Free test now seeds a pulled free row so Free is a fact rather than a gap. `flutter analyze` clean; the three suites **45/45**; full suite **1287 tests, 1 failure** — the pre-existing `phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, which fails on HEAD too.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A new *Entitlement Is Settled Before The Gates Read It* bullet in the plan's §6 Subscription Enforcement, and Task 17 Phase 3 item 4, which owned the launch/resume/reconnect enumeration.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. Not yet acted on, still open from earlier: the live `profiles` row `21b77e36` holds a legacy `mobile_no` (`1835252721`, no leading `0`) that now displays inconsistently against the `+88 01XXXXXXXXX` form.

### 2026-10-03 — Logout now lands on the Login screen — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** After logging out from Settings > Account & Profile > Manage Account, the user should be shown the login screen.
- **What happened before.** `ManageAccountScreen._signOut` called `runBranchedLogout` and, when it completed, `Navigator.of(context).maybePop()` — which only closed Manage Account back onto the Settings list inside the shell. The router could not have helped: `appRouterProvider` reads `authGateRequiredProvider` **once per launch** (its own comment says so), so a device that launched signed in is never re-gated mid-session and its `redirect` leaves a signed-out user wherever they stand once `requiresSignIn` is false.
- **The fix.** On a completed logout the screen now runs `context.go(AppRoutes.login)` — the same explicit navigation `SessionExpiryNotice` already uses. `go`, not `push`/`pop`: Login is a top-level route, so the shell (and the wiped diary behind it) is left behind rather than uncovered. A cancelled branch (`runBranchedLogout` false — a declined offline block, a closed warning, a dismissed passphrase) still returns early and navigates nowhere, which is the cross-contamination guard Task 19's audit added.
- **Files.** `manage_account_screen.dart` (the navigation and its doc) and `manage_account_test.dart` (a Login stand-in route plus two assertions).
- **Tests.** The export branch test now also asserts the Login screen is shown; the "synced account logs out at once" test pins that every branch lands there, not only the export one. `flutter analyze` clean; `manage_account_test` **8/8**; full suite **1284 passed**, the one failure pre-existing (`phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, fails on HEAD too).
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A bullet in the plan's §5 logout flow and item 6 in `docs/tasks/19_realtime_conflicts_and_recovery.md`'s Phase 6.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open.

### 2026-10-03 — Profile: the display name now reaches `profiles.full_name` too — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** Changing Full Name on the Profile screen did not update `Supabase > profiles > full_name`; the column kept the previous name.
- **The cause: `full_name` was not in the PATCH body.** `profileDetailsUpdate()` sent only `bar_association`, `membership_no`, `office_address` and `mobile_no`, so every save wrote those four and left the name on the device. `profiles.full_name` has been client-writable on the holder's own row since Phase 3 (`grant update (full_name)` + `profiles_update_self`), and the plan lists **all five** Profile fields as the account holder's own record — so the push simply never named the fifth column.
- **The fix.** `profileDetailsUpdate` now sends `full_name` as well, so the Profile screen's Save (and the refresh-time push) mirrors the name exactly like the other four. `email` still stays out — it belongs to `auth.users` and a client that could write it could misrepresent who it is.
- **One retry gap closed with it.** `accountRefreshProvider` only retried an owed push when the record had one of the four *details*. Since the name now travels in the same PATCH, the gate is `merged.hasDetails || merged.fullName.isNotEmpty`, so a name-only record still has something to send and a name-only offline save is still retried on launch/resume/reconnect. `ProfileFields.hasDetails` itself is unchanged (it is about the four columns, and tests pin that).
- **Server checked first.** The live project already holds the privileges this needs: `authenticated` has column `UPDATE` on `full_name`, `profiles_update_self` limits it to `id = auth.uid()`, and the only trigger on `profiles` fires on `subscription_expiry` — nothing blocks a name write. No migration was needed.
- **Files.** `profile_repository.dart` (payload + docs), `account_mode.dart` (retry gate), `profile_name_store.dart` (doc), and `profile_details_test.dart` (payload now pinned as five columns; new test for a name-only record being handed to the account).
- **Tests.** `flutter analyze` clean; `profile_details_test` + `account_mode_test` + `profile_name_test` **45/45**; full suite **1284 passed**, the one failure pre-existing (`phase4_widgets_test.dart` Bengali calendar digits, fails on HEAD too).
- **Records (rule 14).** No plan or task-file change: the plan already states the account holder's own five fields are client-writable on their own row, so this change brings the code back to what the plan already described rather than departing from it.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open.

### 2026-10-03 — Profile: `+88` mobile prefix, and a save toast that tells the truth — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, Profile screen only).** (1) The Edit Profile Mobile No. box's fixed prefix is `+880`; make it `+88` so the user types the number with its leading `0`. (2) Saving the profile does not reach Supabase, and the app always says *Profile saved (offline)*.
- **(1) `+880` -> `+88`, hint `1XXXXXXXXX` -> `01XXXXXXXXX`.** The user now types the local number (`01XXXXXXXXX`) and the box supplies the `+88` country code, matching the checkout's phone field; the stored value is that local form as typed, and the read view wears the same `+88`. `mockup_html_screens/settings_profile/code.html`'s read view moved to `+88 01812332721` (its PNG was not regenerated — the same headless-Chrome clamp as the header mockups).
- **(2) The save *does* reach Supabase; the toast was the lie.** The push path was checked end to end against the live project: `20260929130000_profiles_personal_details` is applied, `authenticated` holds column UPDATE on the four columns, `profiles_update_self` restricts it to `id = auth.uid()`, and the API logs show `PATCH /rest/v1/profiles?id=eq....` returning **204** with the values landing on the row. What was wrong is that the screen showed `settingsProfile_saved` ("Profile saved (offline)") **unconditionally**, before the push ran — so a landed save still looked offline. `_openEditSheet` now awaits `pushProfileDetailsProvider` first and picks the new `settingsProfile_savedOnline` ("Profile saved") only when the PATCH was accepted; the offline wording is kept for the real offline cases (nobody signed in, no backend, no connection), which stay owed and are retried on launch/resume/reconnect.
- **Files.** `profile_screen.dart` (prefix, hint, read view, save order), `app_en.arb` / `app_bn.arb` + regenerated `app_localizations*` (one new key), `profile_name_store.dart` doc comments, the mockup's `code.html`, and the two profile tests.
- **Tests.** New assertions pin both toast wordings — a landed push shows *Profile saved*, a refused one shows *Profile saved (offline)*. `flutter analyze` clean; `profile_name_test` + `profile_details_test` **25/25**.
- **Records (rule 14).** The plan's `profiles` table row and its *Lawyer's Own Details* bullet were corrected (`+88` prefix, the honest toast), and the Task 12 Phase 2 section gained the same bullet.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. The `settings_profile` mockup PNG is still stale (HTML moved, PNG not), like the eight header mockups.

### 2026-10-03 — The header's sync button: two clouds, and the idle arrows gone — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, header only).** The app header should not show the `Icons.sync` arrows; offline or paused should show the network-off cloud instead, **even in offline mode**. The scope was narrowed twice: first "only two icons for sync" app-wide, then corrected to "only remove `Icons.sync` (idle) from the app header", and confirmed that **the error state's icon stays**.
- **Only `lib/shared/widgets/sync_button.dart` changed.** `_showOfflineIcon` (`!isOnline && pending == 0`) became `_showsOffCloud` (`!isOnline || paused`), so offline now wears the off cloud **regardless of the queue** and the red count sits on top of it — the count is a fact about the queue, the cloud is a claim about the connection, and the old code conflated them by hiding the off cloud whenever anything was queued. `Icons.sync` is gone: idle and a completed sync both wear `cloud_done_outlined`, so the header's ordinary state is the success cloud. A real error keeps `sync_problem_outlined` ("something went wrong" is not "you are offline"), and a running sync keeps its spinner. Offline keeps its muted ink; every other state keeps its own colour.
- **Nothing app-wide was touched, by the narrowed scope.** Settings' Online Sync row (`sync_outlined`), the Online Sync screen's Sync Now button and status pill, the two Sync Status badges (`sync_problem`), the conflict banner and the Guidelines entry (`cloud_sync_outlined`) are all unchanged — the earlier app-wide plan was abandoned before any of it was edited.
- **Tests.** Three assertions in `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` moved to the new glyphs: the ordinary-ink test now reads `cloud_done_outlined`, the offline-with-nothing-queued test asserts the off cloud and no success cloud, and the offline-with-work-queued test now expects **both** the red count and the off cloud under it (previously it asserted the off cloud was absent). `flutter analyze` clean; that suite passes 35/35, and the Admin Panel and Online Sync suites pass unchanged.
- **Mockups: the HTML moved, the PNGs did not.** The eight header-bearing mockups now draw `cloud_done` in the header. Their `screen.png` files were **not** regenerated: the only renderer available is headless Chrome, and on this machine it clamps the layout viewport to ~500 px while cropping the capture to the requested 390, so every regenerated PNG lost the right-hand side of the header (verified by decoding the PNGs and probing the clamp with a media-query page). The seven committed PNGs and the Admin Panel's were restored to their committed bytes, so they still show the old arrows; regenerating them needs either the team's mockup tool or a render of a temporary 390 px-wide copy.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A *The Header's Sync Button Shows Two Clouds* bullet in the plan's Resolved Design Clarifications and a matching sentence in `docs/tasks/23_admin_panel.md`'s mockup section.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. The eight mockup `screen.png` files are the one known gap, and the reason is recorded above.

### 2026-10-02 — App header: honest ink, and an honest offline icon — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, header only).** (1) The Sync Status icon is green after a successful sync; make it black. (2) Offline with sync off, the app draws two circling arrows; show the offline icon instead, without a count.
- **(1) green → `AppColors.primary`.** Green said "something good happened" on every single launch, which is the fastest way to train an eye to stop reading an icon at all. A healthy sync is now the **ordinary** case and wears the ordinary ink; `paused` and `error` still read differently, and the Online Sync screen's own green "Synced" markers are untouched — this is about the header announcing a success, not about green meaning "done".
- **(2) One condition, asked and answered before I built it.** The offline icon takes over **only when nothing is queued**. Once a change *is* queued the red count returns, because that is how an offline user learns an edit is waiting — the count is the only place they can see it. `_SyncButton` therefore grew an `isOnline` flag (defaulting true, so any caller that does not care keeps the ordinary icon) and the header passes `isOnlineProvider`. The badge is not newly "removed when offline": it never appeared when there was nothing to count, and the offline icon now fills that otherwise-empty state instead of the sync arrows.
- **One test needed a real fix, not a tweak.** My first version of the success-colour test asserted through the button and failed grey — because this harness has no premium account, so the button renders its free-plan variant. The harness now pins `premiumAccessProvider` true, and the test reads the actual icon colour. Left as-is it would have been asserting the free-plan state while claiming to test success.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1280 passed** (3 new, one per behaviour plus the count-vs-offline precedence); `arb_keys_test.dart` green. Version `0.0.359`.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. Two screens now watch `isOnlineProvider` for different reasons — the header for the icon, Online Sync for the row markers — which is fine, but a third caller should follow the same `isOnline` flag rather than re-deriving connectivity.

### 2026-10-02 — Online Sync: queued work outranks the plan, and the header counts it — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** (1) Two pending syncs show correctly in Pending Sync but the Online Sync → Cases row said "Not synced". (2) Should the app header's sync/cloud button show a small red count of pending sync, so a user can see there is an issue?
- **(1) was a third real bug of mine, and the cause was the order of my own checks.** The row marker tested the **blocker first** (signed out / free plan / offline) and only then whether work was queued. So the moment anything was blocked, a queued change was reported as "Not synced" — telling the user nothing was waiting when two things were. Queued work is a **fact** independent of the plan or the connection, so `_markerIcon` / `_markerColor` / `_markerLabel` now check **waiting first**. The blockers only decide wording once there is genuinely nothing waiting, which is the only state "Not synced" actually describes. Pinned by `queued work outranks the blocker on every row` (a queued case edit on a free plan) and `a queued row is amber, never red`.
- **(2) is a good idea and is now done — but it needed one judgement.** The header **already** carries a red `sync_problem` badge, and it deliberately fires only for *blocked or conflicted* work: entries nothing will move on its own. Ordinary pending work is normal, so that badge stays quiet. Putting the count on the **sync button** is a different job — it answers "why has my edit not appeared on the other device yet?" — so it reads `syncQueuePendingCountProvider` rather than the attention provider. Two controls, two different facts, deliberately.
- **The button still syncs when tapped.** One control must do one thing, so the count is information only; where held work is *acted on* remains the Pending Sync entry on Online Sync. A red badge that navigated somewhere would have made the icon a lie. The count is suppressed while a sync is actually running, because a number beside a spinner reads as "stuck" when it is draining.
- **A rule-11 violation the suite caught, and I had introduced it.** `Badge.count` paints a **Latin** digit, so the Daily Cause List's own "counts use Bengali digits" test failed on my badge. It is now `AppDateUtils.formatDigits(pending, l.localeName)` like every other count in the app, and `the badge count uses the locale's digits` pins it.
- **Two sibling tests needed a judgement call, not a workaround.** `daily_cause_list_test.dart` and `upcoming_cases_test.dart` both seeded two cases — which queues a sync row each — then asserted `findsOneWidget` on "২". Both were **correctly** finding two counts: the court's, and now mine. Their actual intent ("no count is interpolated in Latin digits") is asserted by the second line, which stayed strict; only the over-tight "exactly one" became `findsNWidgets(2)`, with the reason recorded at each site.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1273 passed** (5 new); `arb_keys_test.dart` green. Version `0.0.357`.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. Note for whoever adds more header badges: `Badge.count` is Latin-digit by default, and the case screens seed their data through `insertCase`, so any new count will collide with existing per-screen count assertions.

### 2026-10-02 — Online Sync: the redundant card gone, and two marker bugs fixed — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's, five points).** Clearer subtitle; remove the "Premium Sync Is Active Across Your Devices" card as redundant; a synced case wrongly reads "Not synced"; empty Notes and References read "Not synced" **in green**, which should be red; and rename Sync Status to **Pending Sync**, moved to the very bottom after References.
- **Points 3 and 4 were real bugs I shipped yesterday, not wording.** The row marker used a single `queued > 0 ? … : notSynced` ternary with `AppColors.success` on the empty branch, so an entity with nothing waiting always read "Not synced" — in **green**, next to a Sync Now button that was itself disabled. A green "Not synced" is self-contradictory: it says the thing is broken and the thing is fine in the same breath. The row now derives one marker from **two** facts: whether it has work waiting, and **whether it can reach the account at all** (`_syncBlockerOf(ref)`, the same blocker rule the status card uses, so the two cannot disagree). Three states, one per real condition: **Synced** (green, nothing waiting and sync is possible), **"N Waiting"** (muted, work is queued), **"Not synced"** (red, blocked — with "Sign in to sync" when the blocker is that nobody is signed in, because that wording tells the user what to do and "Not synced" does not). A free account therefore never claims Synced for something that was never sent.
- **The account card is deleted, not hidden.** The status card still carries the four blockers, so "what state is sync in" survives; what is gone is the second copy of the account, which Settings > Manage Account already owns. Its now-dead locals (`identity`, `premium`, `online`) and its `profile_name_store` import went with it, rather than being left as unused reads.
- **Pending Sync sits last, and has its own label.** It keeps opening the Sync Status screen but is titled `settingsOnlineSync_pendingSyncTitle` rather than reusing `syncStatus_title`, which **names that screen's own header** — reusing the key would have renamed the Sync Status screen and the header badge tooltip with it.
- **The subtitle now says what actually happens**: records are kept up to date on this device and sent to the account when you are on Premium. The old "Manage your real-time data sync" promised real-time, which this engine does not do.
- **Two of the three new ARB states are regression tests**, not just new coverage: `a row that cannot sync is red, never green` reads the rendered `Text.style.color` and asserts `AppColors.error`, and `a synced row is green` asserts `AppColors.success`. `Pending Sync is the last thing on the screen` compares `getTopLeft` against References so the ordering cannot silently move back.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1268 passed** (13 in this screen's suite); `arb_keys_test.dart` green. Version `0.0.356`.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. The `settings_onlineSync` mockup is now further out of date than before — it still draws the account card this change removed — and is left as the signed-out reference, as recorded above.

### 2026-10-02 — Online Sync: the real account, plan and engine — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** "I'm currently logged in the app but in the screen it says I'm not", and to wire the screen's options properly.
- **The cause was that the screen was never finished.** `OnlineSyncScreen` was still the **Part A stub**: a `StatelessWidget` account card that rendered `settingsOnlineSync_notSignedIn` unconditionally, a hard-coded "Action Required / Last Sync: Never", and a Sync Now button that only showed a snackbar. Every string came from the mockup's *signed-out* frame. It was never wired to the Part B engine, and **no test referenced it at all** — which is exactly how it survived while Tasks 17–19 built the whole sync engine underneath it.
- **The account card now reads the account.** The same three-source name fallback Manage Account uses (this device's saved name → the session's → the server's cached profile), so a signed-in premium user sees their own name and "Premium Sync Is Active"; the signed-out wording is now reached only when there genuinely is no identity.
- **The plan decides whether sync can run, and the button says so.** `premiumAccessProvider` and `isOnlineProvider` feed a small blocker, so the button is **disabled with a reason** rather than firing a no-op: free plan → "Premium Is Required To Sync Across Devices.", offline → "No Connection…", signed out → "Sign In To Sync…". This matters because `SyncService` enforces all three itself by **silently returning** — a button that appears to work while doing nothing is worse than one that explains itself.
- **Sync Now really syncs.** It calls `syncNow(force: true, pull: true)` and then reads `consecutiveFailures` back off the published health, because `syncNow` returns `void` and signals failure through health and notices rather than a result value.
- **The status card is now the engine's.** Real last-sync via `syncLastSuccessProvider` (this **device's** value, the one the recovery lock also reads), the attention count for blocked/conflicted work, the pending-upload count, and health (syncing/paused). The pill is `Up To Date` when there is nothing to say, and `Action Required` when something cannot resolve itself.
- **What the screen still does not claim.** There is **no per-entity "synced" flag anywhere in this app** — the engine stores a device-level last-sync and per-table pull cursors, neither of which is per-entity sync state. So an entity row shows its count and whether it has work **waiting to send** (derived from real `sync_queue` rows by `entity_type`), and is never marked "Synced". This is stated in the file's header so a later reader does not mistake the omission for an oversight.
- **A test suite now exists** — `test/features/online_sync_screen_test.dart`, 10 tests, which the screen never had. Three assertions failed on first run and each was a real finding, not a test bug: "Not signed in" legitimately renders **twice** (account card + status pill, one answering "who" and one answering "what state"), the entity rows needed a tall viewport because a lazy `ListView` never builds an off-screen row, and the never-synced and just-synced cases needed to be separate tests rather than two pumps of one.
- **A careless detour, corrected.** A reorder script I wrote to sync the two ARB files' key order reserialized the whole Bengali file — 326 insertions of unrelated metadata reformatting. I reverted it and hand-moved the four misplaced keys instead, so the final diff is **68 insertions and nothing else**. `arb_keys_test.dart` passes.
- **The mockup is left as-is, deliberately.** `settings_onlineSync/code.html` draws the **signed-out** state, and the screen still renders that state identically, so the design reference still holds. Nothing to correct until the mockups are refreshed.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1265 passed** (10 new). Version `0.0.355`.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. Note for the next pass on this screen: the mockup only shows the signed-out frame, so the premium/free/offline/held-work frames have no design reference yet — worth capturing the way the Admin Panel's mockup still needs writing.

### 2026-10-02 — Task 23: the Admin Panel — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** An Admin Panel in the app: detect the signed-in user against `app_admins`, show the panel only to admins, list it in Settings → View Settings for admins, and give it a collapsible **Pending** (open) / **Resolved** (closed) Payment Requests section. The user chose a new RPC for the admin check, a server-side list returning the payer's details, the full action set (approve, reject, admin note), and payments only in this first version.
- **One finding changed the instruction about `app_admins`.** The columns are worth adding but are *not* the source of payer information: `profiles` already carries `full_name`, `email`, `bar_association`, `membership_no` and `mobile_no` (Task 17 follow-up, 2 rows populated live). They are on `app_admins` as a **contact record for the admins themselves** — who to call when an approval breaks, and who a revoked row still names. The panel's payer details come from `profiles` via the list RPC.
- **`app_admins` is still unreadable by the app.** The user's first instinct was to compare the user id against the table; it has **no RLS policy at all** (verified live), so a client cannot read it. `is_app_admin()` answers the question instead, and the registry row behind the answer stays closed. This also keeps the new `email` column from becoming a published list of admin addresses.
- **The immutability trigger had to be narrowed, and the server caught it.** `reject_admin_update()` refuses *every* UPDATE, so the first apply failed on the email backfill — rolled back atomically, nothing half-written. Replacing it with `reject_admin_grant_change()` refuses only `user_id`/`granted_at`/`granted_by` and permits the two descriptive columns, which is what "the owner hand-fills them" actually requires. Verified live: changing `granted_by` still raises, `full_name` still writes.
- **No RLS was widened.** `payment_submissions` stays `user_id = auth.uid()` and `profiles` stays self-or-firm; the panel reads through one admin-gated RPC. Verified live, and `anon` cannot execute any of the three new functions. This is why the panel needed no service-role key — none exists in the app and none was added.
- **One nav entry, not two — deliberately.** View Settings drives the bottom bar and the drawer from the same list, so a single `bottomNavTabDef` marked `adminOnly` is what makes the screen appear wherever the admin selected. `adminOnly` is separate from `removable`: a non-admin never *sees* the panel rather than *hiding* it, and a leftover `sideNavOptions` entry from an earlier admin session cannot reveal it.
- **Two problems the existing tests caught, both real.** `screen_transitions_test.dart` enforces no-motion on every bottom-nav screen, and the panel is selectable from that bar — so it is a `NoTransitionPage` like its siblings. And because the panel is a *shell* route, the shell's `SafeArea` already insets it; the `SafeArea` I first wrote (right for the paywall, which sits outside the shell) would have doubled the inset.
- **The task is 23, not 22** — `22_verification_plan.md` already exists. The index row, the migration header, the generator labels and the two migration-count reason strings were all corrected.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A *The Admin Panel Is In The App, And The Registry Stays Closed* bullet in the plan's Resolved Design Clarifications, a pointer from §Pricing's Payment Method line, a matching Addendum bullet in `docs/tasks/21_payments_and_subscription.md`, and the new `docs/tasks/23_admin_panel.md` with its acceptance checklist.

That is the first real end-to-end proof that `admin_list_payment_submissions()` returns the payer fields and that `approve_payment` fires from the app. Everything is uncommitted on `BugFix-Supabse`.

### 2026-10-02 — Premium: the paywall insets its own status bar — COMPLETE

- **Request (the user's).** The Premium screen does not show the phone's status bar the way other screens do.
- **Cause.** The paywall is a **top-level route** (`app_router.dart`, outside `ShellRoute`), so the shell's `SafeArea(top: true, bottom: false)` never reaches it. Without that inset the `Scaffold` paints its app bar from y=0, so the clock and status icons land on the app bar's white instead of on the app's own black status-bar band — the one screen where they disappear.
- **The fix is the shell's own pattern, not a new one.** `SafeArea(top: true, bottom: false)` wrapping the whole `Scaffold`, exactly as `AppShellRoot` does. Verified by measurement, not by eye: with a simulated 90 px status bar the app bar now starts at `top = 90` and keeps its own 56 px height, matching `SettingsSubPageModal` and the shell screens. A first attempt that wrapped only the `body` was wrong — the app bar sits outside the body, so it changed nothing and the probe numbers were identical; the `SafeArea` has to be an ancestor of the `Scaffold`.
- **Pinned by test.** New `leaves the phone status bar visible above its app bar` asserts the app bar's `top` equals the injected status-bar height and its height is below it. `pumpPaywall` gained a `statusBarHeight` parameter defaulting to 0, so the existing assertions are unaffected.
- **Not changed:** `checkout_screen.dart` has the same structure and the same latent problem, but it was out of scope. Flagged below.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1246 passed**. Version `0.0.353`.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. Two loose ends: `checkout_screen.dart` needs the same status-bar inset when the user next asks; `paywall_planWasPrice` is still misnamed now that it supplies both the struck-through and the live price.

- **Request (the user's).** Re-price the Premium screen: ৳100 a month unchanged, ৳285 for 3 months (5% off), ৳540 for 6 months (10% off), ৳960 for a year (20% off), and show the old price struck through.

### 2026-10-01 — Auth E-mail links open the app, and Google sign-in checks the network first — COMPLETE

- **Request (two points, both the user's).** (1) *All this time* the Google sign-in failure had been **internet connectivity** — the phone was on a Wi-Fi network with no internet, and turning on mobile data fixed it — so why could the tooling not tell? (2) When Supabase mails a sign-up confirmation or a password-reset link, tapping it opens `localhost:3000` instead of the app.
- **Why the failure was invisible, stated plainly (no defensiveness).** The old `mapAuthFailure` folded every on-device Google `canceled` into `AuthFailureKind.cancelled`, and both screens deliberately render `cancelled` as **no message at all** — so a dead network and a real dismissal produced byte-identical behaviour. The one surviving signal, GMS `[16] Account reauth failed.`, is raised for *both* a missing certificate and a reauth request that never reached Google, and the plugin's README says it cannot distinguish them; reading it as leaning configuration was **over-confident**. The previous "cause: the signing certificate is not registered" conclusion is therefore **corrected below**: registering the debug/release/Play certificates remains a genuine prerequisite for Google sign-in on release builds, but it was never proven to be this cause.
- **Even today's code could not have told, and that is the real gap.** The project's own `ConnectivityMonitor` answers the **link, not the internet** on purpose — a Wi-Fi network with no uplink reports "connected" — so `connectivity_plus` would have said the phone was online. Only an actual request that has to complete can see it.
- **What changed — the links (the user's choice was the real deep link, not a dashboard-only fix).** `SupabaseConfig.authRedirectUrl` = `com.legalcasediary.app://login-callback`, passed as `emailRedirectTo` by `signUpIndividual` and as `redirectTo` by `sendPasswordRecoveryCode`, because Supabase otherwise falls back to the project **Site URL** — whose dashboard default is `http://localhost:3000`, exactly the reported symptom. `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` claims the scheme with its own `<intent-filter>` **and** sets `flutter_deeplinking_enabled=false`, which the `app_links` docs require from Flutter 3.24 or the engine consumes the URI first. A verified recovery link signs the user in as a side effect, so `AuthSessionEvent` gained `passwordRecovery` (from `AuthChangeEvent.passwordRecovery`), `AuthState` gained the matching flag exposed as `passwordRecoveryPendingProvider`, the router gained one rule holding the user on the reset screen until the new password is set, and that screen asks for the password alone (no address, no code) and hides the way back to Login. **The emailed-code flow is unchanged and stays primary** — the earlier "the code, not a deep link, brings the user back" design note is superseded only by *adding* the link as a second way in.
- **What changed — the network probe.** `probeSupabaseReachable` is a four-second request to the project URL, injectable for tests, run by `signInWithGoogle` **before** the account sheet opens; a failure raises `AuthFailureKind.network`, whose existing copy is *"No Connection. Check Your Internet And Try Again."* The cheap `connectivity_plus` alternative was offered and rejected in the plan discussion for exactly the reason above: it cannot see dead Wi-Fi.
- **Records (rule 14 — both changes are absent from the plan).** A long *Auth E-mail Links Return To The App, And Google Sign-In Checks The Network First* bullet in the plan's Resolved Design Clarifications and a matching Addendum bullet in `docs/tasks/17_auth_profiles_and_firm_onboarding.md`. Runbook `docs/google_oauth_setup.md` gained **§10.1** (the two dashboard values, what each link does, the `adb` deep-link test), two symptom rows, and four checklist items; `README.md` gained an *Auth E-mail Links* section and a "check the network first" bullet.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1244 passed** (1241 + 3: the link-path screen test and two probe tests); `record_version.py --check` OK; `generate_all_supabase_sql.dart --check` OK (no SQL this phase). Version `0.0.348`.
- **Still the user's to do (dashboard-only, unreadable from this machine).** Add `com.legalcasediary.app://login-callback` to **Authentication → URL Configuration → Redirect URLs**; point **Site URL** at a real page; keep `{{ .Token }}` in the Reset Password template; then prove both with a real sign-up mail on the device.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. The definitive proof of Google sign-in is still a successful run leaving an `id_token` grant in the Auth logs — and the next offline attempt should now say *No Connection* rather than nothing.

### 2026-10-01 — Google sign-in: the failure is now reported instead of swallowed — COMPLETE

- **Request.** Google sign-in worked at sign-up but later stopped: after picking the account in the sheet the app simply stayed on the login screen.
- **Diagnosis first (the user's ruling), and it was conclusive.** An instrumented build printed `I/flutter: Google sign-in failed: GoogleSignInException(code GoogleSignInExceptionCode.canceled, [16] Account reauth failed., null)` — Android Credential Manager returns `[16]` → `GetCredentialCancellationException` → plugin `CANCELED` → Dart `canceled`. The old `mapAuthFailure` folded that into `AuthFailureKind.cancelled`, and both screens deliberately render `cancelled` as **no message**, so a real failure was indistinguishable from the user closing the sheet. The plugin's own README states the two cases are indistinguishable by design and lists the causes (missing/incorrect signing SHA, wrong package name, wrong `serverClientId`). The server is innocent: 24h of Auth logs on `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau` contain **no `id_token` grant** at all — the app aborts on-device before any Supabase call — and the single user (`asad.msnd@gmail.com`) signed up with Google successfully, so the Supabase provider config is fine. **Cause: the signing certificate is not registered in Google Cloud.** — *Corrected 2026-10-01: the user's later evidence showed the real cause was a **Wi-Fi network with no internet**, fixed by switching to mobile data (see the entry above). The certificate registration remains a prerequisite for Google sign-in on release builds, but it was never proven to be this cause, and `[16]` is consistent with both.*
- **App-side fix (chosen from the offered options).** New `AuthFailureKind.googleSignInFailed` with EN/BN copy (“Google Sign-In Could Not Complete. Please Try Again.” / Bengali in `app_en.arb`/`app_bn.arb`), raised when the cancellation carries a GMS code (matched as `^\[\d+\]` on the exception description) or when the code is `clientConfigurationError`/`providerConfigurationError`; a description-less `canceled` stays silent, which the pre-existing test still pins. The screens needed no change — they already show a banner for every kind except `cancelled`.
- **The temporary `debugPrint` was removed** once it had served its purpose; the diagnostic is now a permanent, tested mapping rather than a log line.
- **Server-side work is the user's, and is documented.** `README.md` gained *Google Sign-In Setup and Troubleshooting*: the debug and release SHA-1/SHA-256 fingerprints, both Android OAuth clients in the same project as the Web client, Play's own app-signing certificate (Play re-signs uploads), the package name `com.legalcasediary.app`, the consent-screen *Test users* rule while unpublished, and the reproducible command. The user chose to register **both** debug and release fingerprints.

Its consequences were carried through instead: `court_names_steps_test.dart`'s expected card label, the `settings_courtNamesSteps` mockup card and the category list in `docs/tasks/12_settings_screens.md` now all read “Sessions Courts (Criminal)”, and `flutter gen-l10n` was rerun so the generated localizations agree. The Bengali ARB was **left untouched** (still “জেলা ও দায়রা (ফৌজদারি)”) — rewriting Bengali copy is the user's call, and it is flagged below. The v0.0.341 history line's claim that the label was *restored to match its mockup* stands as recorded but is superseded by `0.0.342`.

- **Setup runbook written for a novice (the user's request).** `docs/google_oauth_setup.md` walks the whole console: the three-party mental model (app certificate → Android client, `serverClientId` → Web client, consent screen → who may sign in); the exact project number, package name, Web client ID and both SHA-1s; the mandatory Branding/Audience registration and the *Testing* → **Test users** rule; scopes (`openid` added by hand, nothing sensitive); one Android client per certificate — debug, release, and **Play's own app-signing certificate**, because Play re-signs uploads; the iOS client as a later step; checking the Web client's redirect URI against Supabase's callback; the Supabase Google provider step (Web client ID first, secret only there); then ordered pass/fail verification, a symptom → cause table, and a tick-box checklist. Grounded in Google's *Manage OAuth Clients* help page (which confirms the SHA-1 only, and the 5-minutes-to-a-few-hours propagation) and Supabase's *Sign in with Google* guide (Web-first comma-separated client IDs). Linked from `README.md`.
- **Second correction: the Authorized domain must be the full project host, not `supabase.co` (v0.0.347).** `supabase.co` is rejected with *"Invalid domain: must be a top private domain"* — correctly, because `supabase.co` (like `supabase.in` and `supabase.net`) is listed in the **private section of the Public Suffix List**, so it is a public suffix and not a registrable domain. The registrable (top private) domain of the callback host `https://lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback` is therefore **`lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau.supabase.co`**, which is what goes in box 1 — matching the community reports that use the Supabase project URL itself as the authorized domain. §5.3 and the §14 checklist updated.
- **The user's Google Sites pages are excluded from the Branding page, with the reason and the upgrade path recorded (v0.0.346).** The user has a Google Site (`https://sites.google.com/view/legal-case-diary/{about,privacy-policy,terms-of-service}`) and asked whether to use it as the App domain. Answer: **no**, because `sites.google.com` is **Google's** domain — *App Homepage* help page: *"Your homepage and privacy policy should not be hosted on a third-party platform where you can't verify that you own your subdomain. For example: Google Sites, Facebook, Instagram, Twitter."* The homepage must be *"hosted on a verified domain you own"*, and `sites.google.com` cannot be added to *Authorized domains* either. The pages stay useful for the Play Console listing (which does accept them) and in-app links. **Upgrade path documented:** buy a domain, connect it to the same Google Site (Settings → Custom domains), verify it in Search Console, then add it as an authorized domain and point the three App-domain fields at it — a Google Site served on your own verified domain is acceptable; it is the `sites.google.com` host Google rejects. New §5.2.1.
- **Correction to the same runbook: Authorized domains is mandatory, and the correct value is `supabase.co` (v0.0.345).** My first pass told the user to leave the Authorized-domains boxes empty; the console refuses that with *"Invalid domain: must not be empty"*. Google's *Manage OAuth App Branding* help page is explicit that *all* domains used on the Branding page **or in an OAuth client's configuration** must be pre-registered, and this project's only such domain is the Supabase callback host `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau.supabase.co` — so its top private domain `supabase.co` goes in box 1 (the bare domain, no protocol or path; the full project host is the fallback if the form refuses it). Ownership is not checked at entry time; it matters only at verification, where the established Supabase answer is to confirm your own domain and tell Google the Supabase entry is the third-party auth provider, keeping your own domain first. If the user later owns a domain it goes in as a second entry, first in the list, and the §5.2 App-domain links then point at it. §5.3 and the §14 checklist both corrected.
- **The runbook's Branding section was too thin (user's follow-up, fixed).** §5 now walks every field the console shows: the top fields (app name, support email, developer contact email — the last is **not** user-visible), the *App domain* block (**Application home page** / **privacy policy link** / **terms of service link** — all left blank while in Testing, required only for verification, and never pointed at a URL the developer does not own), and the *Authorized domains* block (all three boxes blank for this app: a domain there must be **owned** and ownership-verified, so `supabase.co` must **not** be added; the Supabase callback is a redirect URI on the Web client, a different page and rule, and the app signs in natively without ever visiting it). Added a matching blank-fields tick to the §14 checklist. Version `0.0.344`.
- **Verification.** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **1241 passed** (1239 + 2 new classification tests); `court_names_steps_test.dart` green under the new label; `record_version.py --check` OK. Version `0.0.341` (Google classification), `0.0.342` (label follow-through), `0.0.343` (runbook), `0.0.344` (Branding page expanded).

Next: the user adds the debug (and release) Android OAuth client SHA-1(s), reruns `dart run tool/flutter_with_env.dart run -d M2101K6G`, and confirms a session — an `id_token` grant appearing in the Auth logs is the proof. Nothing else is open; the change is uncommitted.

### 2026-10-01 — Build wrapper: credentials injected for debug and release — COMPLETE

- **Request.** Let the credentials be injected during a debug/release build without having to remember the flag each time, while keeping them out of the repository.
- **What it is.** `tool/flutter_with_env.dart` forwards every argument to `flutter` and appends `--dart-define-from-file=<project root>/env.json`, so both `run` (debug) and `build` (release) go through one path. It resolves `env.json` from the project root (usable from any working directory), uses `flutter.bat` on Windows so the executable resolves reliably, and **exits non-zero with a clear message when `env.json` is missing**, so a release can no longer ship silently offline. The constants are compile-time, so this is genuinely build-time injection — there is no runtime fetch; a hot restart still will not pick up a changed file, only a rebuild will.
- **Usage.** `dart run tool/flutter_with_env.dart run -d <device>` and `dart run tool/flutter_with_env.dart build apk --release`. Documented in `README.md` alongside the existing raw-flag commands.
- **Records (rule 14 — absent from the plan).** A **Build Credentials Seam** bullet was added to the plan's Resolved Design Clarifications, the same follow-up noted in `docs/tasks/17_auth_profiles_and_firm_onboarding.md`'s Addendum, and `README.md` updated.
- **Verification.** `dart analyze tool/flutter_with_env.dart` clean and `flutter analyze` clean; the wrapper ran a real `flutter test test/l10n/arb_keys_test.dart` (4 passed) proving the flag is forwarded, and the no-args path printed usage with exit 2. Version `0.0.340`.
- **Resume point:** no feature work open. The four-plan Premium change (v0.0.339) and this wrapper are both uncommitted.
