What is left is the user's, not the code's: the Google Cloud OAuth client (Android package + SHA-1, Web client ID) with the Google provider enabled; the **Reset Password e-mail template must include `{{ .Token }}`**; Task 16's Realtime Authorization toggle; and the two device passes — `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` and Phase 4's login → airplane mode → restart → data accessible. The next task is Task 18/19 (logout flow, sync service), which owns the two items this task left open on purpose: the branched logout path and the domain write path's `user_id`/`last_modified_by` still coming from the Part A placeholder.

### 2026-09-29 — Task 17 Phase 4: an expired token can never lock a lawyer out of their own diary — COMPLETE

- **Local data is still not gated on JWT validity, and now the reason is written down where it is enforced.** Access tokens last about an hour and refreshing needs network, so nothing in the app consults the token before opening the database: the sign-in gate reads local facts (a session, a satisfied gate, or rows the user authored), and `SessionStatus` only ever produces a *notice*, never a lock. The new `lib/sync/connectivity_monitor.dart` implements the `connectivity_plus` deferral from Task 01 — the current value first, then every change — and reports the **link, not the internet**, deliberately: a captive portal still counts as connected, because this answer only decides whether to *try*. Every network call still classifies its own failure through `mapAuthFailure`, so a wrong answer costs one failed request, never a wrong screen. A platform without the plugin (a unit test, a desktop build) answers "reachable", the safe direction.
- **The two states item 3 asks to distinguish are now distinct, and only one of them asks anything of the user.** `SessionStatus` is `signedOut`, `usable`, `staleOffline` or `reLoginRequired`, decided in one provider: a live session is `usable`; no session plus an account the device remembers and no connection is `staleOffline` (*"offline, token stale"* — the diary is fully usable and sync is simply paused, **nothing is asked and no notice appears**, because there is nothing the user could do); a rejection the server actually issued (revoked session, changed password) while the device is online is `reLoginRequired`. Unknown connectivity resolves to *online*, which is the actionable choice — a stale "offline" would silently hide the one thing the user has to do.
- **The notice offers the action and nothing else** (`presentation/session_state.dart`, mounted above the navigator in `app.dart` like the OEM setup guide and the backup nudge). It is a compact strip, not a window or a dialog: the diary behind it keeps its full height and stays usable, which is the whole point of never gating local data on the session — and its single tap goes to the Login screen through the router it is handed. Tap-outside still reaches the app, because the strip is a `Positioned` child rather than a scrim.
- **Nothing is wiped, and that is now pinned.** A rejected session leaves `cases` and a non-empty `sync_queue` exactly as they were — the plan's rule is that the **branched logout flow** (Task 19) is the only thing that may discard unsynced work, and only with the user: sync-then-wipe, or block-and-offer-an-export, or auto-export for a free user. Task 17's re-login path therefore removes nothing.
- **Reconnect is wired to the same single refresh.** `AccountRefreshScope` now also listens for a false→true connectivity transition and calls `accountRefreshProvider` — the third moment the plan names, alongside launch and resume — so a device that was offline re-pulls its firm permissions as soon as the link is back. The sync workers in Task 19 read the same `isOnlineProvider` to know when to drain the queue.
- **Tests:** 10 new in `test/features/offline_session_test.dart`: all four session states (including the same rejection resolving to `reLoginRequired` online and to `staleOffline` offline), an offline launch with a stale token still opening the diary and reading case data, a rejection leaving the diary *and* a pending queue row intact, the notice appearing only for the online rejection and routing to Login, and the reconnect transition firing the refresh. `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **907 passed**. Version `0.0.304`.
- **Resume point:** Phase 5 — owner-side invite primitives only (generate a ≥50-bit Crockford Base32 code, show the plaintext once, store only its SHA-256 hash; revoke an unused invite; online-only with the "connect to manage your firm" state), plus the invite-onboarding tests: a valid code joins, a used or revoked code is rejected. The full Manage Associates UI stays in Task 20. **Owed by the user:** the Google Cloud OAuth client (Android package + SHA-1, Web client ID) with the Google provider enabled; the **Reset Password e-mail template must include the token**; Task 16's Realtime Authorization toggle. **Still outstanding from Part A:** `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on a device, and the Phase 4 manual step this task names — login → airplane mode → restart → data accessible.

### 2026-09-29 — Task 17 Phase 3: the account's profile is cached once and every firm-conditional decision reads it — COMPLETE

- **`ProfileRepository` is real now** (`lib/features/accounts_and_profile/data/profile_repository.dart`, replacing the Task 01 stub). Its `refresh()` pulls `profiles`, and for a firm member also `firms` and this user's `firm_members` row, into the three **pull-only caches** — never queued, never mirrored anywhere else (AGENTS rule 4). `subscriptionExpiryToEpochMs` is a pure, tested function: PostgREST's ISO-8601 string, the `Z` form, an explicit offset, an already-numeric value and a `DateTime` all land on the cache's epoch ms, and anything unreadable is **null rather than a guess** (a wrong expiry would silently grant or deny premium). `permissions_synced_at` is stamped on every successful refresh — the plan's permission-freshness stamp. A failed refresh is never fatal and never destructive: the cache keeps its last known values and every read is advisory, because `push_changes` stays authoritative.
- **One provider decides firm mode** (`data/account_mode.dart`): `firmModeProvider` is `firm_id != null`, verbatim from the plan, and `accountRoleProvider`, `canAdd/canEdit/canDeleteDirectlyProvider` and `canManageFirmProvider` all derive from the same cached rows — no call site re-derives it, so a refresh cannot leave the drawer, the Profile header and the delete path disagreeing. An individual (or an unreachable cache) keeps full rights over their own diary; a **firm member with no cached membership row fails closed** (`can_add`/`can_edit`/`can_delete` false), which is exactly what the advisory-cache rule wants. `accountCacheUserIdProvider` is the id these caches are keyed by — the account when one is signed in, the placeholder otherwise — and the two Part-A readers of the same caches (the case-write permission check, the entitlement check in the monthly nudge) now go through it, so a signed-in account never reads a row left behind by the local-only install.
- **Refresh runs on launch and on resume** (`presentation/account_refresh_scope.dart`, wrapped around the app in `app.dart`). It starts after the first frame, never blocks a launch, and waits for the session to be known before deciding (a cold start's first frame sees no identity yet — the test caught that). Reconnect arrives with Phase 4's monitor and calls the same `accountRefreshProvider`. A successful refresh also **hydrates a blank local name** from the server's `full_name` (a device that signed up elsewhere, an associate named by the firm); a name the user typed here is never overwritten. A redemption calls the refresh immediately, so a new associate's permission flags are cached before the first case write.
- **The Revision 2.4 Addendum's cache rule holds:** the caches are **emptied, not migrated** on logout (`AuthViewModel.signOut()`) and on an account switch (adoption already did it), so one account's cached name can never be shown to another.
- **Manage Account shows real data inside the unchanged mockup body.** The hardcoded `Asadusjaman` / `advocate.asad@law.com` sample values are gone: the name is the user's own record (falling back to the server's name), the email is the signed-in identity, an empty account renders as dashes and the avatar as `?`. Logout really signs out, empties the account caches, confirms and returns to Settings; **Change Password** reuses the Login screen's emailed-code reset (one password path for the app); **Purchase New Subscription** opens the existing paywall. Role and subscription state had no row in either mockup, and the mockup wins — so they sit behind the **Subscription Details** row, in a sheet this task adds (the same pattern as Profile's pencil): Role, Firm (firm mode only) and Subscription as *Free* or *Premium Until 12/01/2027*, derived by the single `subscription_expiry + 48h grace` rule from `premium_access.dart`.
- **Tests:** 26 new — `account_mode_test.dart` (19: the timestamp conversion, every role/permission combination, the freshness stamp, the firm row, premium from expiry versus the stale status flag, the fake repository's refresh/invalidate paths, sign-out emptying all three caches, redemption pulling permissions, and the launch/resume scope) and `manage_account_test.dart` (6). `profile_picture_test.dart` moved off the mockup's sample name, as it must. `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **897 passed**. Version `0.0.303`.
- **Open integration item handed to Task 19, deliberately not done here:** the **domain write path** still stamps `user_id`/`last_modified_by` from the Part A placeholder (`local_identity_service`), while adoption has re-pointed existing rows to `auth.uid()`. New rows written after a sign-in are therefore owned by a UUID the server has never seen. Cache *reads* are already keyed correctly; making the write path use the authenticated id is the sweep that belongs with the sync service (it touches ~10 repositories plus their tests). It changes nothing in tests, where no account is signed in and the two ids are the same.
- **Resume point:** Phase 4 — offline session handling: never gate local data on JWT validity, distinguish "offline, token stale" from "online, session invalid", re-login without ever wiping local data while `sync_queue` has pending rows, and the connectivity monitor's reconnect hook into `accountRefreshProvider`. **Owed by the user:** the Google Cloud OAuth client (Android package + SHA-1, Web client ID) with the Google provider enabled; the **Reset Password e-mail template must include the token** (the in-app code flow needs it); Task 16's Realtime Authorization toggle. **Still outstanding from Part A:** `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on a device.

### 2026-09-29 — Task 17 Phase 2: both auth screens are the mockups, onboarding is retryable, and no existing install can be locked out — COMPLETE

- **The task file's claim that the login/sign-up mockups do not exist was wrong, and the mockups won.** `mockup_html_screens/settings_onlineSync_login` and `settings_onlineSync_signup` both exist (each says *"the shipped screen is a Part B stub; this mockup is the design"*), so both screens are built to them, in the shared card language of `lib/features/auth/presentation/auth_widgets.dart` — the light page, the one bordered card, the underlined fields, the black primary button, the "Or Continue With" divider, the Google button, the switch line and a hand-drawn four-path `GoogleMark` (no binary asset, no SVG renderer).
- **Things the mockups carry that the task file never mentioned are in scope:** the Google button on both screens, the "Forgot Password?" link, and the required **Bar Association** field. The one deliberate addition the mockup could not show is the **Account Type** choice — Individual or Join Existing Firm — which reveals the plan's single-use **Invite Code** field, and the **Check Your Email** state for a project with confirmation on (the account exists, has no session, so the screen says so and the typed code is kept until the first sign-in that has one). `sharedMessages_signInRequired` no longer says "(Task 17)": no user-facing ARB string names a task number.
- **Every failure has its own words, and a retry clears them.** `AuthErrorBanner` renders a typed `AuthFailureKind` through one switch (`authErrorText`), so wrong credentials, an unconfirmed address, an address already in use, a weak password, the e-mail rate limit, an invalid invite code, the invite rate limit, an already-a-member redemption, *this device belongs to another account*, no connection and *no backend in this build* are twelve different sentences — and a cancelled Google sheet is deliberately **not** an error. The forms validate before anything is sent (required fields, one-`@` e-mail shape, the mockup's eight-character password rule, the terms box), and a failed invite redemption keeps the code in preferences so onboarding stays retryable, which is the Addendum's rule.
- **The first-run gate is decided once, from facts already on the device.** `authGateRequiredProvider` asks for sign-in only when there is no session, no cached account, no satisfied gate **and nothing the user authored locally** — user-authored means rows in `cases`, `notes` or `case_references`, because the seeded reference data and the per-install identity exist on every install, old or new. An install that already holds its author's work opens straight into the diary, and a database that cannot be read is never turned into a lockout.
- **Adoption is one transaction over three classes, and it fails closed.** `IdentityAdoptionService` rewrites ownership, firm scope and attribution for the twelve push/pull domain tables, rewrites `user_id`/`last_modified_by`/`created_by`/`requested_by`/`reviewed_by` inside queue **payload, base payload and pre-mutation snapshot**, re-keys the per-user preference rows (`user_preferences`, `case_notification_preferences` — preferences follow the person, never the firm), and empties the pull-only caches (`profiles`, `firms`, `firm_members`) instead of moving them. It records the placeholder id, the account, the timestamp and the per-table counts in `sync_metadata` under `auth.adoption_provenance`, binds the device under `auth.bound_user_id`, is a no-op when the same account signs in again, and raises `accountMismatch` **having written nothing** when a different account opens the device — the diary stays readable and `authAdoptionBlockedProvider` explains why.
- **One real defect the tests found.** The sign-up card's wordmark row (`gavel + app name`) overflowed a 340px card once the text scale grew; the name is now `Flexible`, so it wraps instead of clipping. Nothing else about the two screens changed.
- **Tests:** 46 new — `auth_login_test.dart` (11), `auth_signup_test.dart` (14), `auth_gate_test.dart` (12: the gate plus adoption, provenance, refusal and cache clearing), `auth_recovery_test.dart` (9) — driven through `auth_test_support.dart`, whose `FakeAuthRepository`/`FakeFirmRepository` sit at the network boundary and whose `pumpAuthRouter` supplies the routes sign-up navigates to. `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **871 passed**. Version `0.0.302`.
- **Resume point:** Phase 3 — filling the `profiles`/`firms`/`firm_members` pull-only caches from the server, the firm-mode provider, wiring Profile and Manage Account to the cached record, and the permission refresh after a redeem. **Owed by the user:** the Google Cloud OAuth client (Android package + SHA-1, Web client ID) with the Google provider enabled in the dashboard; the **Reset Password e-mail template must include the token** or the in-app code flow cannot work; Task 16's Realtime Authorization toggle. **Still outstanding from Part A:** `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on a device.

### 2026-09-29 — Task 17 Phase 1: the app can reach Supabase, and a build without the keys is still the offline diary — COMPLETE

- **Three packages, and the first networking code in the repo.** `supabase_flutter` 2.17.2, `connectivity_plus` 7.3.1 (the Task 01 deferral, first used in Phase 4) and `google_sign_in` 7.2.0 for the native ID-token flow the user chose. Part A's "no networking" rule is now superseded for this task only.
- **The secrets seam is `--dart-define-from-file=env.json`, git-ignored**, the same pattern as `supabase/.env.local`. `lib/core/constants/supabase_config.dart` reads `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` and `GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID` and exposes `isConfigured`. A local `env.json` now exists with the real URL and the project's **legacy anon key** (both key types were fetched read-only through the connector; the JWT was chosen because every PostgREST/GoTrue version accepts it, while `sb_publishable_…` depends on the server build). `GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID` is still empty — the user owes that value with the Google Cloud client.

Pinned by a test.
- **No backend configured = exactly the Part A app.** `main.dart` initializes Supabase only when `isConfigured`, and a failure there is caught and logged by error type only — the diary never waits on the network and never fails to open. With no configuration the auth providers resolve to `UnavailableAuthRepository`, which reports an empty identity, makes `signOut()` a no-op, and fails every auth call with `AuthFailureKind.unavailable`.
- **The session goes to secure storage, not preferences.** `SecureSessionStorage` implements Supabase's `LocalStorage` over the app's existing `flutter_secure_storage` adapter (the two classes in `encryption_key_manager.dart` became public rather than duplicating a second adapter for the same plugin). No password, recovery code or token is stored anywhere else — `LocalAuthService` caches only the last identity (user id + email) so an offline launch knows whose diary this is, plus the first-run gate flag for Phase 2.
- **One typed boundary: `AuthRepository`.** Screens will talk to it, never to the SDK, so a widget test can override one provider and drive every auth state with no server. Failures are classified into screen-level kinds by a pure `mapAuthFailure` (wrong credentials, unconfirmed email, e-mail in use, weak password, rate limit, invite rate limit, dismissed Google sheet, network, unavailable, unknown) — the SDK's "offline" vs "revoked" distinction is preserved as `AuthRetryableFetchException` → `network` versus a non-retryable refresh failure, which gotrue reports as `signedOut(sessionExpired)`, i.e. exactly the split Phase 4 needs.
- **Tests:** 14 new in `test/features/auth_bootstrap_test.dart`; `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **825 passed**. Version `0.0.301`.

### 2026-09-29 — The seeded tables are complete; the dashboard's 100-row pages were the illusion — COMPLETE

- **The report:** `courts` and `case_types` on Supabase looked short of the app's own list in `lib/core/constants/pre_seeded_data.dart`, and there was no time to check the other four tables.

Supabase: the same six totals, and the courts split per court category is identical too (304 district civil, 210 district sessions, 152 CJM, 144 executive magistrate, 106 CMM, 63 High Court Division, 34 metropolitan sessions, 3 appellate). Beyond the totals, the **sets of ids** in the generated migration and in the live tables diff clean: identical for courts, case_types, steps, on_behalfs and case_categories; `court_categories` ids differ only because they are bare category keys (`appellate_division`), not prefixed tags. No row is soft-deleted, custom or user-owned.
- **Why it looked truncated.** The Supabase Table Editor renders **100 rows per page** — and `courts` (1,016) and `case_types` (160) are precisely the two tables above that threshold, while the ones that looked complete (`court_categories` 8, `case_categories` 9, `on_behalfs` 40) are below it. The same illusion has a second cause if the API is used instead: a single PostgREST response stops at the project's `max_rows` (1000 by default), so `/rest/v1/courts` returns 1,000 of 1,016.
- **The answer is now one command, read-only.** `tool/check_supabase_seed.dart` prints the app-side and Supabase count for all six seeded tables, exits non-zero on any disagreement, and names each app constant the number comes from. It reuses the applier's connection-string parsing, `psql` runner and one-project guard, so it cannot read anywhere but `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`. Its statements are read-only, so — unlike the applier's bootstrap DDL — it is not part of `supabase/AllSupabaseSql.md`; the ledger header now states that boundary explicitly (shipped SQL is recorded, read-only checks and the rolling-back `supabase/tests/*.sql` are not).
- **Pinned so it cannot drift:** `test/supabase/seed_parity_test.dart` gained a test asserting the six counts (8 / 9 / 1,016 / 209 / 160 / 40), so trimming the constants without regenerating the seed migration fails the suite.
- **Tests:** `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test test/supabase/` **63 passed**; `dart run tool/check_supabase_seed.dart` reports all six `same`; ledger and seed `--check` clean.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 17 may begin, and the two advisories in `supabase/README.md` (*Known gaps*: `anon`-executable SECURITY DEFINER functions, and `auth.uid()` re-evaluated per row) are still the open hardening items.

### 2026-09-29 — Task 16 is applied to the live project, and the first real apply found four defects that reading could not — COMPLETE

- **One command applies the whole set and leaves the CLI's history intact.** `tool/apply_supabase_migrations.dart` reads `SUPABASE_DB_URL` from the git-ignored `supabase/.env.local`, applies `supabase/migrations/*.sql` in filename order through `psql`, and records each version in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` — the table created with the CLI's own DDL, from the constants in `pkg/migration/history.go`. `supabase db push` now reports all fourteen as applied instead of re-running them. `--dry-run` prints the plan and touches no database; a second real run answers *Nothing to do*; the file list, the apply order and the guard are covered by `test/supabase/migration_applier_test.dart` (13 tests).
- **Each file is one transaction with its own history row**, so a file that fails rolls back whole and is never recorded as applied — proven on the first run, when migration 7 failed and left neither its objects nor its version behind. The one thing the tool prints about its own SQL is filtered output: psql's per-statement status tags and the `... does not exist, skipping` notices from every `drop ... if exists` are counted and suppressed, while a NOTICE that matters (pg_cron being unavailable, say) is printed verbatim — pinned by two tests so the filter cannot hide an error.
- **It refuses to run anywhere but `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`.** The connection string's `postgres.<ref>` user (or the `db.<ref>.supabase.co` host) is checked against a one-entry allow-list before anything connects, `ykbwzodadijtiizobehk` is refused by ref *and* name, and the URL is masked in every line printed. AGENTS.md rule 12 is enforced by code, not by care.
- **The ledger gained the one section that is not a migration.** `supabase/AllSupabaseSql.md` opens with a **Bootstrap** section generated from the applier's own constants — the four statements that create the history table plus the version row written per file — because rule 13 covers every statement this project runs against Supabase, not only the versioned ones. The drift tests pin it.
- **Applied: 14 of 14 versions recorded, and the project's shape verified by query.** 23 public tables, RLS on all 23, 57 policies, 21 functions, and the 1,442 seeded reference rows byte-identical to the client's constants (1,016 courts, 209 steps, 160 case types, 40 on-behalfs, 8 court categories, 9 case categories). `pg_cron 1.6.4` was installed by the last migration and the `purge-tombstones` job is **active** on `15 3 * * *` — so pg_cron was never a dashboard-only setting after all, and `supabase/README.md` now says so. Only **Realtime Authorization** is still the user's to toggle.
- **Defect 1 — `approve_payment` did not compile.** `if v_submission.amount <> case ... when 'monthly' then c_monthly_bdt ... end then` fails: plpgsql reads an IF condition up to the first top-level `then`, so the CASE's own `when ... then` ended it early. The server answered `syntax error at end of input`, and the reported position (statement line 62 = file line 112) is what identified it. The CASE is now parenthesized; it was the only such expression in the set.
- **Defect 2 — `approve_payment` was ambiguous the first time it ran.** The parameter `payment_submission_id` shares its name with a column of `payment_approval_events`, so `where e.payment_submission_id = payment_submission_id` raised *It could refer to either a PL/pgSQL variable or a table column*. The reference is qualified against the function instead of renaming the parameter, so the RPC's public signature is untouched.
- **Defect 3 — the column-privilege migration did not actually protect anything, and the RLS matrix caught it.** Supabase grants **table-level** privileges on every table in `public` to `anon`/`authenticated` through its default privileges, and a table-level GRANT is not undone by a column-level REVOKE: after the migration ran, `has_column_privilege('authenticated','public.profiles','subscription_expiry','UPDATE')` was **still true** and a client could still have promoted itself to premium or relinked itself to another firm. The migration now drops the table-level UPDATE and grants UPDATE back only on what a client may write — `profiles.full_name`, `firms.name`, and nothing at all on `payment_submissions` — so a column added later is closed by default. `test/supabase/schema_contract_test.dart` now pins the table-level revoke *and* that no server-owned column is ever named in a grant, so the weaker form cannot come back.
- **Defect 4 — `anon` could execute both RPCs.** `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC` leaves a role's own direct grant in place, and Supabase's default privileges hand `anon` EXECUTE on every new function in `public`; the smoke test's `anon` check therefore reached `approve_payment` and came back `admin_required` instead of `permission denied`. Both revokes now name `anon` explicitly.
- **The two SQL suites now pass against the live project, which is what closes Task 16's acceptance items.** `supabase/tests/rls_matrix_test.sql` — 39 assertion call sites, exit 0: anon denied everywhere, firm visibility both ways, spoofed INSERTs refused, hard DELETE affecting zero rows, the can_delete trigger firing for a member without it, built-in reference rows unmodifiable, invite/payment/preference scoping, and the column revokes raising `permission denied`. `supabase/tests/rpc_smoke_test.sql` — exit 0: the non-admin refusal, approval, `already_approved` retry, the audit event, the firm mirror, the three-per-24h payment rate limit, the 50-case ceiling and the freed slot after a soft delete, `join_firm`'s invalid-code attempt, normalized-code success, `already_member`, the sixth-attempt refusal, and `anon` denial. Both run as three (resp. two) **real** auth users created for the run and deleted afterwards, and both roll back.
- **`get_advisors`, as the task asks.** Security: three INFO notes for `app_admins`/`invite_attempts`/`mutation_receipts` having RLS with no policies — that is the deny-all design, not a gap — plus a WARN that `anon` can execute 17 SECURITY DEFINER functions, `purge_tombstones()` among them. Performance: 50 policies re-evaluate `auth.uid()` per row (`auth_rls_initplan`), and 24 foreign keys lack a covering index. No ERROR-level finding. The `anon`-EXECUTE family is **the one real gap left open**: the two RPCs are locked, and closing the rest is a one-migration change (`revoke execute on all functions in schema public from anon`, plus an `alter default privileges` so functions added by later tasks stay closed) that the user should approve rather than have me fold into this task's scope. Both gaps are written into `supabase/README.md` under *Known gaps*.

### 2026-09-29 — Task 16 (Supabase schema, RLS & triggers) is complete: 14 ordered migrations, seed parity and a policy matrix the user can run against any project — COMPLETE

- **What landed.** The repo's first `supabase/` tree: `config.toml` (CLI link/push workflow), a README covering the table classes and the apply/test commands, and three Phase 1 migrations — the core schema (`20260929100000`), the sign-up trigger (`20260929100100`) and the generated built-in reference data (`20260929100200`). `.gitignore` now ignores `supabase/.temp/` and `supabase/.branches/`.
- **The schema mirrors the shipped Drift tables column for column**, which is the user's decision for this task: `party_phone` stays a single column (Rev 2.4's separate `petitioner_phone`/`respondent_phone` were withdrawn on 2026-09-29 — see the entry at the top of this file; no Part A change is pending), `notes.items` exists, and no plan-only column was invented. 23 tables total: the 12 push/pull domain tables, 3 pull-only caches (`profiles`, `firms`, `firm_members`), the 2 per-user preference tables the Addendum requires, and the server-managed `firm_invites`/`payment_submissions` (the four remaining server tables arrive in Phase 2). `case_categories` and the preference tables are not in the task file's Phase 1 list but are required by Rev 2.4 and by `case_types.case_category_id` — flagged below.
- **Type conventions, one rule each.** Client-generated row keys stay **TEXT** so a client UUID string is never rejected; identity/scope columns are **UUID** with FKs to `auth.users`/`firms`; client display metadata is **BIGINT** epoch ms (a 32-bit INTEGER cannot hold epoch ms — 24.8 days of range); server instants are **TIMESTAMPTZ**; reference keys are the stable deterministic seed tags. `firms` is created before `profiles` because both it and `firm_members` reference it.
- **The seed migration is generated, not hand-written** — 1,016 courts plus 8 categories, 9 case categories, 209 steps, 160 case types and 40 on-behalf roles, every ID taken verbatim from `pre_seeded_data.dart` so Drift, Supabase and backups name the same rows. `tool/generate_supabase_seed.dart` writes it (`--check` fails if stale); `test/supabase/seed_parity_test.dart` compares the committed file to the generator's output byte for byte after CRLF normalization, and `test/supabase/schema_contract_test.dart` checks the structural contract (ordered filenames, header comments, idempotent creates, sync columns on the domain class only, the two unique constraints, no credentials). Both files are extended by each later phase.
- **`version` starts at 1 on seeded rows and re-seeding never bumps it**; the upsert mirrors the client seed runner (`on conflict (id) do update … where <table>.is_user_modified = false`), so a row a user or firm has customized is never overwritten. A fixed `updated_at` constant (2026-01-01Z) keeps the generated file deterministic; `server_updated_at` is stamped by the Phase 3 trigger at apply time.
- **Not verified, deliberately.** No Supabase project was available and this host has no Docker, so "migrations apply cleanly" is **unverified**. The exact commands (`npx supabase link --project-ref … && npx supabase db push`, or a psql loop) are in `supabase/README.md`.
- **Flagged for the user:** (a) reference-data PKs are TEXT tags, not the plan's "UUID PK"; (b) `cases.party_phone` is single, per the mirror-the-app decision; (c) `conflict_log`'s server mirror carries `user_id`/`firm_id` (RLS and the Realtime topic structurally need an owner column) plus a server `created_at`; (d) `delete_requests.entity_snapshot` and `conflict_log` payloads are TEXT, not JSONB, so a stored JSON string cannot silently become a JSON string value; (e) `case_history.source_mutation_id` and `conflict_log.mutation_id` are TEXT, matching the client-generated keys.
- **Phase 2 landed** (`20260929101000`): `invite_attempts` (with the `(user_id, attempted_at desc)` index), `mutation_receipts`, `app_admins` and `payment_approval_events` (one event per submission via UNIQUE, FK `on delete restrict`). RLS is enabled on all four **with no policy at all**, so `anon`/`authenticated` are denied every command while the owner and the service role keep working. Two immutability triggers: `app_admins` refuses UPDATE only — a grant is immutable but revocation is a DELETE, which an append-only registry must keep possible — and `payment_approval_events` refuses UPDATE and DELETE outright. `mutation_receipts`' ≥210-day retention and the "never purge a referenced receipt" rule are documented in the migration.
- **Phase 3 landed, in six migrations.** (1) *Access helpers* — `current_firm_id`, `is_firm_owner`, `can_edit_firm`, `can_delete_firm`, `is_premium`, `is_admin`, `can_read_topic` and `sha256_hex`, all `SECURITY DEFINER` because a policy on `profiles` that reads `firm_members` and a policy on `firm_members` that reads `profiles` would otherwise recurse; entitlement is derived from `subscription_expiry + 48h`. (2) *Sync and guards* — `server_updated_at` on all twelve domain tables, the delete-transition rule (`can_delete` or the firm owner; individual rows always allowed), the resurrection guard reading `current_setting('app.allow_resurrection')` and comparing **no** client timestamp/version/UUID, the free-tier ceiling (advisory lock, then count live cases, reject at 50) and the payment rate limit (overwrite `submitted_at`, count 24h server time, reject at 3). (3) *Payment approval* — the firm mirror trigger and `approve_payment(uuid)` in one migration: `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC`, `GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated`, an `app_admins` gate (because every caller shares the `authenticated` role), `FOR UPDATE` lock, pending/plan/amount validation with the ৳100‑৳1,000 prices as named constants, `already_approved`/`already_resolved` idempotency, `GREATEST(COALESCE(expiry, now()), now()) + 1 month/year`, submission update, mirror, and exactly one immutable audit event. (4) *join_firm* — rate limit (5 per 15 min), lazy day-old cleanup, sha256 of the normalized (trimmed, uppercased) code, unused-and-unrevoked match, membership + profile relink + invite consumed, and **failed matches return** `{status: invalid_code}` rather than raising so the attempt insert commits. (5) *Realtime* — one `{table, id}` ping per change to `firm:{firm_id}`/`user:{user_id}` (or `requested_by` for `delete_requests`), plus a `realtime.messages` select policy calling `can_read_topic`, created inside an exception-tolerant block because Realtime owns that table. (6) *Column privileges* — client UPDATE revoked on `payment_submissions(status, approved_at, approved_by)`, `profiles(subscription_expiry, subscription_status, role, firm_id)` and `firms(subscription_expiry, subscription_status, owner_id)`.
- **Two deliberate additions beyond the task's literal list, both flagged:** `profiles.role`/`firm_id` are revoked too — RLS cannot stop a user promoting themselves to `firm` owner or relinking to another firm, and every policy and guard trusts those columns; and `app_admins` refuses **UPDATE only**, because revoking admin rights is a DELETE and an append-only registry must keep that possible.
- **`supabase/tests/rpc_smoke_test.sql`** is the runnable proof: it takes `-v admin_id=<uuid> -v user_id=<uuid>`, grants admin inside the transaction, then exercises a non-admin refusal, approval, `already_approved` retry with expiry moved exactly one month from a lapsed base, the audit event, the firm mirror, `not_found`, ledger immutability, three accepted submissions then a refused fourth, the 50-case ceiling and the freed slot after a soft delete, `join_firm`'s wrong-code attempt record, normalized-code success, `already_member`, the sixth-attempt refusal, and `anon` denial — then `rollback`s. **Not run here** (no project), so its green run is still outstanding.
- **Phase 4 landed, in three migrations.** RLS is enabled on every table (re-asserted in the last file so the whole set is one statement, not something to reconstruct), then the matrix: case data scoped to self-or-same-firm with `can_edit_firm` for updates and **no DELETE policy anywhere** (deletion is `is_deleted`); the six reference tables built-in-readable by every authenticated user with custom rows scoped to creator/firm; `delete_requests` readable by firm members, editable by its pending requester or a `can_delete` reviewer, never deletable; `conflict_log` own-rows-only; `profiles` readable by self **and same-firm members**, self-update only, no INSERT (the sign-up trigger owns creation); `firms`/`firm_members`/`firm_invites` owner-scoped (removing an associate is the one legitimate hard delete in the schema); `payment_submissions` own select/insert with no update path; `payment_approval_events` own receipt or admin; the two preference tables strictly self-only; the four server-internal tables keep RLS with **zero** policies. No policy or guard reads `subscription_status` — entitlement is always `is_premium()`.
- **The reference-table rule has one consequence worth knowing:** a *built-in* row has no creator and no firm, so `user_id = auth.uid()` and `can_edit_firm(firm_id)` are both false for it — the plan's rule literally is "creator or same-firm can_edit". Editing a pre-seeded court/step/case type/role/category is therefore local-only until the client models such an edit as a custom row; the contract test pins this (a built-in update removes 0 rows) and `rls_matrix_test.sql` asserts it against a live project.
- **`supabase/tests/rls_matrix_test.sql`** impersonates three users (`owner_id`, `associate_id`, `outsider_id`) the way PostgREST does — `set local role` plus `request.jwt.claims` — and checks the matrix end to end: anon denied everywhere, firm visibility both ways, a spoofed INSERT refused by `WITH CHECK`, can_edit updates allowed, hard DELETE affecting 0 rows, the can_delete trigger firing for a member without it, owner soft-delete allowed and resurrection refused, built-in rows unmodifiable, invite/payment/preference/delete-request scoping, `invite_attempts` invisible, and the column revokes raising `permission denied`. It rolls back. **Not run here** (no project).
- **Phase 5 landed.** `payment_submissions_user_submitted_at_idx (user_id, submitted_at desc)`; delta-pull indexes for all twelve domain tables — the unscoped `(server_updated_at, id)` composite plus `(firm_id, server_updated_at, id)` and `(user_id, server_updated_at, id)` (the one table without a `user_id`, `delete_requests`, gets `requested_by` instead), so every scoped, cursor-paged pull is index-backed; and `purge_tombstones(p_retention_days default 180)`, which deletes tombstoned rows children-first in FK-safe order using the tombstone's own `server_updated_at`, scheduled daily at 03:15 through `pg_cron` — with the extension creation and the schedule both guarded, so a project where pg_cron must be enabled from the dashboard still applies cleanly.
- **One gap found in my own Phase 1 work and closed here.** The Phase 1 file created tables but enabled RLS only in Phase 4. Supabase grants `anon`/`authenticated` table privileges by default, so a push that stopped between the two migrations would have left reachable tables — RLS is now switched on **in the same file that creates each table** (Phase 4's statements remain, idempotently), and the contract test enforces it per file, not merely "somewhere in the migration set".
- **Acceptance against the task file.** All five phases' Dart-side items pass: `flutter analyze` clean, version history recorded through `tool/record_version.py`, and every phase's test artefact present. The three SQL-side items — *migrations apply cleanly to a fresh project*, *RPC smoke tests via SQL pass*, *full clean-slate run succeeds end-to-end* — are **NOT VERIFIED**: no project was available and the user chose migrations-only, so nothing has been applied to Postgres yet. `supabase/tests/rpc_smoke_test.sql` and `supabase/tests/rls_matrix_test.sql` are written for exactly that run and roll back.
- **Addendum items:** 1, 2, 5, 6 and 7 pass (domain class only gets the sync contract; pull-only caches and server tables do not inherit it; `court_categories` has its own RLS, guards, broadcast trigger and delta index; custom reference data is firm-shared in firm mode and user-scoped in individual mode; `push_changes` stays in Task 18 with only its prerequisites created here). Item 4 passes — the self-only `user_preferences` and `case_notification_preferences` tables exist for premium cross-device sync. **Item 3 fails by the letter**: it asks for nullable `petitioner_phone`/`respondent_phone` on `cases`, and the server stores the app's single `party_phone` because the user chose to mirror the shipped client model. `is_user_modified` *is* present on all six editable reference tables. The phone columns therefore need a Part A change first, then one follow-up migration. *(Superseded the same day: the split was withdrawn, so nothing is owed. See the entry above.)*

### 2026-09-28 — The two skipped encryption tests were skipping on a host that has the cipher — COMPLETE

- **The report:** the suite always ends *2 skipped*. Which two, and can they be fixed?
- **They were the SQLite3MultipleCiphers checks in `test/database/encryption_integration_test.dart`** — *raw database bytes do not contain known plaintext* and *database rejects a wrong key without replacing data* — skipped by a hard-coded platform list: `Platform.isAndroid || Platform.isIOS || Platform.isMacOS`. The list was stale, not protective.
- **The cipher is not a platform property here, it is a property of the loaded library.** `pubspec.yaml` builds the native sqlite3 through the build hook with `source: sqlite3mc`, so `sqlite3mc.dll` is what the test VM loads (`build/native_assets/windows/sqlite3mc.dll`) — the same library the app runs against. Forcing the gate open proved it: both tests pass unchanged on this Windows host, including the check that no plaintext reaches the file.
- **The skip condition is now a capability probe instead of a platform name.** `_librarySupportsEncryption()` asks a throwaway in-memory database `PRAGMA cipher;` and treats a non-empty answer as support — the exact question the app's own `DatabaseFactory._hasCipher` already asks before it applies `PRAGMA key`. The cipher knows the pragma; plain SQLite does not know it and returns no rows. So the checks now run wherever the capability is real (this host, macOS, the phones) and still skip gracefully on a host whose library is plain SQLite, where `PRAGMA key` is a silent no-op that would write the data in the clear and make the tests fail for the wrong reason.
- **First probe pragma was wrong, and the run showed it.** `PRAGMA cipher_version` — the obvious choice — returns no rows in this sqlite3mc build (it answers `PRAGMA cipher;` with `[{chacha20: chacha20}]` but has no `cipher_version` and no `pragma_cipher_version` table function), so the group skipped itself again until the probe was aligned with the factory's. Found with a scratch probe test, since deleted.
- **Tests:** the file is **7 tests, none skipped**; `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **748 passed, 0 skipped** — the *2 skipped* is gone from the suite's summary for the first time. `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md`'s status line was corrected to *748 passed, none skipped* (it had said 739 passed, 2 skipped).
- **Version `0.0.288`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`; `--check` clean).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16.

### 2026-09-28 — The calendar's month title and Total Hearings follow the Month/Year wheel — COMPLETE

- **The request (Daily Cause List > the calendar dialog only):** when the user clicks the Month/Year selector, the **Total Hearing count** should update according to the month selected in the scroll wheel.
- **Why it did not.** The dialog's header — month title, year, and `Total Hearings: N` — was computed from `_focusedMonth`, which is the **day grid's** month, and the wheel's live positions lived inside the shared `MonthYearPickerColumns` state, reaching the calendar only through `onPicked`, i.e. on a tap. So while the columns were open and spinning, the header kept describing the month the grid was on.
- **The shared picker reports the spin.** `MonthYearPickerColumns` gained one **optional** `onChanged(month, year)`, fired from the very column callback that already marks the new row bold (and plays the click). Callers that leave it out — Add/Edit Case's date fields, Quick Update, the Edit History Entry sheet — behave exactly as before; nothing else about the wheel moved.
- **The header now describes the month a tap would confirm.** The dialog holds that pair as `_previewMonth` (one nullable `DateTime`, day pinned to 1) while the columns are open and derives the title, the year and the total from it; the day grid keeps its own `_focusedMonth`. The tap still commits through `_pickMonthYear`, and both committing and closing the columns unconfirmed clear the preview — so reopening the columns always starts from the month the grid is on rather than a stale spin.
- **The title follows too, by the user's choice.** The ambiguity put to them was whether the header's month/year title should move with the wheel or stay on the grid's month while only the total moved; they chose both, so the header can never show one month's title over another month's total.
- **Tests (1 new).** `test/shared/widgets/phase4_widgets_test.dart` gained *the header follows the month/year wheel before a tap*: two hearings today, seven in a March a year out, the columns spun to it with `jumpToItem`, and the header expected to read `Total Hearings: 7` with March and the spun year in its own title column — with no tap anywhere. Verified non-vacuous: with the header pinned back to `_focusedMonth` it fails at the total assertion. The file's `columnOf` scroll-controller helper was hoisted to the group so the older *straight columns* test no longer declares its own copy.
- **Tests:** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **746 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Also noted:** the unowned working-tree ARB rename that had made Active/Upcoming read *No Active Case* / *No Cases in Upcoming Dates* is no longer present — both headers read **Active Cases** / **Upcoming Cases** again, so the flagged `case_list_print_test.dart` failure is gone. Nothing in this change touched the ARB.
- **Version `0.0.287`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device look: open Daily Cause List, tap the calendar control's month, spin the month and year columns, and confirm the title and Total Hearings move with the wheel — then close without tapping and confirm the header returns to the selected day's month.

### 2026-09-28 — The Notes and Case Reference empty states sit mid-screen, semibold — COMPLETE

- **The request (the Notes & Case Reference screens only):** the two "nothing yet" lines — *No notes yet. Tap + to add one.* and *No references yet. Tap + to add one.* — should show **in the middle of the screen** in **bold** text, like the Active Cases screen.
- **Why they read as top-aligned.** Each library rendered its empty state as a one-child `ListView` whose child was a `Center`; a `Center` inside a list only centres *horizontally*, and the list's own padding pinned it to the top. The text was also `AppTypography.bodyMd` (14, regular) in `onSurfaceVariant`, not a title. The case lists' shared `CaseListEmptyState` is instead a **bare `Center`** with `Theme.of(context).textTheme.titleMedium` — `AppTypography.titleMd`, Inter 16 semibold — which is the look asked for.
- **The change is exactly that shape, in the two files.** `_NotesList.build` and `_ReferenceList.build` now return a bare `Center` + `Text(style: Theme.of(context).textTheme.titleMedium)` when their list is empty; the shell already wraps the body in an `Expanded`, so the bare `Center` owns the whole area exactly as on Active Cases. The scoped-search variant is untouched: a query that hides every row still says *No Results*.
- **Nothing else moved.** Both strings already existed in both ARBs and were not rewritten, `mockup_html_screens/` documents neither line, and no token, padding or card changed.
- **Tests (1 new).** `test/features/library_search_test.dart` gained *an empty library centres its message, semibold, like the case lists*: it loads the English ARB and asserts, for both screens, that the message's centre matches the body's centre on **both** axes and that its weight is `w600` at size 16. The file's harness now builds its `MaterialApp` with `AppTheme.lightTheme(const Locale('en'))` (the precedent in `settings_about_test.dart`) so `titleMedium` resolves to the app's own semibold face instead of Material's default `w500` — without it the guard would have pinned the wrong weight. The existing *bar above the body* test was corrected in step: with no rows there is no `ListView` any more, so it compares the bar against the body's centred empty state.
- **Flagged, deliberately not touched:** `test/features/case_list_print_test.dart` fails against the **uncommitted** working-tree ARB edit that renamed `activeCasesScreen_title` to *No Active Case* and `upcomingCasesScreen_title` to *No Cases in Upcoming Dates*. Those two keys are the Active/Upcoming **headers** and **print titles** as well as their empty-state placeholders, so both screens' headers now read as an empty message. That edit is outside this task and is not mine to change — it needs the user's decision (its own `*_empty` keys, or revert the titles).
- **Tests:** `flutter analyze` clean; full `flutter test` **744 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed** (the flagged pre-existing failure above); `python tool/record_version.py --check` clean.

### 2026-09-27 — The Settings drawer's Share works like the case screens' — COMPLETE

- **The report:** Share in the side menu works on the case screens but does nothing from Settings.
- **Cause:** the drawer only knows the callbacks it is handed — `onShare` and `onPrint` default to null and their tiles call `callback?.call()`. `cases_screen_modal.dart` supplied Share; `settings_main_screen.dart` passed only `currentRoute` and `sideNavOptions`, so its Share tile was a dead tap. The action itself also lived as a private method inside the case-screen shell, which is why the second caller had nothing to call.
- **The action is now shared, not duplicated.** New `lib/core/services/app_share_flow.dart` holds `shareAppInvite(context, ref)` — the invite text and subject from the ARB, the service call, and the two messages the user must see (the clipboard fallback and a failure) — mirroring how `core/print/print_flow.dart` owns Print. `cases_screen_modal.dart`'s private `_shareApp` is deleted and both screens call the shared action, so there is exactly one implementation.

### 2026-09-27 — Settings' notification switch is a default, not a veto — COMPLETE

- **The report:** with **Default Notification Status** off in Settings → Notifications, switching notifications on for an individual case posted nothing for it. The switch must not decide whether alerts are shown; it only sets the on/off an individual case starts from.
- **The switch was checked in three places, each of them a veto.** `buildNotificationPlan` opened with `if (!enabled) return empty`; `runHearingNotificationJob` opened with `if (!settings.notificationEnabled) return []`; and `syncSchedule` cancelled every armed alarm and returned, so no alarm existed for a per-case case to fire from. A case's own `case_notification_preferences` row already beat the master inside the planner — the blanket vetoes were what silenced it.
- **The rule now, stated once and applied everywhere:** a case alerts when its own row says on, or when it has no row and the Settings default is on. `buildNotificationPlan`'s parameter is renamed `defaultEnabled` (so it cannot be read as a gate again) and reads `preference?.enabled ?? defaultEnabled`; the job no longer returns early; and `syncSchedule` arms the slots that matter — with the default on the default slot plus each case's own time, with it off just the moments the individually-on cases need, and nothing at all when none of them is on, which is the only case that still cancels.
- **The arming rule is pure and tested.** The scheduler's arithmetic moved into `alertTimesFor({defaultEnabled, defaultMinutes, caseTimes})` in `notification_plan.dart` next to `distinctNotificationTimes`, so the "empty means cancel" decision is unit-testable without a device (a corrupt per-case time falls back to the default, as everywhere else). `notification_providers.dart` now feeds it only the rows that may alert (`row.enabled`), so a switched-off case's slot is never armed.
- **The case toggle follows the same default.** `add_edit_case_sheet.dart` (the Add/Edit form and Quick Update) set `preference?.enabled ?? true`, so a case with no row of its own opened On whatever the Settings default was — and, under the new rule, saving it would have started alerting a case the user never switched on. Both now use `preference?.enabled ?? settings.notificationEnabled`, which is what "the switch sets the individual toggles" means.
- **Nothing else moved, and no UI copy did.** The ARB and `mockup_html_screens/settings_notifications/code.html` needed no change: the label has always read *Default Notification Status*. No schema change, no new sync columns (per-user preference table), and Part A stays offline.
- **Tests:** `flutter analyze` clean. `notification_plan_test.dart`: the old *"the master switch off produces an empty plan"* became *"the Settings default off leaves a case with no choice silent"*, plus new tests for *"a case switched on individually alerts while the default is off"* and *"the Settings default decides which cases alert, not arming"* (the `alertTimesFor` rule). `task07_acceptance_test.dart`'s Quick Update test now asserts the toggle starts from the Settings default (off in that harness) and that tapping it saves `enabled: true`. Full `flutter test` **742 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.283`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device check: with Default Notification Status off, switch one case on, wait for its slot, and confirm the alert arrives (and that a case left untouched stays silent).

### 2026-09-27 — Closing a notification-opened case detail lands on the landing page, not a black screen — COMPLETE

- **The report:** the app is closed; a hearing notification is tapped and opens that case's detail view; closing the detail leaves the app on a black screen. It should return to the user's configured default landing page.
- **The root cause was the stack, not the close button.** A notification opens the detail as the stack's **only** route: `appRouterProvider` sets it as `initialLocation` on a cold launch, and `NotificationTapListener._openCase` uses `router.go(...)` on a warm tap (a `go` replaces the whole stack). The Close buttons and the delete flow called `Navigator.of(context).pop()` unconditionally, so at the root they emptied the navigator and GoRouter threw ("nothing to pop") leaving a blank surface. Popping was only ever correct for the normal path, where a case card `push`es the detail on top of its list.
- **The fix — one `_close()` helper on the screen.** It asks the navigator first: `canPop()` true pops exactly as before, and at the root it goes through the router to `AppRoutes.landingPageRoute(settingsViewModelProvider.defaultLandingPage)` — the screen the user chose in View Settings — so the app always lands somewhere real. Both Close controls (the normal header and the case-not-found body) and the post-delete pop now call it.
- **The back gesture gets the same treatment.** The Scaffold is wrapped in a `PopScope(canPop: false)` that delegates to `_close()`, the same pattern `AppShellRoot` uses for its exit confirmation, so the Android back gesture cannot blank the app either. Dialogs and sheets are unaffected — they sit on top of the screen's route.
- **`maybeOf`, not `of`.** `GoRouter.of(context)` asserts when no router is above the screen, which broke the plain-`Navigator` harness in `case_detail_test.dart`'s delete test; `GoRouter.maybeOf(context)?.go(...)` keeps that harness working while the app always has a router.
- **Verified as a real regression, not a coincidence.** The new test was run against the old unconditional pop first and failed with three exceptions from the emptied navigator, then passed once `_close()` was restored.
- **Tests:** `flutter analyze` clean; new test *"closing a notification-opened detail returns to the landing page"* in `test/core/notification_tap_test.dart` (now 4 tests there); full `flutter test` **740 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.280`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device check: cold-launch from a hearing notification, then Close and the back gesture, and confirm both land on the configured default landing page.

- **Phase 7 — verification and recording.** All 30 screens were rendered at 390 px, and every `screen.png` was regenerated. **The Preview browser could not do it in this build:** `preview_screenshot` reports `it produced no frames ... the preview webview is not being composited`, proven twice. The capture therefore ran through the machine's own Chrome headless over the DevTools protocol (a throwaway profile under `build/_shot_profile`): navigate, measure `Page.getLayoutMetrics`, resize to the measured content height, capture. Measuring needs a viewport *shorter* than the content, or the document reports the viewport; the final image carries the design's own 32 px bottom padding, so nothing is clipped. **The new PNGs are 390 px wide where the old ones were 506** — all 30 moved together, so the set stays consistent. No page logged a console error or exception on load.
- **`Texts.txt` needed no change, and that was checked rather than assumed:** all 18 mockup references in it resolve to a real folder/file, so no reference moved, and it never mentioned the four new screens. If the new mockups should be added to its Settings section, that is a one-line addition for the user.
- **Two things left open for the user, both surfaced by building the new mockups:** the screens' ARB strings are sentence case where rule 11 asks Title Case for headings and labels (`paywall_benefitsTitle`, `paywall_limitTitle`, `paywall_comingSoon`, `settingsSupport_channelsLabel`, `settingsSupport_previewLabel`, `settingsSupport_warningTitle`, `settingsSupport_replyTitle`) — the mockups record what ships today rather than silently rewriting the ARB; and `DESIGN.md` still says the rules "keep all 26 files identical" where there are now 30 screen folders. A stale one-word comment (`paywall_screen.dart`'s `_SectionHeader` still says "Uppercase section heading") is noted for the same tidy-up.

### 2026-09-26 — Settings > About: the developer card stays centred in landscape — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > About only):** on some devices and in horizontal mode the **Designed & Developed by** section sits on the left instead of staying centred. Fix it.
- **The cause was the `Stack` in `_DeveloperCard`, not the label.** A `Stack` hands its non-positioned child *loose* width (`StackFit.loose`) and then aligns it with its own `alignment`, which defaults to `topStart`. The identity `Column` has no width of its own, so it shrink-wrapped to its widest line — the single-line court name, `District & Sessions Judge Court, Narayanganj.`, ~641 px wide on a 900 px screen — and the default alignment pinned that ~641 px block to the card's left edge. Every line inside it then centred on the *block*, not on the card, so all of them read ~96 px left of the card's centre. In portrait the court line wraps to the full content width, the block becomes as wide as the card, and the bug is invisible; that is why only landscape (and wide devices) exposed it. The floating label was never the problem: it is a `Positioned(left: 0, right: 0)` + `Center`, which always spans the card.
- **The fix is one wrapper.** The card's `Padding` now sits in a `SizedBox(width: double.infinity)`, so the identity block spans the card exactly as the mockup's block-level `flex flex-col items-center` does, and the existing `CrossAxisAlignment.center` (and the court line's `TextAlign.center`) centre on the card. `_AppTitleCard` was already correct for the same reason — it has no `Stack`, so the list's tight width reaches its `Column` — and the developer card now behaves identically. A comment on the wrapper records why.
- **Measured before and after with a probe test** (since deleted): at 900x420 the identity lines sat at `centerDx=353.6` against a card centre of 450; after the fix all of them, and the floating label, report `450`. Portrait and large-text results are unchanged (210 on a 420 px screen).
- **A regression test pins it.** `settings_about_test.dart` gained *developer card keeps its identity block on the card centre*: it mounts About on a 900x700 surface and asserts the label, the name and the role all share the surface centre line. On the 420x1400 harness the same assertion cannot fail, which is exactly why the old layout passed every existing test.
- **Tests:** `settings_about_test.dart` 12 passed (1 new); `flutter analyze` clean. No ARB, dependency or string changes.
- **Version `0.0.271`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth one device check: open Settings > About in landscape and confirm the developer card's photo, name, role and court line stay on the card's centre line.

### 2026-09-26 — The Edit Profile sheet no longer overflows above the keyboard — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Account & Profile > Profile > Edit Profile form only):** tapping any text box raises the keyboard and the form reports *Bottom overflowed by 177 pixels*. Why, and fix it.
- **Why.** The form is a `showModalBottomSheet(isScrollControlled: true)` whose content is a plain `Column(mainAxisSize: min)` — a fixed-height block, not a scrollable one. It already lifts itself above the keyboard by adding `MediaQuery.viewInsets.bottom` to its bottom padding, but that padding only *moves* the form up; it does not let it get shorter. The sheet's maximum height is the screen, so once the keyboard covers the bottom the required height becomes `form + keyboard`, which is larger than the screen — and a `Column` that cannot shrink clips its last children and paints the overflow banner. The form is ~600 px tall and the keyboard is the rest, which is exactly where the 177 px comes from.
- **The fix is one wrapper.** The `Column` now sits inside a `SingleChildScrollView`, whose own size is the child's height clamped to the space available, so it scrolls instead of overflowing. On a normal screen nothing changes — the scroll view shrinks back to the content height, so the sheet is still only as tall as the form — and with the keyboard up the form is scrollable in the area above it. No widget was moved, restyled or re-ordered, and the existing keyboard padding was kept.
- **This is the pattern the repo already uses** for the same class of bug: the Language screen's body is a `SingleChildScrollView` and `language_screen_test.dart` pins that it "scrolls instead of overflowing on a short, large-text screen". The Profile form was the outlier that lacked it.
- **A regression test reproduces the exact bug.** `test/features/profile_name_test.dart` gained *the edit form scrolls clear of the keyboard*: it mounts the Profile screen on a 400x800 viewport, opens the sheet, sets a 300 px bottom `viewInsets` (the keyboard) and asserts no exception plus a scroll view inside the `BottomSheet`. **Verified non-vacuous:** before the fix it failed with `A RenderFlex overflowed by 186 pixels on the bottom` (the test viewport's version of the reported 177 px), and it passes after.
- **Tests:** the full suite is **738 passed, 2 skipped** (1 new); `flutter analyze` clean. No ARB or dependency changes.
- **Version `0.0.270`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth one device check: open Edit Profile and tap the last field (Mobile No) so the keyboard covers the Save button, then scroll to reach it.

### 2026-09-26 — The bottom navigation changes screens with no motion at all — COMPLETE

- **The request (the screen change the bottom nav performs only):** the transition shown when the user changes screen from the bottom navigation was making some users feel unwell, so it must be removed — no transition at all while the screen changes. Nothing else in the app was to change.
- **The motion was GoRouter's default page transition, not anything the app wrote.** The bottom bar calls `context.go(route)`, and each tab's `GoRoute` was declared with a plain `builder:`, so GoRouter wrapped it in the platform's default page — on Android the zoom-and-fade that was the complaint. There was no `pageTransitionsTheme` and no custom page anywhere in the app, so there was nothing to tune: the eight tab routes are now declared with `pageBuilder:` returning a **`NoTransitionPage`**, which has no animation to run.
- **Scope is the eight screens a tab can open** — Daily Cause List, All Cases, Active, Upcoming, Overdue, Disposed, Notes and Case Reference — declared in `lib/core/router/app_router.dart`. Every other route (case detail, Settings and its children, login/signup/paywall/checkout) keeps the app's normal transition, because the `builder:` form was left untouched everywhere else.
- **One consequence, recorded rather than hidden:** GoRouter chooses the transition from the *route*, not from what was tapped, so those eight screens are also instant when the drawer opens one of them or when All Cases' summary cards push Active or Disposed. Narrowing that to the bottom nav alone would mean duplicating the routes or threading a transient "came from the bar" flag, which is far more machinery than the requirement is worth; the side effect is consistent (a case screen appearing instantly) rather than surprising.
- **A regression guard was added, because nothing else would notice.** `test/core/screen_transitions_test.dart` reads the router source and pins that every screen the bottom bar can open is declared with `NoTransitionPage` and is *not* back on the animated `builder:`. It takes the tab list from `bottomNavTabDefs`, so adding a tab fails the guard until its route is brought in line. **Verified non-vacuous:** reverting Notes to `builder:` was made to fail the guard, then restored. This follows the repo's existing source-guard pattern (`notification_local_only_test.dart`) for rules that no widget test can assert — a transition is motion, not behaviour.
- **Tests:** the full suite is **~731 passed, 2 skipped** (2 new in `screen_transitions_test.dart`); `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Version `0.0.267`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No dependency changes and no UI text, so no ARB work.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device look at the three ways into these screens: a bottom-bar tap, a drawer item, and the All Cases summary card — all three should appear with no motion.

### 2026-09-26 — The Android launcher icon draws the logo large, not as a shrunken legacy icon — COMPLETE

- **The request (the app logo only):** the logo the Android app drawer shows is small — make it as big as it can be so the logo and the *Legal Case Diary* text are clearly seen, without the logo being cut.
- **The cause was the icon *type*, not the artwork.** With only a plain `ic_launcher.png`, Android 8+ gives the icon the **legacy treatment** — it shrinks the art onto a plate, which is the "small" the user saw. An **adaptive icon** is drawn at full size and masked by the launcher itself, so that is the fix; the artwork was not touched.
- **The spec, checked rather than recalled.** The adaptive canvas is **108 dp**, masked to at most **72 dp**, with a 66 dp recommended safe circle (Android docs). The logo is a page-filling "book cover" (its content spans the whole image vertically), so it is the **diagonal** that decides cropping: a centred logo whose diagonal stays under the 72 dp mask is never clipped.
- **The size was chosen with the numbers, not by eye.** `assets/icons/app_logo.png` is the foreground on a **white** plate (`#FFFFFF`), which the navy book sits on (its own white page edge blends into it). **Inset 22%** is set at the user's request, past the fully-safe size: the canvas is 108 dp and may be masked to 72 dp (36 dp radius), and at 22% the farthest logo pixel lies **37.58 dp** from the centre — **1.58 dp (~6 px at xxxhdpi) beyond the rim**, so the outermost corner of the cover's page edge is trimmed slightly. The steps above it: 23% is 36.23 dp (sub-pixel past the rim) and **24% is the largest inset at which *every* pixel stays inside (34.89 dp, 25% being 33.55 dp)** — the value to return to for zero trimming. The first pass at **12%** failed the "must not be cut" requirement outright: it drew the logo bigger (76% of the canvas) but put its farthest pixel at **51.0 dp**, so the cover's outer corners and its white page-edge strokes were clipped well inside the mask.
- **The background must be opaque — a transparent one renders as a black plate.** Asked for a transparent background, the next attempt set `adaptive_icon_background: "#00000000"`; installed on the device, the launcher drew a **black** area around the book, where the original plain-PNG icon (which has no background layer at all) had shown none. This is the adaptive-icon spec, not the phone: Android Developers' *Implementing Adaptive Icons* states the layers are 108 dp and that **"background drawables must be opaque whilst foregrounds can contain transparency"**, and `flutter_launcher_icons` carries it as a still-open request (#535, where `#00000000` is reported as not working). **A transparent adaptive-icon background is not something this format can carry**, so the only levers are the plate colour and the inset. The plate is **white** `#FFFFFF` (the alternative offered was the cover's own navy `#223B59`, which reads slightly larger because it merges with the cover; the user chose white). **Baking the logo into a plain PNG was considered and rejected:** the icon this task started from *was* a plain PNG, and a non-adaptive icon is exactly what makes the launcher wrap the artwork in its own background and scale it down to about two-thirds — the small icon the task set out to fix.
- **Generated by the project's own tool**, so it can be regenerated: `dart run flutter_launcher_icons` now writes `mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` (background → `@color/ic_launcher_background`, foreground → `@drawable/ic_launcher_foreground` with `android:inset="22%"`), `values/colors.xml` (`ic_launcher_background` = `#FFFFFF`, opaque), and `drawable-{m,h,xh,xxh,xxx}dpi/ic_launcher_foreground.png` at 108/162/216/324/432 px. The existing legacy `mipmap-*/ic_launcher.png` files came out **byte-identical** and are left for API < 26; `assets/icons/app_logo.png` itself is unchanged, so the About screen is unaffected.
- **Two out-of-scope files were touched and reverted.** The generator also rewrote `ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` and `web/manifest.json`; `git diff` showed **line-ending-only** changes, so both were restored, leaving the diff to the Android icon and `pubspec.yaml`. iOS and web keep their current icons.
- **`flutter analyze` clean** (no Dart changed). The adaptive foreground PNGs were verified to be the correct 108–432 px canvases.
- **Version `0.0.266`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No dependency changes.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. **This one needs a real device:** only a launcher can show the masked result, so reinstall the app and look at the drawer in the phone's own shape (circle on Pixel, squircle on Samsung). 22% is set: the logo is as large as asked, at the cost of about 6 px of the cover's outermost page-edge corner at xxxhdpi; 24% is the value for zero trimming. The plate colour (`adaptive_icon_background`) is the only other lever, and it has to be a real colour.

### 2026-09-26 — The Courts sheet gains a District column, and lower courts group by it — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Backup & Restore > Template File > Court sheet only):** keep the AD/HCD court names the same; add a **District** column carrying the district so the user can filter courts by it; and sort the lower courts by **District & Court Categories**.
- **Three decisions were asked before any code,** because the request left them open: the lower courts group by **District first, then court category** (not by division); AD/HCD keep their names, stay at the top and get a **blank** District cell (they are not district courts); and the District cell shows the **district's displayed name** in the current app language, matching how the Court Category column already reads.

Since the column is blank for AD/HCD, it is also harmless if a user restores the edited template (the sheet is not read at all).
- **The grouping is sorted in Dart, not SQL.** `_courtsSql` now returns `district`, `cc.order_index` and `c.order_index`, and `_referenceData` sorts the rows itself so the district names come out **alphabetical in whichever language the sheet was generated in** — an SQL `ORDER BY c.district` would have sorted the stable ids, which is wrong under বাংলা. Rows with no district (AD/HCD) sort first in their own category order, and within a district the old category → order → name sequence decides.
- **The label is resolved by the caller, like the category label already is.** `buildExcelTemplateWorkbook` gained a `districtLabel` callback parallel to `categoryLabel`; the screen passes `context.l10n.seedLabel(districtId, fallbackEn: districtId)`, so a district is labelled exactly as the app labels it elsewhere and an unknown id reads as itself.

- **Tests (1 new in `test/features/backup_repository_test.dart`, 1 pinned header added to the existing template test; suite 729 passed, 2 skipped, `flutter analyze` clean).** The new test seeds an AD/HCD court and three lower courts across two districts, then asserts the District column carries the district's name (not its id) and that AD/HCD lead, that one district's courts sit together, and that within a district they follow court-category order. The template test now also asserts the new `District` header.
