### 2026-09-20 — Backup to Google Drive makes a backup, then offers to share it — COMPLETE

- **The request:** the Google Drive button said “Cloud backup is not available yet”, which was not useful. It should make a local backup exactly like Backup to Local Storage and then offer to share that file with Drive through the same share sheet the side navigation's Share action uses.
- **It does exactly that, and nothing pretends.** Part A is offline, so the button cannot upload; it writes the *same* encrypted backup the local action writes — same passphrase window, same `exportSnapshot` path, same platform Save-to dialog — and only then offers the share sheet, where Google Drive is one of the targets. The three design points the mockup does not cover were asked first: the file goes through the same Save-to dialog (so the kept file is the shared file and the status card stays truthful), the offer is a window with Send / Not now, and the button keeps its mockup label.
- **One shared export, two endings.** `_exportBackup(sink)` now holds the passphrase window, the progress window, `exportSnapshot` and the archive record, so the local and Drive actions differ only in their sink and what happens afterwards. It returns null when nothing was written (cancelled dialog or a reported failure), so the Drive flow never offers to send a file that does not exist.
- **The share seam.** `AppShareService` — still the only file in the app allowed to import `share_plus` — gained `shareFile`, which opens that very sheet with the backup attached. Unlike `shareApp` it has **no clipboard fallback**: a clipboard holds text, so a sheet the platform cannot open is reported as a failure and said out loud instead of silently doing nothing.
- **A real platform obstacle, solved in the sink.** Android's Save-to dialog usually returns a `content://` URI, and no share sheet can attach one. `ShareableBackupSink` (in `backup_file_gateway.dart`) checks whether the chosen location is a real file: if it is, that file is shared as it is with no copy; if it is not, the same ciphertext is written to a temporary file in the app's cache and that copy is shared. The copy is a **courier, not the backup** — `savedPath` is what the app records and what the window names, so the status card can never point at something the platform may clear at any time.
- **Declining is not failing.** “Not now” leaves the file and its record exactly as they were; a cancelled passphrase window writes and shares nothing; a dismissed Save-to dialog offers no share at all. The new `showBackupShareDialog` is the only window in this flow that cannot affect the data, because by the time it appears the backup is already written and recorded.
- **Two test-only obstacles, fixed at the source rather than worked around.** (1) Real async file I/O never completes under `testWidgets`' fake clock, so the courier copy is written **synchronously** — a one-shot write of bytes already in memory, happening under the export's progress window, which keeps the export one uninterrupted sequence — and the tests read files synchronously too. (2) `path_provider` has no plugin on a test host, so the tests mock its channel with a real temporary directory, created and removed per test.
- **Tests (15 → 20 in `backup_restore_screen_test.dart`).** The Drive action writing and recording the same encrypted container, the offer naming the saved file with nothing shared before Send, the content-URI fallback proving the sheet receives a byte-for-byte copy of what was written, a real saved file shared with no copy, Not now keeping the backup, a cancelled passphrase writing and sharing nothing, a sheet that cannot open reported while the backup stays recorded, and a dismissed save dialog offering no share. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **557 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.231`.** No new dependency (`share_plus`, `path_provider` and `file_picker` were already present). `settingsBackupRestore_enginePending` is gone, replaced by six localized strings in both languages.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 15 Phase 5 (paywall stub + 50-case gating verification) next, then Phase 6 (monthly nudge). Worth a device run: the share sheet and the `content://` fallback are platform behaviour no widget test can exercise.

### 2026-09-20 — Task 15 Phase 4: the Backup & Restore screen — COMPLETE

- **The screen is the mockup, and only the mockup.** `lib/features/settings/presentation/backup_restore_screen.dart` reproduces `mockup_html_screens/settings_backupRestore/code.html` inside Task 12's sub-page window: one card with the three actions (Backup to Local Storage, Backup to Google Drive, Restore from backup — the last one one step further down, as the mockup's extra `pt-sm` asks), the encrypted-backup note and its offline note, the five-interval automatic-backup radio card, and the last-backup status card. It is registered on the `settingsBackupRestore` route.
- **The platform seam is one file.** `lib/features/settings/data/backup_file_gateway.dart` is **the only file in the app that imports `file_picker`** (the same boundary pattern as `print_service.dart`), and it implements `BackupArchiveSink` as well, so `BackupRepository.exportSnapshot` stays the single export path of Sync Plan §5 and only the destination changes. A dismissed Save-to dialog throws a typed `BackupSaveCancelledException`: the screen reports nothing, and nothing was recorded, because the archive is written only after a successful write.
- **Backup.** Passphrase + confirmation in one window that states the passphrase is never stored anywhere, then `exportSnapshot` behind a **non-dismissible** progress window — the key derivation is ~1 s on purpose and must not look like a freeze — then the platform's save dialog, then the saved path in a snack bar and in the status card.
- **Restore.** Pick a file → passphrase (an empty one opens a legacy plaintext backup, and the dialog says so) → **decrypt and authenticate before anything else** → `hasExistingData()` → Replace-or-Cancel window → one transaction → a completion window → `databaseProvider` invalidated, which is the app's own reload lever, so every screen rebuilds from the restored data.
- **A restore never merges silently.** The confirmation shows the file's name, its export time, the app version that wrote it and the number of records it carries, plus the plain warning that the replacement cannot be undone. Cancel leaves the database exactly as it was (a test counts the rows to prove it).
- **Every refusal says why, in one sentence.** `_restoreFailureMessage` maps the engine's six typed failures to six localized strings in both languages (`errorWrongSecret`, `errorDamaged`, `errorUnsupportedVersion`, `errorInvalidFile`, `errorConflict`, `errorImportFailed`), so a JPEG, a newer file, a tampered file and a wrong passphrase are four different messages rather than one generic failure.
- **The mockup defines no dialogs at all** — the four decisions it left open were asked, not guessed (dialog design and placement, passphrase wording/storage claim, the Replace-or-Cancel content, and what the status card shows). `lib/features/settings/presentation/backup_dialogs.dart` implements them in the app's established window style: a titled `AlertDialog`, centred actions, the shared Cancel label, the 112x48 action size the Delete and Exit windows use, and a show/hide obscured field. `runWithBackupProgress` always pops its window, including on failure, so an error can never leave the screen covered by a spinner.
- **Metadata lives outside the database, deliberately.** `lib/features/settings/data/backup_metadata_store.dart` keeps the last successful backup's timestamp and path and the chosen interval in `SharedPreferences` (read through `backupMetadataProvider`): the database is exactly what a restore replaces, so it cannot be the place that reports on the backup. Task 12 left the schedule in memory and handed its persistence to Task 15; choosing an interval only *stores* it — nothing runs automatically until Part B's background executor exists, and the UI does not claim otherwise.
- **Cloud backup is honest, not broken.** The Google Drive action reports “cloud backup is not available yet” instead of throwing or pretending; Part A has no network, and a test reads the sources to keep it that way (no supabase, http, dio or websocket import in any Task 15 file).
- **One test-only seam, found by a hang.** PBKDF2 yields to the event loop between batches, and the fake clock of `testWidgets` never advances those pauses, so a real export inside a widget test never completed. The screen now reads its iteration count from `backupKdfIterationsProvider`: production keeps the full 210k stretch (Phase 2's test still asserts it) while widget tests lower it — every other part of the export stays real, including the file the test then decrypts.
- **Tests (15 new).** `backup_restore_screen_test.dart`: the mockup's sections and controls, the honest status card before any backup, the stored date rendered in the app's Bangladesh timezone, a stored schedule, the passphrase asked twice before a **real encrypted file** is written and then decrypted by the test, a cancelled passphrase window exporting nothing, a dismissed save dialog recording nothing, a failed export reported, restoring into an empty database with no Replace window, restoring over existing data with Cancel leaving the rows intact and Replace importing, a wrong passphrase, a JPEG, a picker that throws and the cloud notice each reported with the right sentence and nothing changed, plus two source guards (only the gateway imports `file_picker`; the flow stays offline). `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **552 passed, 2 skipped**. New `file_picker` dependency.
- **Version `0.0.230`.**
- **Resume point:** Task 15 Phase 5 — the paywall stub and the 50-case free-tier gating verification (the task file's Phase 5). Phase 6 is the monthly nudge banner, which reads `lastSuccessfulBackupAtKey` / `scheduleDays` from `backup_metadata_store.dart`. The screen itself is done; a device run should confirm the `file_picker` round trip on real Android storage, which no test can cover (the picker is a platform channel).

### 2026-09-20 — Task 15 Phase 3: restore / import with validation — COMPLETE

- **The order of the trust chain is the design.** New `lib/core/backup/backup_restore.dart` (pure Dart) opens a file as: read → **decrypt and authenticate** → verify the envelope's checksum → refuse a newer version → run the per-version adapters → hand back a validated payload. Nothing in it touches the database, so a file that fails any step has provably imported nothing.
- **One typed failure per outcome,** for Phase 4 to localize: `invalidFile`, `unsupportedVersion`, `wrongSecretOrTampered`, `damagedFile`, `conflictNeedsConfirmation`, `importFailed`. A container whose header this build cannot use is `invalidFile`; a decrypted plaintext that disagrees with its recorded checksum is `damagedFile` (the tag already proved the passphrase, so it is damage, not a wrong secret).
- **Legacy plaintext files still restore.** A `.json` envelope written before encryption (0.0.227 and earlier) is sniffed by the absence of the container marker and restored with `kind: legacyPlaintext`, so an existing user's only copy is not reported as corrupt. Phase 4 should still say it was never encrypted.
- **The version seam is real, not a placeholder.** `migrateBackupPayload` applies one registered adapter per schema step (`backupPayloadAdapters`, empty at v1) and **refuses** a missing step instead of guessing, so a future payload change cannot break an old backup and an unknown shape is never imported half-upgraded.
- **Import is one transaction.** `BackupRepository.restoreSnapshot` validates the payload first — the table snapshot must exist, every table name must be one of the 20 portable tables, every column name must match the schema's snake_case shape *before* it is interpolated into the `INSERT`, and every value must be a SQLite scalar — then deletes child-before-parent and inserts parent-before-child. Any failure rolls the whole transaction back, so a restore can never leave a half-replaced database.
- **Collision asks, never merges.** A database that already holds anything — including a lone tombstone or a pending `sync_queue` entry — returns `conflictNeedsConfirmation` **with nothing written**; the caller asks Replace vs Cancel and calls again with `replaceExisting: true` to proceed. Rows keep their original ids and sync columns, so a Part B premium push of restored data is unaffected (Sync Plan §5).
- **A security detail worth knowing:** the importer builds its `INSERT` from the *file's* column names, so column names are whitelisted against a snake_case pattern first — a crafted backup naming `id") VALUES (1); DROP TABLE notes; --` is refused before any SQL runs, and a test pins that every column of every table passes the pattern so the whitelist cannot drift.
- **No backup can contain its own `archive.*` record** (the export writes the file and records the archive afterwards, so a failed write can never claim "complete"). A restore therefore reproduces the archive metadata the file was written *with* — the round-trip test asserts exactly that by comparing every other table verbatim and the archive record against its pre-export value.
- **Tests (24 new).** `backup_restore_test.dart` (12): the encrypted and legacy openings, a wrong secret, a tampered ciphertext/header/container-checksum, newer container and payload versions, unreadable files, the adapter chain including its missing-step refusal, and the empty adapter registry. `backup_restore_repository_test.dart` (12): the full **export → wipe → import** round trip compared table by table (ids, sync columns, tombstones, queue payloads, row counts all identical), a legacy file importing, replace-into-non-empty with the previous data provably gone, a failed insert and a failed replace both rolling back completely, damaged payloads (unknown table, SQL-injecting column name, nested value, empty row, missing table snapshot) refused before any SQL runs, a newer schema version refused with the target untouched, `hasExistingData` counting a queued write and a lone tombstone, and the column-name self-check. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **537 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.229`.** No new dependency.
- **Resume point:** Task 15 Phase 4 — the **Backup & Restore screen** (`features/settings/presentation/backup_restore_screen.dart`) matching `mockup_html_screens/settings_backupRestore/code.html` exactly, inside Task 12's `settings_sub_page_modal` shell: wire **Backup to Local Storage** to `exportSnapshot` behind a `file_picker`-backed `BackupArchiveSink` (showing the saved path and progress — the export is ~1 s and must not block a frame) and **Restore from backup** to picker → passphrase dialog → `openBackupFile` → `hasExistingData()`/`conflictNeedsConfirmation` Replace-or-Cancel dialog → `restoreSnapshot`, then show the outcome; store last-backup metadata for the status card. **Note the mockup defines no restore or Replace/Cancel dialog** — the task file prescribes Replace vs Cancel, so those dialogs need a Justice Ledger-styled design (confirm with the user before building, and localize every new string in both ARBs). Then Phase 5 (paywall stub + 50-case gating) and Phase 6 (monthly nudge banner).

### 2026-09-20 — Task 15 Phase 2: authenticated-encrypted export — COMPLETE

- **The decision the task file reserved was asked, not guessed.** The user chose a **passphrase-derived key** over a silent device key, so a backup written before a reinstall, or on the old phone, can still be opened — and chose the `.lcbak` extension, because the file is no longer readable JSON.
- **What was built.** `lib/core/backup/backup_cipher.dart` is **pure Dart** (no Flutter, Drift or plugin import) and owns the container §12.3.1 specifies: `format` + `format_version`, the KDF parameters (`pbkdf2-hmac-sha256`, its iteration count, a per-export 16-byte salt), the cipher (`aes-256-gcm`), a per-export 12-byte nonce, the ciphertext, the authentication tag and a SHA-256 checksum of the plaintext envelope. The plaintext is the Phase 1 envelope, so restore decrypts first and only then decodes the versioned document inside — one format, now sealed.
- **The header is authenticated, and decryption fails closed.** Everything except the ciphertext, tag and checksum is the AEAD's associated data, so a swapped `format_version`, an iteration count turned down to 1 or an edited `app_version`/`created_at` fails the tag as surely as a flipped ciphertext byte. One typed `BackupDecryptionException` carries four reasons — `malformed`, `unsupportedVersion`, `wrongSecretOrTampered`, `checksumMismatch` — and **no bytes come back for any of them**, so Phase 3 can report a clear error and there is nothing half-decrypted to import.
- **Key hygiene.** The secret's bytes are zeroed immediately after the KDF, the derived key is destroyed after the cipher call (`SecretKeyData(..., overwriteWhenDestroyed: true)`), and no exception message, `toString()` or `describe()` output can carry the secret or key material — a test asserts exactly that. The KDF runs at 210k iterations (~1 s on the host), which is why the file records its own iteration count: a later build can raise it without breaking old backups, and if it ever janks the screen the upgrade path (worker isolate or `cryptography_flutter`) needs no format change.
- **One export path, now encrypted end to end.** `BackupRepository.exportSnapshot` takes the recovery secret as a required argument and always writes the container, so no caller can accidentally produce a plaintext export; the archive's path/checksum/state are still recorded only after the sink reports success, and the receipt's checksum is the SHA-256 of the encrypted file actually written.
- **Tests (24 new).** New `backup_cipher_test.dart` (14): the round-trip with the right secret at the full default iteration count, the versioned header and its public parameters, fresh salt/nonce per call, byte-for-byte reproducibility with injected randomness, the empty-secret guard, and the fail-closed rules — wrong secret, tampered ciphertext/tag/nonce/salt, weakened KDF parameters, an altered authenticated header field, a newer container version, a mismatched checksum and a malformed file (bad JSON, missing field, wrong type, invalid base64, a plain envelope, an unknown cipher/KDF) — plus one test that neither the secret nor the payload text appears in the written bytes. `backup_repository_test.dart` (10) now decrypts the file it recorded, reads the envelope inside it, and pins that the exported `.lcbak` contains no case number, party name, `schema_version` or passphrase while a wrong passphrase cannot open it. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **513 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.228`.** New `cryptography` dependency (AES-GCM + PBKDF2, pure Dart).
- **Resume point:** Task 15 Phase 3 — restore/import with validation: `EncryptedBackup.decode` + `decryptBackup` first (a `malformed`/`unsupportedVersion`/`wrongSecretOrTampered`/`checksumMismatch` reason maps to its own localized error), then `BackupEnvelope.fromJson` with per-version adapters, then a single Drift transaction that rolls back completely on failure, with Replace-or-Cancel for a non-empty database. Note the legacy case: a plaintext `.json` envelope from an older build (0.0.227 and earlier) is not a container, so Phase 3 should sniff it and offer it as a legacy import rather than report corruption. Then Phase 4 (screen), Phase 5 (paywall stub + gating verification) and Phase 6 (monthly nudge banner).

### 2026-09-20 — Task 15 Phase 1: versioned JSON backup format + export engine — COMPLETE

- **What was built.** Two new modules and one DAO. `lib/core/backup/backup_format.dart` is **pure Dart** — no Flutter, Drift or plugin import — and owns the canonical envelope: `schema_version`, `app_version`, `exported_at`, a SHA-256 `checksum` over the payload, and the payload itself. `BackupEnvelope.create` computes the checksum; `decode`/`fromJson` refuse a malformed file with a `FormatException` and a **newer** file with a typed `UnsupportedBackupVersionException` (so Phase 3 can add per-version adapters, and an unknown future format is never half-imported); `verifyChecksum()` re-checks the payload. `lib/sync/sync_metadata_dao.dart` is the small key/value accessor the `sync_metadata` table never had.
- **Why raw rows.** `lib/features/settings/data/backup_repository.dart` snapshots every portable table as **raw rows** (`SELECT *`) instead of through the generated data classes. That is the decision the whole format rests on: it preserves original ids, the global sync columns (`version`, `updated_at`, `is_deleted`, `synced_at`, `last_modified_by`), soft-delete tombstones and the `sync_queue` payloads **verbatim**, with no per-table serializer that can silently drift out of date. A new FK-safe parent→child `backupTableOrder` lists all 20 tables (push/pull domain, pull-only caches, per-user preferences, local operational), and a test asserts it covers **every** table in `AppDatabase` — so adding a table without adding it here fails the build rather than dropping data from every future backup.
- **One format, one path.** `exportSnapshot` is the single export path: snapshot → serialize → hand the bytes to an injectable `BackupArchiveSink` → record. Sync Plan §5 requires the manual Backup to Local Storage, the Part B logout auto-export and the extended-offline safety archive to be byte-for-byte the same format, so they all funnel through this one method; the sink is the only thing that changes (Phase 4 supplies the `file_picker` sink).
- **Archive state recorded only after a successful write.** `archive.path`, `archive.checksum` and `archive.state` (the keys the `sync_metadata` table's own docs already name) are written **after** the sink reports success, so a failed write can never leave a "complete" archive behind — pinned by a failing-sink test. Nonportable secure credentials (the database key, secure-storage material) live outside the database and are excluded by construction; restored rows keep `last_modified_by` so a later premium push is unaffected.
- **Tests (20 new).** `backup_format_test.dart` (11): checksum computation, the encode→decode round-trip still verifying, tamper detection failing the checksum, the `tables` getter, and the version rules (current accepted, newer typed-refused, missing/invalid refused, non-object refused). `backup_repository_test.dart` (9): the export list covers every `AppDatabase` table, `collectTables` returns raw snake_case rows, ids + sync columns + both tombstones survive, pending `sync_queue` rows (with payloads) are included, a soft-deleted-only note still exports, `exportSnapshot` writes a self-verifying envelope and records the archive, no archive after a failed write or before the first export, and the deterministic `legal-case-diary-backup-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.json` name. New `crypto` dependency (SHA-256). `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **498 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.227`.**
- **Resume point:** Task 15 Phase 2 — wrap the envelope in the authenticated-encrypted container (versioned header, KDF parameters, nonce, ciphertext, auth tag, checksum) with a **passphrase**-derived AES-256-GCM key via the new `cryptography` dependency, plus the round-trip/wrong-secret/tamper tests. Then Phase 3 (restore), Phase 4 (screen), Phase 5 (paywall stub + gating verification) and Phase 6 (monthly nudge banner).

### 2026-09-20 — Case Detail header actions: icon-only, one evenly spaced line — COMPLETE

- **The request:** the Delete / Edit / Print / Close row on Case Detail wrapped onto two lines. Remove the text labels, show only the icons, and put the four buttons one line at an even distance.
- **Why it wrapped:** the row was a `Wrap` of `FilledButton.icon`s, so each label's width (and its wrap to a second line) decided the layout — and the labels are longer in Bengali than in English.
- **What it is now:** a `Row` of four equal `Expanded` buttons with equal `SizedBox` gaps, so the four icons sit on one line at even centres. Scaffold-wide margins are unchanged.
- **The labels are not lost.** `_headerButton` gained an `iconOnly` flag; in that mode it renders a plain `FilledButton` wrapped in a `Tooltip(message: label)`, so the label survives as the tooltip and the semantics name, and a long-press still says what the icon does. The 'case not found' fallback keeps its labelled Close button.

The old test asserted the `Edit` label was on screen, which is exactly what this change removes. `case_detail_test.dart` only used the keys, so it was untouched. `flutter analyze` clean; **479 passed, 2 skipped**.

### 2026-09-20 — Print type enlarged: a field's value is 14 pt, and the two renderers now share one scale — COMPLETE

- **The request:** the field values on a printout (Petitioner, Respondent, …) should read at 14 pt, with the rest of the type scaled to match and the page still fitting A4 ("field values like Petitioner, Respondent" maps to `PrintStyle.value`, which was the 8.5 pt / 9.5 pt row in the size table).
- **One scale, not two.** The native (Latin-only) and raster (Bengali) renderers had drifted apart — 18 vs 15 title, 9.5 vs 8.5 value, 8 vs 7 label — so the same document printed visibly smaller in Bengali. Both now use: title 25, subtitle 13, group heading 18, entry heading 16, **field value 14**, field label 12, header fact 13, footer 12. That resolves the drift flagged when the sizes were first asked about.
- **The label column had to grow with the label.** A fixed 92 pt column holding the old 7 pt label would wrap `Respondent's Lead Counsel` and `Remarks or Case Summary` at 12 pt, so `labelColumnWidth` is 158 (92 × 12/7) and the native field/meta widths are 158/126 — the longest labels still fit one line.
- **The footer reservation had to grow too.** A 12 pt footer at a 1.4 line height plus its 4 pt gap is 20.8 pt, past the old `footerHeight` of 20; it is now 24, so the paginator never under-reserves and the page cannot overrun.
- **Fit was measured, not assumed.** A real 40-entry Bengali cause list (seven fields each) rendered through the raster path makes **11 A4 pages at 3.6 entries per page with no overflow** — the paginator measures the very blocks it draws, so larger type costs entries per page rather than spilling off the sheet. A 40-case list is thus ~11 sheets; if that is too many, the title/heading sizes (25/18) are the cheapest to trim.
- **Tests.** `flutter analyze` clean; **479 passed, 2 skipped** (no new tests: the existing Bengali-dialog capture, the opacity check and the 200-row pagination test all still pass at the new sizes).
- **Version `0.0.224`.**
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 14 remains device-only for the manual print preview and the four contact-launch paths; next task in order `docs/tasks/15_backup_restore_and_free_tier_gating.md`.

### 2026-09-20 — The double yellow underline on a print, found at last: the fallback text style — COMPLETE

- **The attached Bengali print finally identified the "doubled, tinted line".** 0.0.221 and 0.0.222 chased it through the line rules and the alpha mask and concluded the app drew no yellow at all — the file was read back byte by byte this time, and it *does*: the embedded page image carries **20,261 pixels of pure `#FFFF00` blended with white**, in two thin lines under *every* text line of the Case Detail sheet. That is a yellow **double underline**, not a rule, and no rule, alpha mask or colour transform could produce it.
- **The source is `MaterialApp`'s fallback text style, which the capture inherits.** The print page is painted **outside any `Material`** — the capture is a sibling of the progress dialog, not a child of it — so the ambient `DefaultTextStyle` is `_errorTextStyle`: red, `monospace`, 48 px, and decorated with a double underline in `#FFFF00`. `TextStyle.merge` copies `decoration`, `decorationColor` and `decorationStyle` from the *other* style even when it leaves them unset, so a `PrintStyle` that declared no decoration kept the fallback's — on every line, in both languages, on the raster path (which is the only path a Bengali document takes).
- **The fix is one property per style: `inherit: false`.** Every `PrintStyle` text style now ignores the ambient style outright, so what is printed is exactly the typography declared in `print_widgets.dart` and no theme, dark mode or fallback style can reach the page. This is what the file already claimed ("the type is fixed rather than themed"), now made literally true. The 0.0.221/0.0.222 work — no rules, opaque white paper — stands unchanged.
- **Reproduced on the host, then pinned.** A capture mounted under `MaterialApp` with no `Material` ancestor (the arrangement the dialog builds) produced `(255,255,210)` and `(255,255,151)` yellow lines; with the fix the same capture is white, ink and muted only. The new test in `print_page_capture_test.dart` paints the page under a verbatim copy of the fallback style and asserts every rendered line resolves to `decoration == null` — it **fails against the old styles** (verified by reverting them). 479 passed, 2 skipped; `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Version `0.0.223`.**
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 14 remains device-only for the manual print preview and the four contact-launch paths; next task in order `docs/tasks/15_backup_restore_and_free_tier_gating.md`.

### 2026-09-19 — No lines on any printed page, and a page is white paper — COMPLETE

- **The reported "double yellow underline under each line" on a Bengali print.** The app does not draw yellow: dumping every pixel of a captured Bengali page shows ink (`#0F172A`), muted (`#475569`) and white — no yellow, no green, nothing. The *lines*, however, were real, so they are gone.
- **The rules are gone from every printed page** — the divider under the header, the hairline that closed each entry and the rule above the page footer — in the native renderer and in the raster widget alike. The 0.0.221 `PrintDocument.showRules` flag went with them: with nothing to gate, the flag was deleted rather than left behind. A printed page now draws text and nothing else, in both languages.
- **The Bengali page was 99% transparent, which is the real find.** A Bengali document takes the raster path, and that page was captured on a transparent background: **1,540,812 of its 1,654,028 pixels had alpha 0**. The `pdf` package gives every raster image an alpha mask, so the Bengali print was the only document carrying a page-sized mask whose content was mostly "transparent" — the shape a viewer or a print driver can decompose into a *doubled, tinted* line. The captured page is now opaque white paper (`PrintStyle.page`), so the mask is uniformly opaque; only the outermost half-pixel the raster grid clips stays part-transparent (2,618 px, asserted under 1%).
- **Tests (478 passed, 2 skipped; `flutter analyze` clean).** The rule removal is pinned at both renderers: the native dump has no grey300/grey400 stroke on a list page *or* the detail sheet while still finding the footer's grey600 and the labels' grey700 (so the check cannot pass vacuously), a page's blocks contain no `ColoredBox` at all, and the two screen suites still pass. The opaque page is pinned by decoding a captured Bengali page (white owns over half the pixels, transparency under 1%) plus the assembled PDF, which still carries its now-uniform mask.
- **Version `0.0.222`.**
- **Resume point:** still device-only for Task 14 — a Bengali print on the Redmi to confirm the lines are gone, then the print preview and the four contact-launch paths. Next task in order: `docs/tasks/15_backup_restore_and_free_tier_gating.md`.

### 2026-09-19 — Bug fix: the Bengali print path never laid out — COMPLETE

- **The report:** printing works in English and fails in Bengali, which lands on "প্রিন্ট তৈরি হচ্ছে" and then "প্রিন্ট করা যায়নি". Reproduced on the host in one test, so no device was needed.
- **Bengali is the only path that draws the raster capture.** In a Bengali UI even a Latin-only list carries Bengali in its footer and page label, so `needsRaster` is always true there and every print goes through `captureDocumentInDialog` → `PrintPageCapture`. English never touches it, which is exactly why one language worked.
- **The fault was layout, not the PDF.** The progress dialog parents the capture with `Positioned(left: -2000, top: 0)`; with no `top`/`bottom` that hands the child **unbounded** constraints. The capture's root `Stack` sizes itself to `constraints.biggest` and asserts `size.isFinite` — so the subtree never laid out, `_measureBlocks` threw on its very first `child.size`, and `print_flow.dart` swallowed the throw into `PrintOutcome.failed`, i.e. the failure card.
- **Why the tests missed it.** The Phase 2 suite mounts `PrintPageCapture` directly under a `Scaffold`, where the parent supplies tight constraints — the one arrangement the dialog does not produce. The new suite drives the dialog itself.
- **The fix bounds only what the parent left unbounded.** `LimitedBox` gives the capture a finite box where the parent offered none and passes a bounded parent through untouched, so the page is never clamped to the parent's height and every block keeps the natural height the paginator measures (a bare `Transform.translate` or `OverflowBox` was tried first and each broke a different placement).
- **Two hardenings in the same file.** A capture failure now goes to the log with its stack instead of vanishing into an outcome enum, and a block measurement that finds nothing laid out throws "The print blocks were never laid out; cannot measure." instead of an assertion about an anonymous render box.
- **Tests (3, new file `print_bengali_dialog_test.dart`).** The real path — `captureDocumentInDialog` with the engine's own `encodePagePng` and the real isolate assembler — must yield a one-page `%PDF` and close its dialog; a captured Bengali page is decoded and counted for ink, because a blank page is the silent failure of any offscreen-paint capture and worse than an error; and a capture parented by a dialog-style positioned child must still lay out. `flutter test` **469 passed, 2 skipped**; `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Resume point:** Task 14 is complete in code. Remaining work is device-only: retry a Bengali print on the Redmi (a list **and** Case Detail), then the manual print preview and the four contact-launch paths. Next task in order: `docs/tasks/15_backup_restore_and_free_tier_gating.md`.

### 2026-09-19 — Task 14 Phase 5: unified search on the Drift path — COMPLETE

- **The search bar was filtering a copy, not the database.** Each case screen loaded its dataset and then filtered those rows in Dart (`matchesCaseQuery`), so a case saved by another surface — or by the notification job — was invisible until the screen reloaded. Search now runs as a Drift query: `CasesDao.searchScoped` applies the plan §12.1 field set inside the screen's own dataset predicate, and `ScopedSearch<T>` hands the screen the query's rows.
- **One field set, one dataset rule.** Case number, petitioner, respondent, both lead counsels, the represented role row resolved through `on_behalf_id`, the party phone, the case title/section and the remarks; scope is `any` / `activeOnly` / `disposedOnly` plus an inclusive `next_date` window (`CaseSearchScope`, `CaseSearchDisposal`), with the standard `is_deleted = 0` filter and the repository's `limit`/`offset` paging. Each of the six screens passes the same predicate it lists by — Active and Disposed their disposal state, Upcoming its tomorrow-through-`period` window, Overdue everything before today, Daily Cause List the selected day — so the same query still means something different on each screen, which is the point of plan §12.1.
- **The bug the first screen test caught.** A nullable column guarded as `IS NULL OR LIKE …` is *true* for every NULL row, so a query that matched nothing returned the entire table — the DAO unit tests missed it (one seeded row) and the widget test found it immediately. The guard is now `IS NOT NULL AND LIKE …`, which is FALSE rather than NULL for a NULL column. Worth remembering: with one row per fixture, a "not empty" assertion proves nothing.
- **Blank and stale queries behave.** `ScopedSearch.run('')` clears the result synchronously, so closing the bar restores the screen's dataset with no round trip; every query carries a generation, so a slow answer can never overwrite a newer one's rows; and each `_loadCases` re-runs an open query instead of leaving it answering from the previous dataset.
- **Notes and Case References too.** The Notes bar now runs `NotesDao.search` (title and body) and the Case Reference bar `CaseReferencesDao.search` (all five text fields), replacing the same in-memory `hay.contains` filter. The Settings search already filtered its localized submenu labels, and the task's dataset predicate (`is_deleted`, paging) lives in the DAOs.
- **Tests.** `case_list_search_test.dart` replaced its pure-matcher group with a `CasesDao.searchScoped` group (the field set, dataset predication and both date windows, soft-delete plus paging, and the client-name rule) and gained a widget test that proves the bar hits the database: a case saved *after* the screen loaded is found by the query, and closing the bar restores the dataset. `flutter test` **466 passed, 2 skipped**; `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Resume point:** Task 14 is implemented in full across its five phases plus the Addendum. What remains for it is device work, not code: the manual print-preview check on the Daily Cause List and Case Detail, and the four launch paths (dialer, SMS, WhatsApp chat, and the refusal message) on a real phone. Next task in order: `docs/tasks/15_backup_restore_and_free_tier_gating.md`.

### 2026-09-19 — Task 14 Phase 2: the bounded raster fallback — COMPLETE

- **A budget, not a hope.** `PrintPageBudget` fixes A4 geometry, a 2x pixel-ratio cap and an 8 MB decoded-RGBA ceiling per page. `pixelRatioFor` solves the ratio and then **trims it until the rounded page buffer verifiably fits**, because pixel dimensions round up and rounding only ever adds area — the first cut of this test failed by 464 bytes for exactly that reason. A 3x phone would otherwise allocate ~15 MB for one page of a cause list.
- **Pagination follows measured heights.** `paginateBlockHeights` is pure: a block that no longer fits starts the next page, a block taller than a page gets one of its own, and nothing anywhere counts rows. The heights come from the *very widgets the capture draws* — the capture lays the whole block list out once at page width and reads each child's laid-out height out of the flex, so the page break matches what is actually printed.
- **One page alive at a time.** The capture draws the print-only tree off the top-left corner of a non-clipping `Stack`, captures a page through an injectable encoder, releases that page's image before touching the next, and only then assembles the PDF with `assembleInIsolate` — the only large allocation (the RGBA page buffer) never outlives its page, and only compressed PNG bytes are carried between pages. `Offstage` and zero opacity were both rejected: a capture needs a *painted* layer.
- **Bengali is shaped by Flutter, which is the whole point.** `print_widgets.dart` renders the same `PrintDocument` model in fixed print typography (Latin in Manrope/Inter/IBM Plex Sans, every style falling back to Tiro Bangla), so the raster path draws Bengali correctly where `pdf` cannot.
- **The user can see it working and leave.** The progress dialog shows a live page count and a Cancel button (checked between pages, so a cancel never leaves a half-written PDF), a failure shows `Could Not Print` instead of throwing, and `print_flow.dart` returns an outcome (`presented`, `cancelled`, `failed`, `nothingToPrint`) so a print job can never take a screen down.
- **Tests (13).** Budget caps and the byte budget beating the cap, the block-area geometry, four pagination rules, one PDF page per captured page with a `%PDF` header, assembly on a real worker isolate, and the whole pipeline driven through injected encoder/assembler: exactly one page's RGBA alive and inside budget, cancellation stopping between pages, an encoder failure reported rather than thrown — plus one test that uses the **real engine encoder** (pump frames interleaved with a real async drain) and asserts the captured page really is a PNG.
- `flutter analyze` clean; **437 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.216`.
- **Resume point:** Task 14 Phase 3 — wiring Print on the six case screens (each printing its own dataset, filters and search) plus the Case Detail header's renamed **Edit** and new **Print** (the full detail sheet at the screen's current history expansion), and the separate Share service.

### 2026-09-19 — Task 14 Phase 1: the PDF print core (document model, native renderer, print service) — COMPLETE

- **The dependencies were never added.** `pdf`, `printing` and `share_plus` were missing from `pubspec.yaml` (no lock entries either) and `lib/core/utils/pdf_generator.dart` was still the empty Task-14 placeholder, so "confirm the packages" became `flutter pub add`. `share_plus` lands now because the Share service in Phase 3 needs it; nothing imports it yet.
- **The printed wording has one home.** New `lib/core/print/print_document.dart` is pure Dart — no plugin, no Flutter import, no database — and holds the canonical list layout every case screen will share: the case number and case type as the entry heading, then Court (only on the flat lists, since a grouped screen already names the court in its heading), Petitioner, Respondent, Lead Counsel, On Behalf, Step, the screen's own date column and Note. Blank values are left off the page rather than printing a label with nothing after it; serials are localized and only the flat screens ask for them; the footer (`Printed on DD/MM/YYYY`) and the page counter come from the two new ARB keys, so a Bengali device prints Bengali digits.
- **The decision that matters is `needsRaster`, and it is in the model.** `pdf` 3.13.0 has no Indic shaper — its only shaping table is `arabic.dart` — so Bengali conjuncts, reph and the pre-base vowel sign ি are drawn in the wrong positions while the text still extracts perfectly. No text-extraction test can catch that, which is why the document itself answers "can the native path be trusted for this content?" and the screens will honour it. A Latin-only document keeps the searchable text path.
- **The native renderer is real, not a stub.** `pdf_native_renderer.dart` loads all four bundled fonts through an **injectable loader** (the asset bundle is not guaranteed to answer under `flutter test`, so the tests read the same files from disk) and lays the document out with `pw.MultiPage`: page breaks are the layout engine's measured decisions, never a row count, and Bengali runs on a mixed line are reached by the theme's font fallback rather than by per-string font juggling.
- **The plugin sits behind one seam.** `print_service.dart` is the platform boundary and the only file importing `package:printing`; screens and tests never see the plugin, and `PrintService.present` returns `false` on cancellation instead of throwing.
- **Tests (20 new).** `print_document_test.dart` (10): field order and blank handling, the flat-versus-grouped court rule, localized labels/footer/page counter and serials, and the script-aware decision. `pdf_native_text_test.dart` (6): all four fonts load into the pdf package, Tiro Bangla has a glyph for every fixture character (no tofu), **the printed Bengali characters round-trip out of the `ToUnicode` map the file itself writes, in draw order**, the embedded font is `TiroBangla-Regular` rather than a substitution, and 3 rows make one page while 200 make several. `print_platform_boundary_test.dart` (4 source guards): only the service imports the plugin, the model stays pure, and the print path stays offline (Part A).
- **Flagged:** the task asked for golden/text-extraction checks and the `pdf` package ships no extractor, so extraction is verified through the font cmap plus that `ToUnicode` map; and one bookkeeping edit (`docs/version_history.md`) had to be scripted, because the `## Current Version` copy and the log copy of the newest entry are byte-identical, which leaves no anchor for "append at the end of the file".
- `flutter analyze` clean; **424 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.215`.
- **Resume point:** Task 14 Phase 2 — the bounded widget-to-image raster fallback (`print_page_capture.dart`: tested page-width/height/pixel-ratio/RGBA budgets, measured-height chunking, one page alive at a time, encoding off the main thread, progress and cancellation), which is the path every Bengali document takes.

### 2026-09-19 — Task 13 Phase 3: OEM battery/auto-start guide + first-launch offer — COMPLETE

- **Policy is pure, Android knowledge is not.** `lib/core/notifications/oem_setup_guide.dart` turns the reported manufacturer/brand into a family (Xiaomi/Redmi/Poco, Oppo/Realme/OnePlus, Vivo/iQOO, Huawei/Honor, Infinix/Tecno/itel, Samsung, generic), decides the steps (auto-start → battery Unrestricted → exact alarms on the aggressive families; the two standard steps on Samsung and unknown phones) and the ordered settings targets each step tries (the OEM's own page first, then the app-details page that exists on every phone). Which component actually opens a MIUI/ColorOS/Funtouch/EMUI/XOS page is Android knowledge and stayed in the gateway.
- **The guide screen has no mockup** — built, as approved, from the Settings chrome and the same `settings_controls.dart` the Notifications mockup established: intro card, one numbered card per step, a "Take Me There" button that walks its target list and says `Could Not Open That Settings Page` when *nothing* opens (never a dead tap), an "Exact Alarms Are Already Allowed" line replacing the button on the Android 12+ step when the permission is already granted, and Done / Skip buttons.
- **Done and Skip mean different things.** Done stores `notification_setup_done` + `notification_guide_shown`; Skip stores only `notification_guide_shown`, because the plan wants the banner to keep nudging while the user is spared the screen reappearing. Both live in the per-user `view_settings_json` (no schema change), and both re-arm the alarm afterwards — the user may have just granted exact alarms, and the armed alarm's precision was decided before that.
- **First launch, once, only where it matters.** `notification_setup_gate.dart` offers the guide on first launch for the aggressive families alone; the flag is written *before* navigating so a rebuild cannot open a second guide, and the gate stands down entirely when a notification launched the app into a case detail (the tap is what the user asked for).
- **The banner is real now.** Settings → Notifications' reminder banner and guide row push the actual guide instead of a "Task 13" snackbar, the banner disappears once the setup is done, and the mockup's empty Test Notification section became a working action: it asks for the OS permission when missing and reports `Could Not Show a Notification` rather than claiming success for an alert Android would drop.
- **Tests.** New `oem_setup_guide_test.dart` (7) and `battery_setup_guide_test.dart` (10 — localized steps in both languages, the two-step phone, the target fall-through and the unreachable case, the already-allowed state, Done/Skip storage, and the first-launch offer appearing once on a Redmi but never on a Pixel or a notification launch); `notification_settings_test.dart` is 11 (banner visibility both ways, the test action with and without permission). Two harness lessons: a step below the fold is *not built*, so these suites set a 600×1500 logical viewport (an off-screen finder fails for the wrong reason), and the first-launch test must reuse one `SharedPreferences` instance — resetting them mints a new local identity, and the notification flags are per-user.
- **Device checklist still to run by hand** (nothing in the test host can prove these): (1) first launch on the Xiaomi/Redmi phone — the guide should appear once, and `Take Me There` should land on MIUI's auto-start page; (2) grant "Alarms & reminders" from the guide's exact-alarm step and confirm the state line flips; (3) `Send Test Notification` posts a banner (and reports a refusal if the notification permission is denied); (4) set the time to a minute or two ahead with the app swiped away and confirm the alert arrives with the app closed; (5) reboot with the app closed and confirm the alarm is re-armed (the plugin's `RebootBroadcastReceiver`); (6) tap a posted alert and confirm the case's Detail view opens, including from a cold start; (7) on a non-aggressive phone (or by clearing the app data), confirm no guide is offered at launch.
- `flutter analyze` clean; **404 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.214`.
- **Resume point:** Task 13 is implemented in full; what remains is the device checklist above. Next task in order: Task 14 (search) unless the user directs otherwise.

### 2026-09-19 — Task 13 Phase 2: exact-alarm permission, fallback and rescheduling — COMPLETE

- **The permission is asked, not assumed.** `syncSchedule` (and the job's own re-arm) resolves the alarm through the pure `alarmPrecisionFor(exactAllowed:)`: `setExactAndAllowWhileIdle` when Android grants `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`, and `setAndAllowWhileIdle` (`oneShotAt(exact: false, allowWhileIdle: true)`) when it does not — Android 14 denies it by default for new installs, so without this the daily alert would simply never fire on those devices. **Flagged:** the plugin exposes no `setWindow`, so the fallback is the inexact idle-safe alarm it does provide; precision is minute-level, and no alarm is ever silently dropped.
- **The grant screen is one call away.** `NotificationScheduler.openExactAlarmSettings()` → `requestExactAlarmsPermission()` jumps straight to the system "Alarms & reminders" page (the plugin's own Intent). Phase 3 wires the button into the OEM guide; the state is read through the new `canScheduleExactAlarms()` so the guide can also show whether a step is already done.
- **Reboot and app update.** Reboot rescheduling stays the plugin's `RebootBroadcastReceiver` (`rescheduleOnReboot: true` armed in Phase 1). App updates needed our own native receiver, since the plugin only handles `BOOT_COMPLETED`: new `android/app/src/main/kotlin/com/legalcasediary/app/PackageReplacedReceiver.kt` (15 lines) hands `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` to the plugin's public `AlarmService.reschedulePersistentAlarms`, declared in the manifest — it needs no Flutter engine, since it regenerates the alarms from stored state.
- **Re-arm on the two other places it can go stale.** Every app launch re-arms through `initialize()`, and every notification-settings write does too — ON/OFF (which cancels the alarm when switched off), day offset and time now all call the scheduler's `syncSchedule()` after storing the preference, so the armed alarm can never disagree with the screen.
- **A real gap the full suite caught.** `lib/core/constants/app_version.dart` was still `0.0.211` after Phase 1's bump; `app_version_test.dart` compares it against `pubspec.yaml` and the `## Current Version` line, so a version bumped in two places out of three is now impossible to miss. Brought to `0.0.213` here.
- **Tests.** `notification_plan_test.dart` +1 (the exact/inexact decision), `notification_settings_test.dart` +1 (all four settings writes re-arm, with the fake scheduler counting calls), new `notification_tap_test.dart` (3): a launch payload opens that case's Detail view instead of the landing page, an ordinary launch still lands on All Cases, and a warm tap delivered to the registered handler opens the case. `flutter analyze` clean; **382 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.213`.
- **Resume point:** Task 13 Phase 3 — the localized battery/auto-start guide screen (planned from the app's Settings chrome, as approved, since no mockup exists), first-launch detection for aggressive OEMs, `Take Me There` intents, the banner wired to real navigation, and a working Send Test Notification.

### 2026-09-19 — Task 13 Phase 1: the notification core (platform abstraction + scheduling) — COMPLETE

- **What was built.** New `lib/core/notifications/`. (1) `notification_plan.dart` is **pure Dart** — no plugin, no database — and holds every rule the alert job follows: offset matching against **Bangladesh civil dates** (`next_date − today` ∈ the case's effective offsets, so 23:59 today and 00:01 tomorrow are a day apart, not a minute), the **per-case override → per-user default** resolution (`case_notification_preferences` beats `user_preferences`, a case switched off never alerts, a disabled master switch yields no alerts), the localized title/body — case number, `Petitioner vs Respondent`, and the court name through the same `seedLabel` lookup every other surface uses, so a Bengali device gets `আগোমীকাল শুনানি`/বনাম and Bengali digits — plus `nextAlarmEpochMs` (today's time if still ahead, else tomorrow) and a deterministic FNV-1a notification id so a re-post replaces rather than duplicates. (2) `NotificationScheduler` is the platform contract (`initialize`, `onCaseSelected`, `launchCaseId`, permission queries, `syncSchedule`, `cancelAll`); `AndroidNotificationScheduler` implements it and forwards **every** platform call to (3) `android_notification_gateway.dart`, **the only file in the app importing `flutter_local_notifications` or `android_alarm_manager_plus`** — high-importance `hearing_alerts` channel, one notification per matching case under one group key with the case id as payload, `oneShotAt(exact: true, allowWhileIdle: true, wakeup: true, rescheduleOnReboot: true)` = `setExactAndAllowWhileIdle`, and no foreground-service notification.
- **The daily job runs in the alarm isolate** (`notification_alarm_callback.dart`, `@pragma('vm:entry-point')` + `DartPluginRegistrant.ensureInitialized()`): it builds its own encrypted Drift connection (plugin registration is automatic for the engine the plugin constructs, because the manifest carries `flutterEmbedding=2`), reads the per-user row and the case overrides, queries only the window the configured offsets can reach, posts the day's alerts and **re-arms tomorrow's alarm from inside the job**, so the chain survives without the app ever being opened. `LocalIdentityService.peekId` exists so the isolate can never mint an identity, and the job is wrapped so a failure cannot crash the alarm service.
- **The enforced local-only rule is now a test.** `notification_local_only_test.dart` (5) reads the sources: no network package is imported anywhere on the notification path, the job names no `Supabase`/`sync_queue`/`SyncWriteHelper` and imports no Android package outside the gateway, and the entry point keeps its AOT annotation.
- **Tap → Detail View, cold or warm.** A tap that *launches* the app is read back through `getNotificationAppLaunchDetails()` before the router is built, so `appRouterProvider` starts on `caseDetailPath(id)` instead of the landing page; a tap while the app runs is delivered by the new `NotificationTapListener` mounted around the navigator, which also initializes the scheduler and re-arms the schedule on every launch. The manifest gained the plugin's service + both receivers (`BOOT_COMPLETED` included) and `WAKE_LOCK`/`RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`/`SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`.
- **Small supporting edits.** `CaseNotificationPreferencesDao.getByUserId` (a one-shot read for the isolate; only a stream existed), `LocalIdentityService.peekId`, and the screen's private `_defaultNotificationMinutes` moved into the plan file as `defaultNotificationMinutes` so the stored default and the job's fallback cannot drift.
- **Tests.** New `notification_plan_test.dart` (18) — offset matching including the per-case override, the switched-off case, the civil-date boundary, ordering, the English/Bengali copy and the helper contracts (`decodeNotificationOffsets`, `effectiveOffsets`, `widestOffset`, `nextAlarmEpochMs`, `notificationIdFor`) — plus the 5 guards above. `flutter analyze` clean; **377 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.212`.
- **Resume point:** Task 13 Phase 2 — runtime `canScheduleExactAlarms` check with the deep link to the system "Alarms & reminders" screen, inexact windowed fallback when refused, `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` rescheduling, and re-arming whenever a notification setting changes.

### 2026-09-19 — Compact card view: the same pinned, foldable court headings — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the compact/card view of Overdue Dates, Daily Cause List and Upcoming Cases).** The compact card list now heads each court with the same pinned, foldable `CaseTableGroup` the table uses. The court heading holds the top of the list for as long as that court's own cards are on screen and is pushed out as the next court's heading arrives; tapping the band (the whole band is the tap target) folds that court's cards away and tapping it again brings them back; a folded court is the only one showing the down chevron, because an expanded court's cards already say they are showing; and every court opens expanded. `CaseTableGroup` already took its cases as plain widgets, so the table hands it rows and the card list hands it `_buildCard`s — the pinning and fold behaviour is one implementation, not a second one for cards.
- **The fold choice is one state, read by both views.** No new setting and no ARB key: the card list reads and writes the same per-screen `case_table_collapsed_courts` entry the table does (`watchCaseTableCollapsedCourts` / `setCaseTableCourtCollapsed`, through a small per-screen `toggleCourt` helper each screen now shares between its two bodies), so a court folded in the card list opens folded in the table and the choice survives a restart and a view switch. One screen's fold still never touches another screen's copy of the same court, exactly as before.
- **What deliberately did not change.** The compact body is now a `CustomScrollView` of `CaseTableGroup`s (cards as the group's cases) instead of one `ListView` item per court, so the heading can be a pinned sliver. The cards keep the same gutters and spacing — the group's cases sliver insets by `AppSpacing.md` and spaces cases by `AppSpacing.md`, and the list is topped and tailed by `AppSpacing.md`, which is what the old `padding: EdgeInsets.all(AppSpacing.md)` did — Overdue Dates and Upcoming Cases keep their `RefreshIndicator`, and Daily Cause List's card list keeps the no-pull-to-refresh body it has always had. Upcoming's date heading stays an ordinary (non-pinned) row above each day's groups; it is now built by one `dateHeadingSliver` helper both of that screen's bodies share, so the two views cannot drift apart.
- **Tests.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` is 26 — the pinning test now asserts the compact list draws the same pinned groups over its cards (and no chevron while every court is expanded), a new case pins that a court's heading holds the top of the card list and hands it over to the next court after scrolling on, and another pins that tapping a heading in the card list hides that court's cards only, stores the choice for the screen, keeps it across a restart, shows it in the table as one shared fold state, and clears it when the court is re-opened. Folding in one view and asserting the other, or drawing the compact list with plain headings, fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; **352 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.210`.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-19 — Extended view: Overdue Dates' Step/Result column back, and foldable court headings — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the extended/table view of Overdue Dates, Daily Cause List and Upcoming Cases).** (1) **Overdue Dates has its own Step/Result column again**, directly before Note and identical to the other five screens': the folded "Previous Date & Step" cell of 0.0.206 is gone, the date column is now the previous date alone (red, one line, labelled from `caseCard_previousDate`), and `CaseTableRow.dateShowsStep` plus the `subStyle` hook that backed it were removed with their last users — so the step is never printed twice and an AD/HCD result fills its own Step/Result cell here like everywhere else. (2) **The court headings fold.** Tapping a heading hides that court's rows, tapping it again shows them, and the band carries a chevron pointing **down** while the cases are hidden and up while they are showing. **A court opens folded**, so the extended view greets the user with the court headings and they open only the courts they are reading; the fold state is per group inside `CaseTableGroup` (a folded group contributes its heading sliver alone, so the next court's heading comes straight up behind it) and the **whole band** is the tap target, not just the arrow. One deliberate placement call: the chevron sits at the end of the band beside the hearing count rather than under the heading, because a band tall enough for a second line would have to grow `caseTableGroupHeaderHeight` and with it every pinned-band measurement.

Version `0.0.208`.

### 2026-09-19 — Extended view: the Step/Result column and pinned court headings — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the extended/table view only).** (1) The table's **Step column is now "Step/Result"** and it **carries an AD/HCD case's free-text Result**. Higher courts have no step dropdown, so the result is the only value in that slot; the row shows it in the step's place, or stacked beneath the step through the same `subText` slot when a case somehow holds both. The label is composed from `caseCard_lastStep` + `addEditCaseSheet_result`, so no ARB key was added. (2) **Overdue Dates' folded cell falls back to the result** when the case has no step, so an overdue higher-court case still shows its result in the table. (3) **The court heading on the three grouped screens (Overdue Dates, Daily Cause List, Upcoming Cases) now pins to the top of the list** for as long as that court's cases are on screen, and is pushed out as the next court's heading arrives. The extended body on those screens is a `CustomScrollView` of the new `CaseTableGroup` (heading + rows in one `SliverMainAxisGroup`, the heading a pinned `SliverPersistentHeader`); the pinned band paints the screen background so rows slide underneath, and Upcoming's date heading stays an ordinary row above each day's groups. The compact card list on all three screens is untouched and keeps its scroll-away headings.
- **The grouping is load-bearing, not decoration.** Plain consecutive pinned headers in one scroll view do **not** push each other out: the viewport lays each one out at `SliverConstraints.overlap` = the pinned extent of the ones before it, so every heading ends up stacked at the top and all of them stay visible at once (measured: the first court's heading still at the viewport top with the second's 40 px below it after scrolling to the bottom). Wrapping each heading and its rows in a `SliverMainAxisGroup` scopes the pinning to the group, which is what produces the handover the user asked for.
- **One dead end worth recording.** The first attempt kept the rows in one `ListView` and moved the pinned headings into slivers only, which is why the stacking behaviour was discovered by measurement rather than assumed. `caseTableGroupHeaderHeight` sizes the pinned band from the heading's own line height (title `fontSize` × `height`), scaled with the user's text size.
- **A test-data trap worth recording.** `result` doubles as the disposal marker: a result whose text contains *Dismissed*, *Disposed* or *Discharged* (`disposedResultTermLabels`) makes `CasesDao._isDisposed` true, so the case leaves Overdue Dates / Active and appears under Disposed. The first version of the new Overdue test seeded `'Appeal dismissed'` and saw the empty state; an active AD/HCD case now seeds `'Judgment reserved'`.
- **Tests.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` is 22 — the header reads "Step/Result" with the bare "Step" gone, an AD/HCD result fills the step cell and a case holding both stacks the result under the step, Overdue's folded cell carries the result, the first court's heading holds the list top while its cases are visible and the second takes over after scrolling on, the pinning exists only in the extended view, and the compact lists still draw plain headings. Removing the result from the step cell, or `pinned: true` from the group, fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; **348 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.207`.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — Extended view rebuilt as an Excel-like table with resizable columns — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the extended/table view only).** The extended view is now a spreadsheet instead of a squeezed card: one **header row** (`CaseTableHeader`) names every column once — **Serial → Update → Name of Court → Case Type → Case Number → Petitioner → Respondent → On Behalf → Step → Note → Date** — and each case row carries **values only** (no per-cell labels), each in a **single line** (`maxLines: 1`, `softWrap: false`, ellipsis; widen the column to read more). - **`CaseTableView` (`features/cases/presentation/case_table_view.dart`) owns the widths and the scroll.** It lays the header and the body out at one computed minimum width (`caseTableMinimumWidth`: columns + rules + the fixed chrome of a 2 px border, a 4 px strip lane, a 12 px gutter and the 16 px screen margin) inside one horizontal scroll view, so the header and the rows scroll together and the header's cells sit **exactly** over the data columns. That alignment is why the header and every row reserve the 4 px strip lane — the header never paints it, only overdue rows do. A test asserts the alignment column by column.

Fields without a column ride along in the nearest one: judges' name → Name of Court, free-text case title/section → Case Type, joined with `·`. Because the header gives every screen a Serial column, the grouped screens now number their rows too (in render order); the serial is still a position, not an identity.

### 2026-09-18 — Case lists: the view toggle works and the extended view exists — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** `CasesScreenModal` drew a compact ↔ expanded view-toggle icon in the header of **all nine** screens that use it, but no screen ever passed `onViewToggle`, so the button was enabled, tappable and did nothing (on Notes and Case Reference too, where a case-view toggle makes no sense). `case_list_display_mode` — Settings → View Settings → **Case List Display** — was stored and shown but read by nobody.
- **What it does now.** The shell draws the toggle only when its host wires it, so the two libraries lost it automatically with no extra flag. The six case-list screens read the stored mode from the new shared `core/providers/settings_providers.dart` (`userSettingsStreamProvider`) and write it through `case_list_view_mode.dart` (`setCaseListViewMode`); extended mode swaps each screen's `CaseCard` for the new shared `CaseTableRow`, and `CaseTableBody` gives the body the mockup's 600 px minimum width with a horizontal scroll. `CaseTableRow` carries **full data parity** with the card — serial cell, bordered Update column, date and step/result columns, the case block (number, parties, section, court · judges, note) and the phone/message actions — inside the mockup's row chrome (white row, outline border, card radius, red strip on overdue rows).
- **Decisions (user-approved).** Serial numbers are the screen's own running order (`index + 1` of what is visible, so a filter or the Newest-First toggle renumbers from 1) and appear **only** on the flat lists — All Cases, Active Cases, Disposed Cases — rendered through `AppDateUtils.formatDigits`, so বাংলা reads ১. The grouped screens (Daily Cause List, Upcoming Cases, Overdue Dates) keep their headings and carry no serial. Rows carry the card's full field set rather than the mockup's thinner row, and the app keeps its single cycling toggle icon instead of the mockup's two-icon pair.
- **The write path, and why.** The header writes through `settingsViewModelProvider.notifier.updateSettings` semantics via `settingsRepositoryProvider` (reading the whole row first, so no other preference can be dropped while the ViewModel state is loading) and then invalidates the ViewModel, because View Settings reads the **ViewModel**, not the stream. Without that invalidation it would keep showing the mode the app started with — the same stale-arrangement class of bug as the bottom-nav order (0.0.200). Renders read the stream, never the ViewModel, so a toggle cannot flash the compact list mid-write.
- **Tests:** new `test/features/case_list_view_mode_test.dart` (9) — toggle switches cards ↔ rows and stores the choice, a stored `expanded` opens a screen already extended, the write is visible to the settings ViewModel, flat lists number their rows (`1`, `2` / `১`, `২`), the grouped lists have no serial and overdue rows keep their strip, rows keep every card field and action, and Notes/Case Reference never show a toggle. Reverting the invalidation, the toggle gate, or the "no serial on grouped screens" rule each fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; 333 passed, 2 skipped. Version `0.0.203`.
- **One real bug the tests caught (and the fix).** The new 360 dp narrow-phone test found the row overflowing by 20 px: the on-behalf chip in the case block is laid out at its intrinsic width, so a long role name — very reachable, since users add their own on-behalf names in Edit Court/Steps — pushed the case number out of the row. The chip is now width-capped at 104 px and ellipsises (the case number is the primary label), and the three column rules moved to the 8 px gutter to give the case block its width back. With the cap removed the test fails by 196 px, so the guard is real. Checking compact mode at the same width first proved the overflow was mine and not pre-existing.
- **Same gotcha as last time:** these screens hold live Drift streams (settings, plus their own queries), so any suite on them must unmount the tree before the test ends and should prefer bounded pumps over `pumpAndSettle`.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — Notes & Case Reference: the search box is the case-list search box — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** Both libraries already used the shared `ScopedSearchBar`, but they rendered it as the *first row of their scrolling list* instead of letting `CasesScreenModal` mount it. So it scrolled away with the cards, sat inside the list's own 16 px padding (double-spaced), and — worse — an empty library threw it away: the empty-state branch returned a `ListView` that discarded the children list holding the bar, so an empty Notes or Case Reference library could not be searched at all.
- **The fix.** The two screens pass `isSearching`, `searchController` and `onSearchChanged` to `CasesScreenModal`, exactly as the six case-list screens do, and their bodies (`_NotesList`, `_ReferenceList`) lost the controller, the search callback and the `onCloseSearch` argument they no longer need. The bar now sits under the header, above the list, stays while the list scrolls and while the library is empty, and the header icon, the always-on (x) and tap-outside-to-hide-the-keyboard all keep working because they are the same widget and the same modal wiring as the case screens. No ARB change.
- **Tests:** new `test/features/library_search_test.dart` (3) — a loop over both libraries asserts the bar opens above the list (explicitly not a descendant of the `ListView`), closes with the (x), and does all of that with an empty library; each library's query filters only its own rows, and closing clears the query. Dropping `isSearching` from the modal call fails the placement test. `flutter analyze` clean; 324 passed, 2 skipped. Version `0.0.202`.
- **Note for future tests on these screens:** they hold live Drift streams, so a suite must unmount the widget tree (pump a bare `SizedBox`) before the test ends — disposing the provider container with a query stream live leaves a pending timer that fails the test.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — View Settings: the Bottom Navigation order is remembered — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** The dropdown re-seeded its rows from the canonical `bottomNavTabDefs` order every time it opened. A drag did take effect in the cases screen — it rewrote `bottom_nav_tabs`, which the bar renders — but the dropdown showed the original list again, so the reorder looked forgotten.
- **The fix.** The row order is now the user's own per-user value, `bottom_nav_order` (inside `view_settings_json`, no schema change), written by the same drag that rewrites the tab order. The dropdown therefore opens in exactly the order the user left, and the bar and the dropdown cannot disagree. An empty or partial serial falls back to the canonical order for the screens it does not name, so an install that never reordered a row is unchanged and a screen added later still appears (at its canonical place).
- **Tests:** `view_settings_test.dart` is 18 — a dragged order survives closing and reopening the dropdown, and a stored serial that predates a screen still lists that screen, last. `repositories_test.dart` round-trips `bottom_nav_order` through the stored JSON. `flutter analyze` clean; 320 passed, 2 skipped. ### 2026-09-17 — Settings → Language redesign — COMPLETE

- **The screen no longer reads as empty.** `language_screen.dart` was a `Padding` + `Row` of two 48 px buttons, so the page ended ~48 px into an ~800 px body. It is now one group centred in the viewport: a 72 px round language badge (`Icons.language` on `surfaceContainer`), a `settingsLanguage_selectHeading` heading with a one-line `settingsLanguage_intro` under it, the two option cards, a preview card showing **today's date in the active language**, and a `settingsLanguage_note` card that says the change affects only the interface. Heights: badge 72 + heading + 2 × 84 px cards + preview + note ≈ 560 px, so the body is filled with content the user reads rather than with padding.
- **Layout mechanics:** `LayoutBuilder` → `SingleChildScrollView` (16 px edge padding) → `ConstrainedBox(minHeight: viewport − 32)` → `Center` → `ConstrainedBox(maxWidth: 480)` → `Column(mainAxisSize: min)`. A tall screen centres the group; a short one (320 × 480) or a large text scale scrolls instead of overflowing — the test asserts no exception at 320 × 480 with `textScaler` 1.3.
- **Options:** 84 px, full-width, radius 4. The active language is filled black with white text and a white `Icons.check`; the other is white with black text, an `AppColors.outline` border and a transparent check so the label never shifts. `Semantics(selected:, button:)` plus the check's `semanticLabel` (`settingsLanguage_selected`) carry the state to screen readers, and each label keeps its own script's font (`titleMd` / new `bengaliTitleMd`).
- **Preview is the proof:** `AppDateUtils.formatFullDate(today, locale: l.localeName)` in the active script's title font, so switching language visibly changes the line — English "Thursday, September 17, 2026" vs Bengali "বৃহস্পতিবার, সেপ্টেম্বর ১৭, ২০২৬".
- **New token:** `AppTypography.bengaliTitleMd` (Tiro Bangla 16 / 24, w600) — the Bengali counterpart of `titleMd`, symmetric with the existing `bengaliBodyLg/Md/Sm` set.
- **ARB:** new `@@_section_settingsLanguage` section (en + bn) with `settingsLanguage_selectHeading` / `_intro` / `_previewTitle` / `_selected` / `_note`; the two autonyms `settingsScreen_languageEnglish` / `_Bengali` moved into it (`settingsScreen_language` stays in the settings-main section, which renders it as the main-list row). `test/l10n/arb_keys_test.dart` gained `settingsLanguage` to the marker guard.
- **Mockup:** new `mockup_html_screens/settings_language/code.html` (the screen had none — Texts.txt lists no reference for it), showing the composition twice side by side, English active and বাংলা active. No `screen.png` yet.
- **Tests:** `language_screen_test.dart` rewritten to 8 (framing, black/outline states, a check painted only on the active option, the `selected` flag + check semantics, per-script fonts, the preview date following the locale, persistence, and the short/large-text layout). `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **266 passed, 2 skipped**. Drag & drop reorder, edit and FAB add are unchanged; add now stores the **category being listed** instead of always `chief_judicial_magistrate`. Seeded court names are rendered through `context.l10n.seedLabel(...)`, so they follow the active language exactly like the form's Court Name dropdown. The add button is the Add Case button (black `FloatingActionButton`, white `Icons.add` size 28, default bottom-right) so both this screen and the Lower Court Categories screen match the case screens.
- **Delete is now rendered only on courts the user added.** `LocalCourtsRepository.delete` refuses pre-seeded courts, so every delete tap on a seeded row previously threw an unhandled `StateError`. The screen now mirrors the Lower Court Categories rule (delete action only when `court.isCustom`), which keeps a working delete for user-added courts and removes the crash for built-ins. Same reasoning recorded for the category rows in 0.0.173.
- **New shared helper** `edit_court_steps/court_category_label.dart` (`courtCategoryLabel` / `courtCategoryName`): resolves a category label from either a seed key or a user-typed name. `lower_court_category_list_screen.dart` had that lookup privately inline; both screens now call the helper, so the two category lists cannot disagree.
- **Tests** `test/features/edit_court_names_test.dart` (13, in-memory fakes — Drift streams in widget tests leave pending timers): the header and the two division cards, the routes each card pushes (URL + `extra`), Lower Courts → division picker, that each division lists only its own court names, that a real pre-seeded court shows its **Bengali** label under `Locale('bn')`, the district picker offering exactly the categories with courts (plus the user's own, minus the court-less seeded one) and its empty state, the picker pushing a category-scoped court list, category-scoped listing (another category's court stays out), the Add Case style FAB adding into the listed category, custom-only delete, and rename. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **220 passed, 2 skipped** (was 207 + 2).
- **Docs:** `docs/tasks/12_settings_screens.md` gained an “Edit Court Names (added during implementation, 2026-09-17)” section recording the restructure (it matches Phase 3 item 2’s intended hierarchy, so the body of the task file was left alone), and the two new files were added to the file-layout tree in `docs/implementation_plan.md`. Version `0.0.175` in `pubspec.yaml`, `version_history.md`, `currentlyWorking.md`. **Note:** `pubspec.yaml` was still at `0.0.173` while the history said `0.0.174` (the previous commit did not bump it), so this bump sets `0.0.175` — the file and the log now agree again.
